fast_pairs.pyPeriodic candidate-pair searches with a k-d treeSee code ↗

The accelerated search preserves the engine’s periodic pair convention.

"""Use a periodic ``cKDTree`` for candidate-pair searches.

Importing this module replaces ``modified_engine._celllist_pairs_periodic``
with a tree-based implementation. The search radius is unchanged, so the
routine returns the same candidate-pair set as
``cKDTree(boxsize=[L, W]).query_pairs(r=r_query)``.

The tree is useful when ``r_query = 2 r_max + 2 U_max dt`` is much smaller
than the mean particle spacing. In that regime, the engine's fixed grid has
many empty cells and the cell scan can dominate the pair search.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np
from scipy.spatial import cKDTree

import modified_engine as _eng

_EMPTY = (np.empty(0, np.int64), np.empty(0, np.int64))


def _kdtree_pairs_periodic(pos, L, W, r_query):
    """Return periodic pairs within ``r_query`` using the engine contract."""
    p = np.mod(np.asarray(pos, float), [L, W])
    # cKDTree with boxsize requires points strictly inside [0, L) x [0, W)
    p[p[:, 0] >= L, 0] = 0.0
    p[p[:, 1] >= W, 1] = 0.0
    tree = cKDTree(p, boxsize=[L, W])
    prs = tree.query_pairs(r=float(r_query), output_type="ndarray")
    if len(prs) == 0:
        return _EMPTY
    return (prs[:, 0].astype(np.int64), prs[:, 1].astype(np.int64))


_original = _eng._celllist_pairs_periodic
_eng._celllist_pairs_periodic = _kdtree_pairs_periodic
PATCHED = True


def restore():
    """Restore the engine's original cell-list search."""
    _eng._celllist_pairs_periodic = _original


def verify(N=20000, L=None, W=None, r_query=6.06e-3, seed=0):
    """Compare the tree and cell-list candidate sets for a random sample."""
    from slab_interp import LX, LZ
    L = LX if L is None else L
    W = LZ if W is None else W
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pos = np.column_stack([rng.uniform(0, L, N), rng.uniform(0, W, N)])
    a1, b1 = _original(pos, L, W, r_query)
    a2, b2 = _kdtree_pairs_periodic(pos, L, W, r_query)
    s1 = {(int(i), int(j)) for i, j in zip(a1, b1)}
    s2 = {(int(i), int(j)) for i, j in zip(a2, b2)}
    ok = s1 == s2
    print(f"verify: {len(s1)} vs {len(s2)} pairs -> "
          f"{'IDENTICAL' if ok else 'DIFFER (' + str(len(s1 ^ s2)) + ' mismatches)'}")
    return ok
progress.pyLightweight progress and runtime diagnostic reportingSee code ↗

A compact terminal bar keeps long parameter studies readable.

"""
Progress reporting for long engine runs.

This module wraps the ``fluid_velocity`` callable used at each integration
step. The wrapper receives the particle positions and time, allowing it to
report progress without changing the engine.

    from progress import monitor, SweepProgress

    field = monitor(dns_slab_fluid(H5, radius=a), T_final=89.8,
                    label="St+=10", L=L, W=W, diagnostics=True)
    simulate_drag_friction(..., fluid_velocity=field, ...)

Output:

    St+=10   [############--------]  61%  t= 54.8/89.8   12m elapsed   ETA  7m
             steps 2740   1.9 kstep/min   VMR16 3.42   drift 4.1%

In-flight diagnostics
    VMR near 1.0 suggests little clustering, while a VMR that is still rising
    near the end of the record suggests that the run has not converged.

    The diagnostic uses a 16x16 histogram of the positions already available
    to the wrapper and is computed at most once every ``every`` time units.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import sys
import time
import numpy as np


def _fmt(sec):
    if not np.isfinite(sec) or sec < 0:
        return "  --"
    if sec < 90:
        return f"{sec:3.0f}s"
    if sec < 5400:
        return f"{sec/60:3.0f}m"
    return f"{sec/3600:3.1f}h"


def _bar(frac, width=20):
    n = int(np.clip(frac, 0, 1) * width)
    return "[" + "#" * n + "-" * (width - n) + "]"


class ProgressMonitor:
    """
    Wraps a `fluid_velocity` callable and reports progress as the engine runs.

    Parameters
    ----------
    field : callable(pos, t) -> (N, 2)
    T_final : float
        Engine end time, used for the fraction complete and the ETA.
    label : str
        Prefix, e.g. "St+=10".
    every : float
        Minimum engine-time interval between reports.
    L, W : float
        Domain, needed only if diagnostics=True.
    diagnostics : bool
        Also report VMR (16x16) and the mean drift, computed from the positions
        the engine is already passing in.
    inline : bool
        Overwrite one line with '\\r' (terminal). Set False for Spyder or for
        log files, which then get one line per report.
    stream : file
        Where to write. Defaults to stdout.
    """

    def __init__(self, field, T_final, label="", every=2.0, L=None, W=None,
                 diagnostics=False, inline=True, stream=None):
        self.field = field
        self.T = float(T_final)
        self.label = label
        self.every = float(every)
        self.L, self.W = L, W
        self.diagnostics = bool(diagnostics) and L is not None and W is not None
        self.inline = inline
        self.stream = stream or sys.stdout

        self.t0 = time.time()
        self.calls = 0
        self.last_report_t = -np.inf
        self.last_t = 0.0
        self.p0 = None
        self.history = []          # (t, vmr) for later plotting

    # -------------------------------------------------------------- report
    def _diag(self, pos):
        H, _, _ = np.histogram2d(np.mod(pos[:, 0], self.L),
                                 np.mod(pos[:, 1], self.W),
                                 bins=16, range=[[0, self.L], [0, self.W]])
        c = H.ravel()
        vmr = float(c.var() / c.mean())
        d = pos - self.p0
        d[:, 0] -= self.L * np.round(d[:, 0] / self.L)
        d[:, 1] -= self.W * np.round(d[:, 1] / self.W)
        drift = float(np.hypot(d[:, 0], d[:, 1]).mean() / self.L * 100)
        return vmr, drift

    def _emit(self, t, pos):
        el = time.time() - self.t0
        frac = np.clip(t / self.T, 0, 1)
        eta = el * (1 - frac) / frac if frac > 1e-6 else np.nan
        rate = self.calls / max(el, 1e-9) * 60 / 1000        # ksteps per minute

        line = (f"{self.label:<10s} {_bar(frac)} {100*frac:3.0f}%  "
                f"t={t:6.1f}/{self.T:.1f}  {_fmt(el)} elapsed  ETA {_fmt(eta)}")
        if self.diagnostics and self.p0 is not None:
            vmr, drift = self._diag(pos)
            self.history.append((t, vmr))
            line += f"   VMR16 {vmr:5.2f}   drift {drift:4.1f}%"
        line += f"   {rate:.1f} kstep/min"

        if self.inline:
            self.stream.write("\r" + line.ljust(118))
        else:
            self.stream.write(line + "\n")
        self.stream.flush()

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- call
    def __call__(self, pos, t):
        self.calls += 1
        self.last_t = t
        if self.p0 is None:
            self.p0 = np.array(pos, copy=True)
        if t - self.last_report_t >= self.every:
            self.last_report_t = t
            self._emit(t, pos)
        return self.field(pos, t)

    # --------------------------------------------------------------- close
    def done(self, extra=""):
        """Call after the run to finish the line and print the summary."""
        el = time.time() - self.t0
        if self.inline:
            self.stream.write("\r" + " " * 118 + "\r")
        self.stream.write(
            f"{self.label:<10s} done  t={self.last_t:.1f}  "
            f"{_fmt(el)}  {self.calls} field calls{('  ' + extra) if extra else ''}\n")
        self.stream.flush()
        return el

    # Forward attributes provided by the wrapped field.
    def __getattr__(self, name):
        return getattr(self.__dict__["field"], name)


def monitor(field, T_final, **kw):
    """Shorthand for ProgressMonitor(field, T_final, **kw)."""
    return ProgressMonitor(field, T_final, **kw)


class SweepProgress:
    """
    Tracks a sweep of runs, so you know where you are in the whole study
    rather than only within the current run.

        sw = SweepProgress([0, 1, 5, 10, 20, 50], label="St+")
        for st in sw:
            ...run...
            sw.tick()
    """

    def __init__(self, values, label="case", stream=None):
        self.values = list(values)
        self.label = label
        self.stream = stream or sys.stdout
        self.t0 = time.time()
        self.i = 0
        self.times = []

    def __iter__(self):
        for v in self.values:
            self.current = v
            el = time.time() - self.t0
            mean = np.mean(self.times) if self.times else np.nan
            left = mean * (len(self.values) - self.i) if self.times else np.nan
            self.stream.write(
                f"\n=== {self.label} {v}  ({self.i+1}/{len(self.values)})   "
                f"elapsed {_fmt(el)}   remaining ~{_fmt(left)} ===\n")
            self.stream.flush()
            yield v

    def tick(self):
        """Call at the end of each case."""
        now = time.time()
        prev = self.t0 + sum(self.times)
        self.times.append(now - prev)
        self.i += 1

    def summary(self):
        el = time.time() - self.t0
        self.stream.write(f"\nsweep finished: {len(self.values)} cases in "
                          f"{_fmt(el)}  (mean {_fmt(np.mean(self.times))} each)\n")
        self.stream.flush()
        return el

DNS data and interpolation

Dataset conventions, wall units and velocity reconstruction.

dataset.pyDataset geometry, component ordering and wall-normal conventionsSee code ↗

A local three-point Lagrange derivative respects the stored wall-normal node positions.

"""Dataset-specific conventions for the near-wall DNS pipeline.

The canonical component order is streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal, denoted
``(x, z, y)``. Canonical arrays use axes ``(z, x, y)``: spanwise on axis 0,
streamwise on axis 1, and wall-normal on axis 2. ``load_snapshot`` performs
the dataset-specific transpose into this layout.

The wall-normal coordinate is distance from the wall. Thus ``y = 0`` is the
no-slip surface, ``y`` increases into the flow, and the centre of a sphere of
radius ``a`` resting on the wall is sampled at ``y = a``. The two-dimensional
engine uses ``pos[:, 0]`` for streamwise position and ``pos[:, 1]`` for
spanwise position; its second coordinate is not wall-normal.

``WALL_NORMAL_SIGN`` converts derivatives with respect to wall distance into
derivatives with respect to channel ``y``. It is -1 for a top-wall slab,
where channel ``y`` decreases with distance, and +1 for a bottom-wall slab.
Use ``check_sign`` to confirm the choice from continuity.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
import os
import numpy as np
import h5py


# ===================================================================== spec
@dataclass
class DatasetSpec:
    """Conventions and externally supplied metadata for one DNS file."""
    path: str
    # dataset names inside the HDF5 file
    comp_stream: str            # streamwise velocity
    comp_normal: str            # wall-normal velocity
    comp_span: str              # spanwise velocity
    name_time: str
    # Axis roles for one snapshot, excluding the leading time axis.
    axis_span: int
    axis_stream: int
    axis_normal: int
    # coordinates
    name_x: str | None = None           # streamwise nodes
    name_z: str | None = None           # spanwise nodes
    name_wall_distance: str | None = None
    # externally supplied
    re_tau: float = np.nan
    wall: str = "bottom"                # "bottom" | "top"
    wall_normal_sign: float = np.nan    # set by check_sign(), not by hand
    n_planes_used: int | None = None    # None = all

    # --------------------------------------------------------------- geom
    def read_geometry(self):
        """
        Grid, extents and wall-normal nodes, straight from the file.

        Returns a dict. Nothing is assumed about Lx or Lz: they come from the
        coordinate arrays. A uniform axis of n points spanning a periodic
        domain has L = n * h, because the last node is one spacing short of L.
        """
        with h5py.File(self.path, "r") as f:
            shape = f[self.comp_stream].shape
            t = np.asarray(f[self.name_time][:], float).ravel()
            x = (np.asarray(f[self.name_x][:], float).ravel()
                 if self.name_x else None)
            z = (np.asarray(f[self.name_z][:], float).ravel()
                 if self.name_z else None)
            yw = (np.asarray(f[self.name_wall_distance][:], float).ravel()
                  if self.name_wall_distance else None)

        snap = shape[1:]
        g = {
            "n_t": shape[0],
            "n_span": snap[self.axis_span - 1],
            "n_stream": snap[self.axis_stream - 1],
            "n_planes": snap[self.axis_normal - 1],
            "t": t,
            "y_nodes": yw,
        }
        if yw is not None:
            # Wall distance starts at the no-slip surface and increases into
            # the flow. Lagrange stencils therefore require ascending nodes;
            # wall_normal_sign maps d/d(distance) to d/dy_channel.
            if abs(yw[0]) > 1e-9:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"wall_distance[0] = {yw[0]:.6e}, not 0. Node 0 must be "
                    "the wall itself. If this is a channel coordinate "
                    "(running -1..1) rather than a distance, convert it "
                    "first: for the bottom wall, distance = y + 1; for the "
                    "top wall, distance = 1 - y.")
            if not np.all(np.diff(yw) > 0):
                raise ValueError(
                    "wall_distance is not strictly ascending. dataset."
                    "lagrange_weights assumes ascending nodes and "
                    "np.searchsorted will silently return nonsense "
                    "otherwise. Reverse it, and reverse the plane axis of "
                    "the velocity arrays with it.")
        for nm, arr, n in (("x", x, g["n_stream"]), ("z", z, g["n_span"])):
            if arr is None:
                continue
            d = np.diff(arr)
            uniform = bool(np.allclose(d, d[0], rtol=1e-10))
            if not uniform:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"{nm} axis is NOT uniform (spacing {d.min():.4e} .. "
                    f"{d.max():.4e}). The spectral derivatives in structures.py "
                    "and the map_coordinates sampling in slab_interp both "
                    "assume a uniform wall-parallel grid.")
            g[f"h{nm}"] = float(d[0])
            g[f"L{nm}"] = float(len(arr) * d[0])
        # Catmull-Rom interpolation in slab_interp assumes uniform time steps.
        dt = np.diff(t)
        g["dt"] = float(np.median(dt))
        g["t_uniform"] = bool(np.allclose(dt, dt[0], rtol=1e-6))
        return g

    # ------------------------------------------------------------ loading
    def load_snapshot(self, k, planes=None, f=None):
        """
        Snapshot `k` in canonical (spanwise, streamwise, plane) layout.

        This function contains the dataset-specific axis mapping. It returns
        (u_stream, v_normal, w_span), each (n_span, n_stream, n_planes).

        planes : sequence of plane indices to retain. Restricting the read also
                 limits subsequent spline filtering to the required planes.
        f      : an already-open h5py.File, to avoid reopening in a loop.
        """
        close = f is None
        f = h5py.File(self.path, "r") if close else f
        try:
            out = []
            for nm in (self.comp_stream, self.comp_normal, self.comp_span):
                a = np.asarray(f[nm][k], float)
                # (axis_span, axis_stream, axis_normal) -> canonical order
                order = (self.axis_span - 1, self.axis_stream - 1,
                         self.axis_normal - 1)
                a = np.transpose(a, order)
                if planes is not None:
                    a = a[:, :, list(planes)]
                out.append(a)
        finally:
            if close:
                f.close()
        return tuple(out)

    def load_plane_stack(self, k, plane, f=None):
        """One wall-parallel plane of each component, (n_span, n_stream)."""
        u, v, w = self.load_snapshot(k, planes=[plane], f=f)
        return u[:, :, 0], v[:, :, 0], w[:, :, 0]

    # -------------------------------------------------------- wall scales
    def wall_scales(self, n_snapshots=40, plane=1):
        """
        Estimate ``u_tau``, ``nu`` and ``t_nu`` from the data for a given
        ``Re_tau``.

        With h = 1 the wall gradient fixes everything:
            u_tau = (du/dy|wall) / Re_tau
            nu    = u_tau / Re_tau
            t_nu  = 1 / (Re_tau * u_tau)

        du/dy|wall is taken as <u>_plane / y_plane averaged over snapshots,
        which is exact only in the linear sublayer. ``plane`` should therefore
        be the first node away from the wall.

        The estimate averages over each plane and over snapshots to reduce
        spatial and temporal variability.
        """
        g = self.read_geometry()
        y = g["y_nodes"]
        if y is None:
            raise ValueError("no wall_distance in this file; supply y_nodes")
        ks = np.linspace(0, g["n_t"] - 1, min(n_snapshots, g["n_t"])).astype(int)
        acc = 0.0
        with h5py.File(self.path, "r") as f:
            for k in ks:
                u, _, _ = self.load_plane_stack(int(k), plane, f=f)
                acc += float(u.mean())
        u_mean = acc / len(ks)
        dudy = u_mean / y[plane]
        u_tau = dudy / self.re_tau
        nu = u_tau / self.re_tau
        return {"dudy_wall": dudy, "u_tau": u_tau, "nu": nu,
                "t_nu": 1.0 / (self.re_tau * u_tau),
                "delta_nu": 1.0 / self.re_tau,
                "y_plus": y * self.re_tau, "n_snapshots": len(ks),
                "plane_used": plane}



# ==================================================================== config
#
# User inputs comprise defaults, command-line overrides, help text, and a JSON
# cache for automatically detected values.
#
#     python dataset.py --show                     what is configured now
#     python dataset.py --re-tau 360               set one field and save
#     python dataset.py --redetect                 re-measure every "auto"
#     python dataset.py --wall-normal-sign +1      pin it by hand
#
# "auto" requests measurement from the data; explicit values are retained.
#
CONFIG_PATH = "dataset_config.json"

DEFAULTS = {
    "path": "velocity_bottom_10.h5",
    "re_tau": 360.0,
    "wall": "bottom",
    "wall_normal_sign": "auto",
    "comp_stream": "auto",
    "comp_normal": "auto",
    "comp_span": "auto",
    "axis_stream": "auto",
    "axis_normal": "auto",
    "axis_span": "auto",
    "name_time": "auto",
    "name_x": "auto",
    "name_z": "auto",
    "name_wall_distance": "auto",
    "wn_degree": 2,
}

HELP = {
    "path": "the HDF5 slab file",
    "re_tau": "friction Reynolds number -- the ONE externally supplied number",
    "wall": "which wall the slab was cut from: bottom | top",
    "wall_normal_sign": "auto | +1 | -1. Converts d/d(wall distance) into "
                        "d/dy_channel. auto picks whichever closes continuity",
    "comp_stream": "dataset holding the streamwise velocity (auto = the one "
                   "with a large plane-mean)",
    "comp_normal": "dataset holding the wall-normal velocity",
    "comp_span": "dataset holding the spanwise velocity",
    "axis_stream": "which snapshot axis is streamwise (1-based, incl. time)",
    "axis_normal": "which snapshot axis is wall-normal",
    "axis_span": "which snapshot axis is spanwise",
    "name_time": "time dataset",
    "name_x": "streamwise coordinate dataset",
    "name_z": "spanwise coordinate dataset",
    "name_wall_distance": "wall-normal coordinate, as DISTANCE FROM THE WALL",
    "wn_degree": "wall-normal Lagrange degree. 2 reproduces the old 3-plane "
                 "scheme exactly; 3+ needs 4+ planes",
}

_INT_KEYS = ("axis_stream", "axis_normal", "axis_span", "wn_degree")
_FLOAT_KEYS = ("re_tau", "wall_normal_sign")


def _resolve(fname):
    """
    Resolve a relative data-file path.

    Search the supplied path, the configuration directory, the module
    directory, and the parent of the current working directory, in that order.
    """
    if not fname or os.path.isabs(fname) or os.path.exists(fname):
        return fname
    here = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    for d in (os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(CONFIG_PATH)), here,
              os.path.abspath(os.path.join(os.getcwd(), ".."))):
        cand = os.path.join(d, os.path.basename(fname))
        if os.path.exists(cand):
            return cand
    return fname


def load_config(overrides=None, path=CONFIG_PATH):
    """DEFAULTS, then the JSON cache if present, then explicit overrides."""
    import json, os
    cfg = dict(DEFAULTS)
    if not os.path.exists(path):
        alt = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)),
                           os.path.basename(path))
        if os.path.exists(alt):
            path = alt
    if os.path.exists(path):
        try:
            cfg.update(json.load(open(path)))
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"  (ignoring unreadable {path}: {e})")
    cfg.update(overrides or {})
    cfg["path"] = _resolve(cfg.get("path"))
    return cfg


def save_config(cfg, path=CONFIG_PATH):
    import json
    with open(path, "w") as f:
        json.dump(cfg, f, indent=2, sort_keys=True)
    return path


def detect_orientation(path, n_snapshots=6):
    """
    Infer axis and component roles from near-wall flow statistics.

    The streamwise component has a large plane mean, while the wall-normal
    component has the smallest near-wall rms (``v ~ y^2`` versus
    ``u, w ~ y``). Streamwise fluctuations remain correlated farther along
    the elongated streak direction than across it.
    """
    with h5py.File(path, "r") as f:
        table = {k.lower().replace("_", ""): k for k in f.keys()}
        get = lambda *n: next((table[x.lower().replace("_", "")]
                               for x in n if x.lower().replace("_", "") in table),
                              None)
        comps = {r: get(f"Velocity{r}", f"Velocity_{r}") for r in "XYZ"}
        if None in comps.values():
            raise KeyError(f"no three velocity datasets in {list(f.keys())}")
        wd_name = get("wall_distance")
        if wd_name is None:
            raise KeyError("no wall_distance dataset")
        wd = np.asarray(f[wd_name][:], float).ravel()
        snap = f[comps["X"]].shape[1:]

        cand = [i for i, n in enumerate(snap) if n == len(wd)]
        if len(cand) != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"wall_distance ({len(wd)}) matches "
                             f"{len(cand)} axes of {snap}; ambiguous")
        ax_n = cand[0]

        ks = np.linspace(0, f[comps["X"]].shape[0] - 1,
                         min(n_snapshots, f[comps["X"]].shape[0])).astype(int)
        mean, rms_near = {}, {}
        for r in "XYZ":
            sl_t, sl_n = [slice(None)] * 3, [slice(None)] * 3
            sl_t[ax_n], sl_n[ax_n] = len(wd) - 1, 1
            m = rn = 0.0
            for k in ks:
                a = np.asarray(f[comps[r]][int(k)][tuple(sl_t)], float)
                m += abs(a.mean()) / max(a.std(), 1e-30)
                rn += float(np.asarray(f[comps[r]][int(k)][tuple(sl_n)],
                                       float).std())
            mean[r], rms_near[r] = m / len(ks), rn / len(ks)
        c_stream = max(mean, key=mean.get)
        rest = [r for r in "XYZ" if r != c_stream]
        c_normal = min(rest, key=lambda r: rms_near[r])
        c_span = [r for r in rest if r != c_normal][0]

        # Infer wall-parallel axis roles from streak anisotropy.
        sl = [slice(None)] * 3
        sl[ax_n] = len(wd) - 1
        U = np.asarray(f[comps[c_stream]][int(ks[len(ks) // 2])][tuple(sl)],
                       float)
        keep = [i for i in range(3) if i != ax_n]
        Ls = {}
        for pos, ax in enumerate(keep):
            Up = U - U.mean()
            n = Up.shape[pos]
            var = float((Up * Up).mean())
            c = np.array([float((Up * np.roll(Up, m, axis=pos)).mean()) / var
                          for m in range(n // 2)])
            below = np.where(c < 1 / np.e)[0]
            Ls[ax] = float(below[0]) / n if len(below) else 1.0
        ax_s = max(Ls, key=Ls.get)
        ax_z = min(Ls, key=Ls.get)

        cname = {}
        for c in "XYZ":
            k = get(c)
            if k is not None and f[k].size < 10_000:
                cname[c] = k
        name_x = next((cname[c] for c in cname
                       if len(np.ravel(f[cname[c]][:])) == snap[ax_s]), None)
        name_z = next((cname[c] for c in cname
                       if len(np.ravel(f[cname[c]][:])) == snap[ax_z]), None)

    return {
        "comp_stream": comps[c_stream], "comp_normal": comps[c_normal],
        "comp_span": comps[c_span],
        "axis_stream": ax_s + 1, "axis_normal": ax_n + 1, "axis_span": ax_z + 1,
        "name_time": None, "name_x": name_x, "name_z": name_z,
        "name_wall_distance": wd_name,
        "_anisotropy": max(Ls.values()) / max(min(Ls.values()), 1e-30),
        "_mean_ratio": mean[c_stream],
    }


def build_spec(cfg=None, verbose=True, save=True):
    """
    A DatasetSpec from the config, resolving every "auto" field by measuring.

    Resolved values are written back to the JSON cache, so the detection cost
    is paid once. Delete dataset_config.json or pass --redetect to redo it.
    """
    cfg = load_config() if cfg is None else dict(cfg)
    path = cfg["path"]
    auto = [k for k, v in cfg.items() if isinstance(v, str) and v == "auto"]

    if any(k in auto for k in ("comp_stream", "comp_normal", "comp_span",
                               "axis_stream", "axis_normal", "axis_span",
                               "name_x", "name_z", "name_wall_distance")):
        det = detect_orientation(path)
        if verbose:
            print(f"  detected orientation: stream={det['comp_stream']} "
                  f"axis {det['axis_stream']}, span={det['comp_span']} axis "
                  f"{det['axis_span']}, normal={det['comp_normal']} axis "
                  f"{det['axis_normal']}")
            print(f"    mean/rms {det['_mean_ratio']:.1f}, streak anisotropy "
                  f"{det['_anisotropy']:.1f}x")
        if det["_anisotropy"] < 2.0:
            raise ValueError(
                f"axis detection is unreliable on this file: the streamwise/"
                f"spanwise correlation-length ratio is only "
                f"{det['_anisotropy']:.2f}, and near-wall streaks should give "
                f"well over 2.\n"
                "Either the plane used has no streak structure, or the field "
                "is not what it looks like. Run check_orientation.py to see "
                "the full reasoning, then PIN the axes by hand:\n"
                "    python dataset.py --axis-stream 3 --axis-span 1 "
                "--axis-normal 2")
        for k, v in det.items():
            if not k.startswith("_") and cfg.get(k) == "auto" and v is not None:
                cfg[k] = v
    if cfg.get("name_time") in ("auto", None):
        with h5py.File(path, "r") as f:
            table = {k.lower().replace("_", ""): k for k in f.keys()}
            cfg["name_time"] = table.get("time", table.get("t"))

    spec = DatasetSpec(
        path=path,
        comp_stream=cfg["comp_stream"], comp_normal=cfg["comp_normal"],
        comp_span=cfg["comp_span"], name_time=cfg["name_time"],
        axis_span=int(cfg["axis_span"]), axis_stream=int(cfg["axis_stream"]),
        axis_normal=int(cfg["axis_normal"]),
        name_x=cfg["name_x"], name_z=cfg["name_z"],
        name_wall_distance=cfg["name_wall_distance"],
        re_tau=float(cfg["re_tau"]), wall=cfg["wall"],
        wall_normal_sign=np.nan,
    )

    spec._suspect = False
    if cfg["wall_normal_sign"] == "auto":
        d = check_sign(spec, k=0, verbose=verbose, detail=True)
        spec.wall_normal_sign = d["sign"]
        cfg["wall_normal_sign"] = float(d["sign"])
        if d["margin"] < 10 or d["residual"] > 0.25:
            # Cache the sign only when one choice closes continuity clearly;
            # a weak margin usually indicates an incorrect component or axis
            # assignment rather than an ambiguous derivative sign.
            spec._suspect = True
            save = False
            print("  !! NOT SAVING this configuration: continuity does not "
                  f"close for either sign\n     (best residual "
                  f"{100*d['residual']:.1f} %, margin {d['margin']:.1f}x). "
                  "The component/axis\n     pairing is probably wrong. Run "
                  "check_orientation.py, then pin it:\n"
                  "       python dataset.py --axis-stream N --axis-span N "
                  "--axis-normal N \\\n"
                  "                         --comp-stream Velocity_? "
                  "--wall-normal-sign auto")
    else:
        spec.wall_normal_sign = float(cfg["wall_normal_sign"])

    if save:
        save_config(cfg)
    spec._cfg = cfg
    return spec


def show_config(cfg=None):
    cfg = load_config() if cfg is None else cfg
    print(f"dataset configuration   ({CONFIG_PATH})")
    for k in DEFAULTS:
        v = cfg.get(k)
        flag = "  [auto]" if isinstance(v, str) and v == "auto" else ""
        print(f"  {k:<20} = {str(v):<22}{flag}  {HELP[k]}")
    return cfg


def _cli(argv):
    import argparse
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="DNS dataset configuration",
                                 formatter_class=argparse.RawTextHelpFormatter)
    for k, v in DEFAULTS.items():
        ap.add_argument(f"--{k.replace('_', '-')}", default=None, help=HELP[k])
    ap.add_argument("--show", action="store_true", help="print and exit")
    ap.add_argument("--redetect", action="store_true",
                    help="reset every field back to auto and re-measure")
    a = ap.parse_args(argv)

    if a.redetect:
        import os
        if os.path.exists(CONFIG_PATH):
            os.remove(CONFIG_PATH)
            print(f"  removed {CONFIG_PATH}")
    over = {}
    for k in DEFAULTS:
        v = getattr(a, k)
        if v is None:
            continue
        if v != "auto":
            if k in _INT_KEYS:
                v = int(v)
            elif k in _FLOAT_KEYS:
                v = float(v)
        over[k] = v
    cfg = load_config(over)
    if a.show:
        show_config(cfg)
        return
    spec = build_spec(cfg)
    print()
    show_config(spec._cfg)
    print()
    probe(spec)


# ================================================================ registry
#
# SPEC is built from the configuration. Update it with
#     python dataset.py --re-tau 360 --wall-normal-sign auto
# or edit dataset_config.json. SLAB395 remains available as a reference spec.

SLAB395 = DatasetSpec(
    path="Slab_complete.h5",
    comp_stream="VelocityX", comp_normal="VelocityY", comp_span="VelocityZ",
    name_time="t",
    axis_span=1, axis_stream=2, axis_normal=3,
    re_tau=395.0, wall="top", wall_normal_sign=-1.0,
)

SPEC = None      # built below, once check_sign is defined



# =================================================================== probe
def probe(path_or_spec=SPEC, n_check=6):
    """
    Print the geometry and diagnostics needed to configure a dataset.
    """
    spec = path_or_spec
    if isinstance(path_or_spec, str):
        spec = _sniff(path_or_spec)
        print(f"sniffed a spec for {path_or_spec}\n")

    print("=" * 70)
    print(f"DATASET  {spec.path}")
    print("=" * 70)

    with h5py.File(spec.path, "r") as f:
        print("\ncontents:")
        f.visititems(lambda n, o: print(f"    {n:<22} {o.shape} {o.dtype}")
                     if isinstance(o, h5py.Dataset) else None)

    g = spec.read_geometry()
    print(f"\ngrid:")
    print(f"    spanwise   {g['n_span']:4d}"
          + (f"   hz = {g['hz']:.8f}   Lz = {g['Lz']:.6f}"
             f"  ({g['Lz']/np.pi:.4f} pi)" if "hz" in g else ""))
    print(f"    streamwise {g['n_stream']:4d}"
          + (f"   hx = {g['hx']:.8f}   Lx = {g['Lx']:.6f}"
             f"  ({g['Lx']/np.pi:.4f} pi)" if "hx" in g else ""))
    print(f"    planes     {g['n_planes']:4d}")
    print(f"    snapshots  {g['n_t']:4d}   t = {g['t'][0]:.4f} .. "
          f"{g['t'][-1]:.4f}   dt = {g['dt']:.6f}")
    if not g["t_uniform"]:
        print("    !! snapshot spacing is NOT uniform. slab_interp's "
              "Catmull-Rom\n       weights assume it is -- use "
              "time_order='linear' or reweight.")

    y = g["y_nodes"]
    if y is not None:
        print(f"\nwall-normal nodes (wall_distance):")
        print(f"    {np.array2string(y, precision=6)}")
        print(f"    y+ = {np.array2string(y * spec.re_tau, precision=3)}"
              f"   (Re_tau = {spec.re_tau:g})")
        print(f"    ascending    : {bool(np.all(np.diff(y) > 0))}")
        print(f"    slab depth   : {y[-1]:.6e}  =  y+ {y[-1]*spec.re_tau:.2f}")
        cheb = 1 - np.cos(np.pi * np.arange(len(y)) / 192)
        print(f"    Chebyshev(193) match to {np.abs(y - cheb).max():.2e}")

    # no-slip: which plane is the wall?
    print("\nwall plane check (|u| per plane, snapshot 0):")
    u, v, w = spec.load_snapshot(0)
    for p in range(u.shape[2]):
        print(f"    plane {p:2d}  <|u|> = {np.abs(u[:, :, p]).mean():.4e}"
              f"   <|v|> = {np.abs(v[:, :, p]).mean():.4e}"
              f"   <|w|> = {np.abs(w[:, :, p]).mean():.4e}")
    print("    -> plane 0 should be zero to roundoff; if not, the axis "
          "mapping is wrong")

    print(f"\ncanonical snapshot shape (span, stream, plane): {u.shape}")

    if not np.isnan(spec.re_tau):
        ws = spec.wall_scales(n_snapshots=n_check, plane=1)
        print(f"\nwall scales (measured over {ws['n_snapshots']} snapshots):")
        print(f"    du/dy|wall = {ws['dudy_wall']:.4f}")
        print(f"    u_tau      = {ws['u_tau']:.6f}")
        print(f"    nu         = {ws['nu']:.5e}")
        print(f"    t_nu       = {ws['t_nu']:.6f}")
        if not (0.04 <= ws["u_tau"] <= 0.08):
            print(f"    !! u_tau = {ws['u_tau']:.4f} is outside the range a "
                  "unit-normalised\n       channel should give. The Re label "
                  "is probably not Re_tau.")
    print()
    return spec, g


def _sniff(path):
    """Construct a provisional specification from an unfamiliar file."""
    with h5py.File(path, "r") as f:
        keys = list(f.keys())
        cand = {k.lower().replace("_", ""): k for k in keys}
        get = lambda *n: next((cand[x] for x in n if x in cand), None)
        stream, normal, span = (get("velocityx"), get("velocityy"),
                                get("velocityz"))
        tname = get("t", "time")
        shape = f[stream].shape[1:]
        nx = len(f[get("x")][:].ravel()) if get("x") else None
        nz = len(f[get("z")][:].ravel()) if get("z") else None
    ax = {}
    for i, n in enumerate(shape, start=1):
        if nz is not None and n == nz and "span" not in ax:
            ax["span"] = i
        elif nx is not None and n == nx and "stream" not in ax:
            ax["stream"] = i
        else:
            ax["normal"] = i
    return DatasetSpec(path=path, comp_stream=stream, comp_normal=normal,
                       comp_span=span, name_time=tname,
                       axis_span=ax.get("span", 1),
                       axis_stream=ax.get("stream", 2),
                       axis_normal=ax.get("normal", 3),
                       name_x=get("x"), name_z=get("z"),
                       name_wall_distance=get("walldistance"))


# =============================================================== sign test
def check_sign(spec=None, k=0, plane=None, verbose=True,
               detail=False):
    """
    Determine ``WALL_NORMAL_SIGN`` from the continuity residual.

    For incompressible flow, ``du/dx + dv/dy + dw/dz = 0``. The periodic
    in-plane terms are evaluated spectrally, leaving the wall-normal sign as
    the discrete choice tested here.

    Return the sign and optionally print both residuals. A margin below about
    10 suggests that the component or axis assignment should also be checked.
    """
    spec = SPEC if spec is None else spec
    g = spec.read_geometry()
    y = g["y_nodes"]
    plane = (len(y) // 2) if plane is None else plane
    k = int(np.clip(k, 0, g["n_t"] - 1))
    u, v, w = spec.load_snapshot(k)

    def dsp(F, ax, h):
        n = F.shape[ax]
        kk = np.fft.fftfreq(n, d=h / (2 * np.pi))
        sh = [1, 1]; sh[ax] = n
        return np.real(np.fft.ifft(1j * kk.reshape(sh) * np.fft.fft(F, axis=ax),
                                   axis=ax))

    dudx = dsp(u[:, :, plane], 1, g["hx"])
    dwdz = dsp(w[:, :, plane], 0, g["hz"])
    # Central Lagrange derivative on the physical, potentially nonuniform nodes.
    lo = max(plane - 1, 0); hi = min(plane + 1, len(y) - 1)
    dvdy_mag = _lagrange3_derivative(v[:, :, [lo, plane, hi]],
                                     y[[lo, plane, hi]], at=1)

    out = {}
    for s in (+1.0, -1.0):
        res = dudx + dwdz + s * dvdy_mag
        scale = np.sqrt(np.mean(dudx**2) + np.mean(dwdz**2)
                        + np.mean(dvdy_mag**2))
        out[s] = float(res.std() / max(scale, 1e-30))
    best = min(out, key=out.get)
    ratio = out[-best] / max(out[best], 1e-30)
    if verbose:
        print(f"  continuity at plane {plane} (snapshot {k}): "
              f"sign +1 -> {100*out[+1.0]:.2f} %,  "
              f"sign -1 -> {100*out[-1.0]:.2f} %")
        print(f"  -> wall_normal_sign = {best:+.0f}  ({ratio:.0f}x margin, "
              f"residual {100*out[best]:.2f} %)")
    if ratio < 10:
        print("  !! weak margin: suspect the component/axis pairing rather "
              "than the sign")
    if detail:
        return {"sign": best, "margin": ratio, "residual": out[best]}
    return best


def _lagrange3_derivative(F3, y3, at=1):
    """d/d(distance) at node `at` from three nodes at true positions y3."""
    z0, z1, z2 = y3
    zc = y3[at]
    dL0 = ((zc - z1) + (zc - z2)) / ((z0 - z1) * (z0 - z2))
    dL1 = ((zc - z0) + (zc - z2)) / ((z1 - z0) * (z1 - z2))
    dL2 = ((zc - z0) + (zc - z1)) / ((z2 - z0) * (z2 - z1))
    return F3[:, :, 0] * dL0 + F3[:, :, 1] * dL1 + F3[:, :, 2] * dL2


# ======================================================= n-plane wall-normal
def lagrange_weights(y_nodes, y_wall, degree=2):
    """
    Local Lagrange weights for an arbitrary number of planes.

    A local stencil of ``degree + 1`` nodes is centred near each query height.
    This avoids the conditioning and cost of a single high-degree polynomial
    over the full wall-normal grid.

    Returns (idx, wts), each (n_query, degree + 1), so that
        u(y_wall) = sum_m wts[:, m] * u_plane[idx[:, m]]

    With three planes and ``degree=2``, this reduces to the quadratic scheme.
    """
    y = np.asarray(y_nodes, float)
    y_wall = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(y_wall, float))
    n, m = len(y), degree + 1
    if m > n:
        raise ValueError(f"degree {degree} needs {m} planes, file has {n}")
    zc = np.clip(y_wall, y[0], y[-1])
    # Left edge of a stencil centred on the query point.
    j = np.searchsorted(y, zc) - 1
    j = np.clip(j - (m - 2) // 2, 0, n - m)
    idx = j[:, None] + np.arange(m)[None, :]
    yy = y[idx]                                  # (nq, m)
    num = zc[:, None] - yy                       # (nq, m)
    wts = np.ones_like(yy)
    for a in range(m):
        for b in range(m):
            if a == b:
                continue
            wts[:, a] *= num[:, b] / (yy[:, a] - yy[:, b])
    return idx, wts


# ------------------------------------------------ build the active spec
try:
    SPEC = build_spec(verbose=False)
except Exception as _e:                       # file missing, or unreadable
    SPEC = DatasetSpec(
        path=DEFAULTS["path"],
        comp_stream="Velocity_X", comp_normal="Velocity_Y",
        comp_span="Velocity_Z", name_time="time",
        axis_span=1, axis_normal=2, axis_stream=3,
        name_x="X", name_z="Z", name_wall_distance="wall_distance",
        re_tau=float(DEFAULTS["re_tau"]), wall=DEFAULTS["wall"],
        wall_normal_sign=np.nan,
    )
    SPEC._config_error = _e
    print(f"dataset.py: could not auto-configure from "
          f"{DEFAULTS['path']!r}\n  ({type(_e).__name__}: {_e})\n"
          f"  Run:  python dataset.py --show      to see the configuration\n"
          f"        python dataset.py --path YOURFILE.h5 --re-tau 360")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import sys
    _cli(sys.argv[1:])
slab_interp.pySpatial and temporal interpolation of the near-wall DNS slabSee code ↗

The wall-unit conversion is kept explicit at the interpolation boundary.

"""Interpolate velocity ``u(x, z, y, t)`` in a near-wall DNS slab.

The component order is streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal, denoted
``(x, z, y)``. Canonical snapshot arrays have layout
``(spanwise, streamwise, plane)``. Wall-normal ``y`` is distance from the
wall, with ``y = 0`` at the no-slip surface. The two-dimensional engine uses
its second position coordinate for spanwise location, not wall-normal height.

Lengths and velocities remain in the file's nondimensional units, with
``h = 1`` and ``y+ = y Re_tau``. Wall-parallel interpolation uses periodic
cubic B-splines, wall-normal interpolation uses a local Lagrange stencil of
``WN_DEGREE + 1`` nodes, and time interpolation uses Catmull-Rom weights for
uniformly spaced snapshots. ``WALL_NORMAL_SIGN`` converts derivatives with
respect to wall distance into derivatives with respect to channel ``y``.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
from collections import namedtuple
import numpy as np
import h5py
from scipy.ndimage import spline_filter, map_coordinates

import dataset as _ds
from dataset import lagrange_weights

try:                                   # tensor-product B-spline evaluator
    from numba import njit as _njit    # (values + in-plane derivatives)
    _NUMBA = True
except ImportError:                    # pure-numpy fallback, same numbers
    _NUMBA = False

    def _njit(*a, **k):
        def deco(f):
            return f
        return deco if not (len(a) == 1 and callable(a[0])) else a[0]

#: Velocity in pipeline order: streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal.
#: Values may be unpacked positionally or accessed by attribute, so
#: `u, w, v = itp(...)` and `f.stream` / `f.wall_normal` are both valid.
Velocity = namedtuple("Velocity", "stream span wall_normal")

# Wall-normal degree 2 gives the three-plane quadratic scheme.
WN_DEGREE = 2

# ------------------------------------------------------------- wall scales
#
# Re_tau and nu are declared inputs: nu is the solver input
# (dnsflags.txt: %nu), and Re_tau is the stated friction Reynolds number.
# The remaining viscous scales follow from this pair:
#
#     u_tau    = Re_tau * nu / h        (h = 1)
#     delta_nu = nu / u_tau = 1/Re_tau
#     t_nu     = nu / u_tau**2
#
# The wall-gradient estimate is retained as an independent consistency check.
# Disagreement may reflect limited sublayer resolution, a one-sided gradient
# on a stretched grid, or an inconsistent Re_tau assignment.
#
# Particle trajectories use tau_p in file time units. Wall quantities are
# reporting labels and do not enter the engine dynamics.
#
NU_DECLARED = 1.25e-4       # dnsflags.txt %nu. None -> fall back to measured.
RE_TAU_DECLARED = 360.0     # None -> use the spec's re_tau.

# --------------------------------------------------------------- configure
_SPEC = None
_GEOM = None


_MEASURED = {}
_PROVENANCE = {"wall_scales": "unset"}


def configure(spec=None, wn_degree=None, measure_wall=True, n_snapshots=40,
              re_tau=None, nu=None):
    """
    Read a ``DatasetSpec`` and refresh the module-level geometry and scales.

    The function runs once at import with ``dataset.SPEC``. Modules that import
    constants directly retain their existing values, so reconfiguration should
    occur before those modules are imported.
    """
    global _SPEC, _GEOM, WN_DEGREE
    global N_SPAN, N_STREAM, N_PLANES, LX, LZ, LY, HX, HZ, Y_NODES
    global RE_TAU, DELTA_NU, DUDY_WALL, U_TAU, NU, T_NU, WALL_NORMAL_SIGN
    global T_FIRST, T_LAST, DT_SNAP

    _SPEC = _ds.SPEC if spec is None else spec
    if wn_degree is not None:
        WN_DEGREE = int(wn_degree)
    _GEOM = g = _SPEC.read_geometry()

    N_SPAN = g["n_span"]
    N_STREAM = g["n_stream"]
    N_PLANES = g["n_planes"]
    HX = g["hx"]                       # streamwise spacing
    HZ = g["hz"]                       # spanwise   spacing
    LX = g["Lx"]                       # streamwise domain
    LZ = g["Lz"]                       # spanwise   domain
    LY = 2.0                           # full channel height, 2h
    Y_NODES = np.asarray(g["y_nodes"], float)

    T_FIRST, T_LAST, DT_SNAP = float(g["t"][0]), float(g["t"][-1]), g["dt"]

    # --- declared inputs -------------------------------------------------
    RE_TAU = float(_SPEC.re_tau if re_tau is None else re_tau)
    if RE_TAU_DECLARED is not None and re_tau is None:
        RE_TAU = float(RE_TAU_DECLARED)

    _nu_declared = NU_DECLARED if nu is None else nu

    # Independent wall-gradient estimate for consistency reporting.
    if measure_wall:
        ws = _SPEC.wall_scales(n_snapshots=n_snapshots, plane=1)
        DUDY_WALL = float(ws["dudy_wall"])
        _MEASURED.update(u_tau=float(ws["u_tau"]), nu=float(ws["nu"]),
                         t_nu=float(ws["t_nu"]), dudy_wall=DUDY_WALL)
    else:
        DUDY_WALL = np.nan
        _MEASURED.update(u_tau=np.nan, nu=np.nan, t_nu=np.nan,
                         dudy_wall=np.nan)

    # Derive a consistent viscous-scale set from the declared pair.
    if _nu_declared is not None:
        NU = float(_nu_declared)
        U_TAU = RE_TAU * NU                 # h = 1
        DELTA_NU = NU / U_TAU               # == 1/RE_TAU, by construction
        T_NU = NU / U_TAU ** 2
        _PROVENANCE["wall_scales"] = "declared"
    else:
        NU = _MEASURED["nu"]
        U_TAU = _MEASURED["u_tau"]
        T_NU = _MEASURED["t_nu"]
        DELTA_NU = 1.0 / RE_TAU
        _PROVENANCE["wall_scales"] = "measured"

    # Check that the viscous length uses the same Re_tau as wall-unit labels.
    if not (np.isfinite(RE_TAU) and RE_TAU > 0):
        raise ValueError(f"RE_TAU must be finite and positive, got {RE_TAU!r}")
    if not np.isclose(DELTA_NU, 1.0 / RE_TAU, rtol=1e-12, atol=0):
        raise AssertionError(
            f"delta_nu ({DELTA_NU:.8e}) != 1/Re_tau ({1.0 / RE_TAU:.8e}). "
            f"The viscous length and the declared Re_tau disagree, which is "
            f"the mixed-provenance bug this block exists to prevent.")

    WALL_NORMAL_SIGN = _SPEC.wall_normal_sign
    if np.isnan(WALL_NORMAL_SIGN):
        raise ValueError(
            "wall_normal_sign is unset for this dataset. Run\n"
            "    python -c 'import dataset; dataset.check_sign()'\n"
            "and put the result in the spec. It is +1 for a bottom-wall slab "
            "and -1 for a top-wall slab, but do not set it from that rule -- "
            "a wrong sign does not raise, it silently turns a ~1% continuity "
            "residual into ~140%.")
    return g


configure()


def to_wall_units(y_wall):
    """Convert wall distance to ``y+ = y Re_tau`` for ``h = 1``."""
    return np.asarray(y_wall, float) * RE_TAU


def from_wall_units(y_plus):
    """Convert ``y+`` to wall distance using ``y = y+ / Re_tau``."""
    return np.asarray(y_plus, float) / RE_TAU


def check_round_trip(y=None):
    """
    Check the numerical round trip ``y -> y+ -> y``.

    The pipeline accepts outer-unit lengths; wall units are intended for
    reporting. The check ensures that both conversions use the same factor.
    """
    y = np.array([1.0, 2.00775e-4, 5.35413e-4]) if y is None else \
        np.asarray(y, float)
    back = from_wall_units(to_wall_units(y))
    if not np.array_equal(back, y):
        raise AssertionError(
            f"wall-unit round trip is lossy: max|diff| = "
            f"{np.abs(back - y).max():.3e}")
    return True


def wall_scale_consistency(verbose=True):
    """
    Compare declared viscous scales with estimates from the wall gradient.

    The gradient estimate uses the first wall-normal nodes and can be biased
    when the linear sublayer is poorly resolved. The returned differences
    quantify that sensitivity for downstream reporting.
    """
    m = dict(_MEASURED)
    out = {"provenance": _PROVENANCE["wall_scales"],
           "re_tau_declared": RE_TAU,
           "nu_declared": NU, "u_tau_declared": U_TAU, "t_nu_declared": T_NU,
           "nu_measured": m.get("nu", np.nan),
           "u_tau_measured": m.get("u_tau", np.nan),
           "t_nu_measured": m.get("t_nu", np.nan),
           "dudy_wall": m.get("dudy_wall", np.nan)}
    um = out["u_tau_measured"]
    out["re_tau_measured"] = (um / out["nu_measured"]
                              if np.isfinite(um) and out["nu_measured"] else
                              np.nan)
    out["u_tau_rel_gap"] = (um / U_TAU - 1.0) if np.isfinite(um) else np.nan
    # Re_tau, nu, and the wall gradient satisfy
    #     du/dy|wall = Re_tau**2 * nu        (h = 1)
    # so any two determine the third.
    out["dudy_implied"] = RE_TAU ** 2 * NU
    out["dudy_rel_gap"] = ((out["dudy_wall"] / out["dudy_implied"] - 1.0)
                           if np.isfinite(out["dudy_wall"]) else np.nan)
    if verbose:
        print("wall scales")
        print(f"  provenance : {out['provenance']}")
        print(f"  declared   : Re_tau = {RE_TAU:g}   nu = {NU:.6e}   "
              f"u_tau = {U_TAU:.6f}   delta_nu = {DELTA_NU:.6e}   "
              f"t_nu = {T_NU:.6f}")
        if np.isfinite(um):
            print(f"  measured   : Re_tau = {out['re_tau_measured']:.1f}   "
                  f"nu = {out['nu_measured']:.6e}   u_tau = {um:.6f}   "
                  f"(du/dy|wall = {out['dudy_wall']:.4f})")
            print(f"  gradient   : implied by the declared pair = "
                  f"{out['dudy_implied']:.4f}   measured = "
                  f"{out['dudy_wall']:.4f}   "
                  f"({100 * out['dudy_rel_gap']:+.1f}%)")
            print(f"  u_tau gap  : {100 * out['u_tau_rel_gap']:+.2f}%"
                  + ("   <-- large; see the docstring before quoting y+"
                     if abs(out["u_tau_rel_gap"]) > 0.05 else ""))
            print("  Re_tau, nu and du/dy are one relation "
                  "(du/dy = Re_tau^2 nu); two of them were declared, so the "
                  "third is a consequence, not a measurement.")
        else:
            print("  measured   : not available (measure_wall=False)")
        print("  NOTE: labels only. tau_p drives the engine; trajectories are "
              "unchanged by any of this.")
    return out


def describe():
    """Print the active configuration. Run this after any dataset change."""
    print(f"slab_interp configured from {_SPEC.path}")
    print(f"  grid    : {N_SPAN}(span) x {N_STREAM}(stream) x {N_PLANES}(planes)")
    print(f"  extents : Lx = {LX:.6f} ({LX/np.pi:.4f} pi)   "
          f"Lz = {LZ:.6f} ({LZ/np.pi:.4f} pi)")
    print(f"  spacing : hx = {HX:.8f}   hz = {HZ:.8f}")
    print(f"  time    : {T_FIRST:.3f} .. {T_LAST:.3f}, dt = {DT_SNAP:.4f}")
    print(f"  y nodes : {np.array2string(Y_NODES, precision=6)}")
    print(f"  y+      : {np.array2string(to_wall_units(Y_NODES), precision=3)}")
    print(f"  scales  : Re_tau = {RE_TAU:g}  u_tau = {U_TAU:.6f}  "
          f"nu = {NU:.5e}  t_nu = {T_NU:.6f}  "
          f"[{_PROVENANCE['wall_scales']}]")
    _g = wall_scale_consistency(verbose=False)["u_tau_rel_gap"]
    if np.isfinite(_g) and abs(_g) > 0.05:
        print(f"            u_tau from the wall gradient differs by "
              f"{100 * _g:+.1f}% -- run wall_scale_consistency()")
    _w = getattr(_SPEC, "wall", "?")
    print(f"  wall    : {_w} wall; y = 0 is the no-slip surface and y "
          f"increases INTO the flow")
    print(f"  scheme  : wall-normal degree {WN_DEGREE}, "
          f"sign {WALL_NORMAL_SIGN:+.0f}"
          + ("  (consistent)" if
             {"bottom": 1.0, "top": -1.0}.get(_w) == WALL_NORMAL_SIGN
             else "  <-- DOES NOT MATCH THE DECLARED WALL"))


def seam_jump(spec=None, crop=None, plane=None, snapshot=200):
    """
    Size of the artificial periodic seam for a given crop, relative to a normal
    neighbour difference. 1.0 means no discontinuity.

    crop : (n_stream, n_span), or a scalar for both. A direction left at its
           full size has no seam.
    """
    spec = _SPEC if spec is None else spec
    plane = (N_PLANES - 1) if plane is None else plane
    n_stream, n_span = (crop, crop) if np.isscalar(crop) else crop
    u, _, _ = spec.load_snapshot(snapshot, planes=[plane])
    U = u[:, :, 0]                                    # (span, stream)
    nat_x = float(np.abs(np.diff(U, axis=1)).mean())
    nat_z = float(np.abs(np.diff(U, axis=0)).mean())
    Us = U[:n_span, :n_stream]
    jx = (1.0 if n_stream >= U.shape[1] else
          float(np.abs(Us[:, -1] - Us[:, 0]).mean()) / nat_x)
    jz = (1.0 if n_span >= U.shape[0] else
          float(np.abs(Us[-1, :] - Us[0, :]).mean()) / nat_z)
    fx = 0.0 if n_stream >= U.shape[1] else 4.0 / n_stream
    fz = 0.0 if n_span >= U.shape[0] else 4.0 / n_span
    return {"streamwise": jx, "spanwise": jz, "worst": max(jx, jz),
            "corrupted_fraction": 1.0 - (1.0 - fx) * (1.0 - fz)}


# ───────────────────────────────── cubic B-spline values AND derivatives
#
# scipy.ndimage.map_coordinates(order=3, prefilter=False, mode="grid-wrap")
# evaluates ``f(x) = sum_i c_i beta3(x - i)`` on the prefiltered coefficients
# cached by _coeffs(), but does not return derivatives. The kernels below use
# the same coefficients, periodic wrapping, and basis to evaluate the series
# and its analytic in-plane derivatives.
#
# Cubic B-spline weights for s = x - floor(x) in [0, 1), nodes i0-1 .. i0+2:
#   w0 = (1-s)^3/6, w1 = (3s^3-6s^2+4)/6, w2 = (-3s^3+3s^2+3s+1)/6, w3 = s^3/6
# and their s-derivatives.


@_njit(cache=False)
def _bspline_weights(s):
    s2 = s * s
    s3 = s2 * s
    w0 = (1.0 - s) ** 3 / 6.0
    w1 = (3.0 * s3 - 6.0 * s2 + 4.0) / 6.0
    w2 = (-3.0 * s3 + 3.0 * s2 + 3.0 * s + 1.0) / 6.0
    w3 = s3 / 6.0
    d0 = -0.5 * (1.0 - s) ** 2
    d1 = 0.5 * (3.0 * s2 - 4.0 * s)
    d2 = 0.5 * (-3.0 * s2 + 2.0 * s + 1.0)
    d3 = 0.5 * s2
    return w0, w1, w2, w3, d0, d1, d2, d3


@_njit(cache=False)
def _bspline2d_eval(c, q0, q1, out_v, out_d0, out_d1):
    """
    c        : (n0, n1) prefiltered cubic B-spline coefficients (periodic)
    q0, q1   : (npts,) query coordinates in grid units along axis 0 / 1
    out_v    : value;  out_d0, out_d1 : d/dq0, d/dq1 in grid units
    """
    n0 = c.shape[0]
    n1 = c.shape[1]
    for p in range(q0.shape[0]):
        x0 = q0[p]
        x1 = q1[p]
        i0 = int(np.floor(x0))
        i1 = int(np.floor(x1))
        a0, a1, a2, a3, e0, e1, e2, e3 = _bspline_weights(x0 - i0)
        b0, b1, b2, b3, f0, f1, f2, f3 = _bspline_weights(x1 - i1)
        v = 0.0
        g0 = 0.0
        g1 = 0.0
        for m in range(4):
            ii = (i0 - 1 + m) % n0
            if m == 0:
                wa = a0; da = e0
            elif m == 1:
                wa = a1; da = e1
            elif m == 2:
                wa = a2; da = e2
            else:
                wa = a3; da = e3
            row_v = 0.0
            row_d = 0.0
            for n in range(4):
                jj = (i1 - 1 + n) % n1
                cc = c[ii, jj]
                if n == 0:
                    row_v += b0 * cc; row_d += f0 * cc
                elif n == 1:
                    row_v += b1 * cc; row_d += f1 * cc
                elif n == 2:
                    row_v += b2 * cc; row_d += f2 * cc
                else:
                    row_v += b3 * cc; row_d += f3 * cc
            v += wa * row_v
            g0 += da * row_v
            g1 += wa * row_d
        out_v[p] = v
        out_d0[p] = g0
        out_d1[p] = g1


def _bspline2d_values(c, q0, q1):
    """Value + grid-unit derivatives (d/dq0, d/dq1) of the cached spline."""
    q0 = np.ascontiguousarray(np.asarray(q0, float).ravel())
    q1 = np.ascontiguousarray(np.asarray(q1, float).ravel())
    v = np.empty(q0.shape[0]); d0 = np.empty_like(v); d1 = np.empty_like(v)
    if _NUMBA:
        _bspline2d_eval(np.ascontiguousarray(c, dtype=np.float64), q0, q1,
                        v, d0, d1)
        return v, d0, d1
    # NumPy fallback with the same pointwise arithmetic.
    n0, n1 = c.shape
    i0 = np.floor(q0).astype(np.int64); i1 = np.floor(q1).astype(np.int64)
    wa = np.stack(_bspline_weights(q0 - i0), axis=0)       # (8, npts)
    wb = np.stack(_bspline_weights(q1 - i1), axis=0)
    v[:] = 0.0; d0[:] = 0.0; d1[:] = 0.0
    for m in range(4):
        ii = (i0 - 1 + m) % n0
        row_v = np.zeros_like(v); row_d = np.zeros_like(v)
        for n in range(4):
            jj = (i1 - 1 + n) % n1
            cc = c[ii, jj]
            row_v += wb[n] * cc
            row_d += wb[4 + n] * cc
        v += wa[m] * row_v
        d0 += wa[4 + m] * row_v
        d1 += wa[m] * row_d
    return v, d0, d1


#: Fluid angular velocity ``omega_f = 1/2 curl U_f`` about the streamwise,
#: spanwise, and wall-normal axes, in pipeline order.
Omega = namedtuple("Omega", "stream span wall_normal")


class SlabInterpolator:
    """
    Parameters
    ----------
    h5path : str, optional
        Overrides the spec's path. Passing a path alone is enough for the
        common case; the rest of the conventions come from the spec.
    spec : DatasetSpec, optional
    y_nodes : array_like, optional
        Wall-normal node positions (h = 1). Defaults to the file's.
    time_order : {'cubic', 'linear'}
    cache_size : int
        Snapshots held as pre-filtered spline coefficients.
    planes : sequence of int, optional
        Restrict loading and spline filtering to these planes. Query heights
        outside the retained wall-normal range are clamped.
    crop : int or (n_stream, n_span)
        Sub-block of the wall-parallel domain. Cropping a periodic field
        creates an artificial wrapped seam, which the cubic stencil spreads
        over about four cells. ``seam_jump`` quantifies this discontinuity.
    """

    def __init__(self, h5path=None, spec=None, y_nodes=None, time_order="cubic",
                 cache_size=8, crop=None, planes=None, wn_degree=None):
        self.spec = (_SPEC if spec is None else spec)
        if h5path is not None and h5path != self.spec.path:
            self.spec = _clone_spec(self.spec, h5path)
        self.h5path = self.spec.path
        self.time_order = time_order
        self.wn_degree = WN_DEGREE if wn_degree is None else int(wn_degree)

        g = self.spec.read_geometry()
        self.t = np.asarray(g["t"], float)
        self.Nt = g["n_t"]
        self.Nx = g["n_span"]            # array axis 0
        self.Ny = g["n_stream"]          # array axis 1
        self.Nz = g["n_planes"]          # array axis 2

        full_nodes = (np.asarray(g["y_nodes"], float) if y_nodes is None
                      else np.asarray(y_nodes, float))
        self.planes = (None if planes is None
                       else np.asarray(sorted(set(int(p) for p in planes)), int))
        if self.planes is not None:
            if self.planes.min() < 0 or self.planes.max() >= self.Nz:
                raise ValueError(f"planes must lie in [0, {self.Nz - 1}]")
            if len(self.planes) < self.wn_degree + 1:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"wall-normal degree {self.wn_degree} needs "
                    f"{self.wn_degree + 1} planes, {len(self.planes)} kept")
            self.y_nodes = full_nodes[self.planes]
            self.Nz = len(self.planes)
        else:
            self.y_nodes = full_nodes

        if crop is None:
            self.crop = None
        elif np.isscalar(crop):
            self.crop = (int(crop), int(crop))          # (stream, span)
        else:
            self.crop = (int(crop[0]), int(crop[1]))
        if self.crop is not None:
            n_stream, n_span = self.crop
            if not (4 <= n_stream <= g["n_stream"]):
                raise ValueError(f"streamwise crop must be in "
                                 f"[4, {g['n_stream']}], got {n_stream}")
            if not (4 <= n_span <= g["n_span"]):
                raise ValueError(f"spanwise crop must be in "
                                 f"[4, {g['n_span']}], got {n_span}")
            self.Nx, self.Ny = n_span, n_stream

        self.d0, self.d1 = g["hz"], g["hx"]     # spanwise, streamwise spacing
        self.L0 = self.Nx * self.d0             # spanwise   extent
        self.L1 = self.Ny * self.d1             # streamwise extent

        self._cache, self._order = {}, []
        self._cache_size = int(cache_size)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------- internals
    def _coeffs(self, k):
        k = int(np.clip(k, 0, self.Nt - 1))
        if k in self._cache:
            return self._cache[k]
        raw = self.spec.load_snapshot(k, planes=self.planes)   # canonical order
        if self.crop is not None:
            raw = tuple(r[:self.Nx, :self.Ny, :] for r in raw)
        out = tuple(
            np.stack([spline_filter(comp[:, :, p], order=3, mode="grid-wrap",
                                    output=np.float64)
                      for p in range(self.Nz)], axis=-1)
            for comp in raw)
        self._cache[k] = out
        self._order.append(k)
        while len(self._order) > self._cache_size:
            self._cache.pop(self._order.pop(0), None)
        return out

    def _time_bracket(self, tq):
        if not (self.t[0] - 1e-9 <= tq <= self.t[-1] + 1e-9):
            raise ValueError(
                f"t={tq} outside the record [{self.t[0]}, {self.t[-1]}]. "
                "The field is not defined there; no extrapolation is performed.")
        tq = float(np.clip(tq, self.t[0], self.t[-1]))
        k1 = int(np.searchsorted(self.t, tq, side="right"))
        k1 = min(max(k1, 1), self.Nt - 1)
        k0 = k1 - 1
        s = 0.0 if self.t[k1] == self.t[k0] else \
            (tq - self.t[k0]) / (self.t[k1] - self.t[k0])
        return k0, k1, s

    def _sample(self, k, x_stream, z_span, y_wall):
        """
        Arguments and return values use streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal order.

        Only planes touched by the local Lagrange stencils are sampled in the
        wall-parallel directions. A monodisperse query therefore uses
        ``WN_DEGREE + 1`` planes.
        """
        cX, cY, cZ = self._coeffs(k)
        coords = np.vstack([(np.asarray(z_span, float).ravel() / self.d0),
                            (np.asarray(x_stream, float).ravel() / self.d1)])
        idx, wts = lagrange_weights(self.y_nodes, np.asarray(y_wall, float).ravel(),
                                    degree=self.wn_degree)
        need = np.unique(idx)
        col = {p: m for m, p in enumerate(need)}
        # Integer plane-to-row lookup shared by all components in this call.
        lut = np.full(int(need.max()) + 1, -1, dtype=np.int64)
        lut[need] = np.arange(len(need))
        rows_m = [lut[idx[:, m]] for m in range(idx.shape[1])]
        ar = np.arange(coords.shape[1])

        out = {}
        for name, coeff in (("stream", cX), ("wall_normal", cY),
                           ("span", cZ)):
            vals = np.empty((len(need), coords.shape[1]))
            for p in need:
                vals[col[p]] = map_coordinates(coeff[:, :, p], coords, order=3,
                                               mode="grid-wrap", prefilter=False)
            acc = np.zeros(coords.shape[1])
            for m in range(idx.shape[1]):
                acc += wts[:, m] * vals[rows_m[m], ar]
            out[name] = acc
        return Velocity(out["stream"], out["span"], out["wall_normal"])

    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- public
    def __call__(self, x_stream, z_span, y_wall, tq):
        """
        Velocity at arbitrary points, in the file's stored units.

        Arguments and return values use streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal order.

        x_stream : streamwise coordinate (h = 1), wrapped periodically over Lx
        z_span   : spanwise   coordinate (h = 1), wrapped periodically over Lz
        y_wall   : wall distance inside [0, slab depth]
        tq       : time, must lie inside the record

        Returns a Velocity(stream, span, wall_normal) namedtuple, so

            u, w, v = itp(x, z, y, t)          # positional, new order
            u = itp(x, z, y, t).stream         # or by name

        The wall-normal range check also detects callers that pass a
        wall-parallel coordinate in the ``y_wall`` position.
        """
        x_stream = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(x_stream, float))
        z_span = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(z_span, float))
        y_wall = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(y_wall, float))
        self._check_wall_normal(y_wall)
        shape = np.broadcast(x_stream, z_span, y_wall).shape
        x_stream, z_span, y_wall = np.broadcast_arrays(x_stream, z_span, y_wall)
        k0, k1, s = self._time_bracket(float(tq))

        if self.time_order == "linear" or self.Nt < 4:
            a = self._sample(k0, x_stream, z_span, y_wall)
            b = self._sample(k1, x_stream, z_span, y_wall)
            out = [(1 - s) * a[i] + s * b[i] for i in range(3)]
        elif k0 == 0 or k1 == self.Nt - 1:
            # At record ends, use a one-sided quadratic Lagrange form through
            # the three available slabs. Clamping the missing control point
            # would halve the one-sided end slope.
            if k0 == 0:
                ks = (k0, k1, min(k1 + 1, self.Nt - 1))
                w = (1 - 1.5 * s + 0.5 * s * s, 2 * s - s * s,
                     -0.5 * s + 0.5 * s * s)
            else:
                ks = (max(k0 - 1, 0), k0, k1)
                w = (0.5 * s * s - 0.5 * s, 1 - s * s,
                     0.5 * s + 0.5 * s * s)
            f = [self._sample(k, x_stream, z_span, y_wall) for k in ks]
            out = [w[0] * f[0][i] + w[1] * f[1][i] + w[2] * f[2][i]
                   for i in range(3)]
        else:
            km1, kp1 = k0 - 1, k1 + 1
            f = [self._sample(k, x_stream, z_span, y_wall)
                 for k in (km1, k0, k1, kp1)]
            s2, s3 = s * s, s * s * s
            out = []
            for i in range(3):
                fm1, f0, f1, f2 = f[0][i], f[1][i], f[2][i], f[3][i]
                out.append(0.5 * (2 * f0 + (-fm1 + f1) * s
                                  + (2 * fm1 - 5 * f0 + 4 * f1 - f2) * s2
                                  + (-fm1 + 3 * f0 - 3 * f1 + f2) * s3))
        return Velocity(*(o.reshape(shape) for o in out))

    # ------------------------------------------- velocity gradient / omega
    def _time_weights(self, tq):
        """
        (slab indices, weights) reproducing __call__'s time scheme: Catmull-
        Rom in the interior, one-sided quadratic Lagrange at the record ends,
        linear when time_order == "linear" or the record is too short.
        """
        k0, k1, s = self._time_bracket(float(tq))
        if self.time_order == "linear" or self.Nt < 4:
            return (k0, k1), (1.0 - s, s)
        if k0 == 0:
            return ((k0, k1, min(k1 + 1, self.Nt - 1)),
                    (1 - 1.5 * s + 0.5 * s * s, 2 * s - s * s,
                     -0.5 * s + 0.5 * s * s))
        if k1 == self.Nt - 1:
            return ((max(k0 - 1, 0), k0, k1),
                    (0.5 * s * s - 0.5 * s, 1 - s * s, 0.5 * s + 0.5 * s * s))
        s2, s3 = s * s, s * s * s
        return ((k0 - 1, k0, k1, k1 + 1),
                (0.5 * (-s + 2 * s2 - s3), 0.5 * (2 - 5 * s2 + 3 * s3),
                 0.5 * (s + 4 * s2 - 3 * s3), 0.5 * (-s2 + s3)))

    def velocity_gradient(self, x_stream, z_span, y_wall, tq,
                          with_velocity=False):
        """
        Velocity-gradient tensor ``G[a, b] = d u_a / d x_b`` at arbitrary
        points, with both indices ordered streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal.

        In-plane derivatives are analytic derivatives of the cubic B-spline
        sampled by ``__call__``. The wall-normal derivative uses the same local
        Lagrange stencil as the value, differentiated (lagrange_derivative_
        weights), and is converted from d/d(wall distance) to d/dy_channel
        with ``WALL_NORMAL_SIGN``. The same time weights are applied to the
        per-slab gradients, yielding the gradient of the interpolated field.

        with_velocity=True also returns the Velocity from the same series.
        """
        x_stream = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(x_stream, float))
        z_span = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(z_span, float))
        y_wall = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(y_wall, float))
        self._check_wall_normal(y_wall)
        x_stream, z_span, y_wall = np.broadcast_arrays(x_stream, z_span, y_wall)
        shape = x_stream.shape
        xs = x_stream.ravel(); zs = z_span.ravel(); yw = y_wall.ravel()
        npts = xs.shape[0]
        q0 = zs / self.d0                      # spanwise  -> array axis 0
        q1 = xs / self.d1                      # streamwise-> array axis 1
        idx, wts = lagrange_weights(self.y_nodes, yw, degree=self.wn_degree)
        _, dwts = lagrange_derivative_weights(self.y_nodes, yw,
                                              degree=self.wn_degree)
        dwts = dwts * float(WALL_NORMAL_SIGN)  # d/d(distance) -> d/dy_channel
        need = np.unique(idx)
        col = {p: m for m, p in enumerate(need)}
        ar = np.arange(npts)
        # Plane-to-row lookup shared by every slab and component in this call.
        lut = np.full(int(need.max()) + 1, -1, dtype=np.int64)
        lut[need] = np.arange(len(need))
        rows_m = [lut[idx[:, m]] for m in range(idx.shape[1])]

        G = np.zeros((npts, 3, 3))
        U = np.zeros((npts, 3))
        ks, tw = self._time_weights(tq)
        for k, wk in zip(ks, tw):
            cX, cY, cZ = self._coeffs(k)       # stream, wall_normal, span
            # Output component order: streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal.
            for a, coeff in ((0, cX), (1, cZ), (2, cY)):
                vals = np.empty((len(need), npts))
                dq0 = np.empty((len(need), npts))
                dq1 = np.empty((len(need), npts))
                for p in need:
                    v, g0, g1 = _bspline2d_values(coeff[:, :, p], q0, q1)
                    vals[col[p]] = v
                    dq0[col[p]] = g0 / self.d0      # d/dz  (span)
                    dq1[col[p]] = g1 / self.d1      # d/dx  (stream)
                u = np.zeros(npts); dudx = np.zeros(npts)
                dudz = np.zeros(npts); dudy = np.zeros(npts)
                for m in range(idx.shape[1]):
                    rows = rows_m[m]
                    v_m = vals[rows, ar]
                    u += wts[:, m] * v_m
                    dudx += wts[:, m] * dq1[rows, ar]
                    dudz += wts[:, m] * dq0[rows, ar]
                    dudy += dwts[:, m] * v_m
                U[:, a] += wk * u
                G[:, a, 0] += wk * dudx          # d/d stream
                G[:, a, 1] += wk * dudz          # d/d span
                G[:, a, 2] += wk * dudy          # d/d wall-normal (channel y)
        G = G.reshape(shape + (3, 3))
        if with_velocity:
            U = U.reshape(shape + (3,))
            return G, Velocity(U[..., 0], U[..., 1], U[..., 2])
        return G

    def omega(self, x_stream, z_span, y_wall, tq):
        """
        Fluid angular velocity omega_f = 1/2 curl U_f at arbitrary points
        (notes_sep26 Eq. 2: the full 3-D curl, evaluated at the particle
        centre height), as Omega(stream, span, wall_normal).

        Right-handed frame (e_x streamwise, e_y wall-normal = +channel y,
        e_z spanwise), pipeline component order (x, z, y):
            omega_x = 1/2 (d u_z/d y - d u_y/d z)
            omega_z = 1/2 (d u_y/d x - d u_x/d y)
            omega_y = 1/2 (d u_x/d z - d u_z/d x)     <- the only component
                                                         a horizontal contact
                                                         impulse can change
        """
        G = self.velocity_gradient(x_stream, z_span, y_wall, tq)
        # G[a, b]: a, b in (0 stream x, 1 span z, 2 wall-normal y)
        om_x = 0.5 * (G[..., 1, 2] - G[..., 2, 1])
        om_z = 0.5 * (G[..., 2, 0] - G[..., 0, 2])
        om_y = 0.5 * (G[..., 0, 1] - G[..., 1, 0])
        return Omega(om_x, om_z, om_y)

    def _check_wall_normal(self, y_wall, tol=1e-9):
        """
        The wall-normal argument must lie inside the slab.

        Small roundoff-level overshoots are clamped downstream. Values far
        outside the slab are rejected because they usually indicate that a
        wall-parallel coordinate was passed as ``y_wall``.
        """
        lo, hi = self.y_nodes[0], self.y_nodes[-1]
        span = hi - lo
        bad = (y_wall < lo - 10 * span) | (y_wall > hi + 10 * span)
        if np.any(bad):
            v = float(y_wall[np.argmax(bad)])
            raise ValueError(
                f"wall-normal coordinate {v:.6g} is far outside the slab "
                f"[{lo:.4e}, {hi:.4e}].\n"
                "The argument order is (x_stream, z_span, y_wall, t). It "
                "USED TO BE (span, stream, wall-normal); a caller still "
                "written that way lands a wall-parallel coordinate here. "
                "Use .at(x=..., z=..., y=..., t=...) if you want to be "
                "immune to the order.")

    def at(self, *, x=None, y=None, z=None, t=None):
        """
        Keyword-only velocity lookup using the dataset coordinate names.

            x : streamwise                      (Velocity_X direction)
            y : wall distance (Velocity_Y direction)
            z : spanwise                        (Velocity_Z direction)

        Returns Velocity(stream, span, wall_normal), the same type __call__
        returns.

        For a particle of radius ``a`` resting on the wall, sample at ``y=a``:

            f = itp.at(x=pos_stream, y=a, z=pos_span, t=t)
            u_stream, w_span = f.stream, f.span
        """
        if any(v is None for v in (x, y, z, t)):
            raise TypeError("at() requires all of x=, y=, z=, t=")
        return self(x, z, y, t)

    def gradient(self, x_stream, z_span, y_wall, tq, eps=None):
        """
        Velocity-gradient tensor A[..., i, j] = du_i/dx_j, stored units per h.

        Both indices use pipeline order: 0 streamwise, 1 spanwise,
        2 wall-normal. So A[..., 0, 2] is du_stream/dy, the mean shear.

        Differentiating the interpolant is less accurate than differentiating
        on the grid. For strain, vorticity, or
        dissipation, compute the gradient spectrally on the grid
        (structures.velocity_gradient) and interpolate the result.
        """
        eps = 1e-2 * self.d1 if eps is None else eps
        z_span = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(z_span, float))
        x_stream = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(x_stream, float))
        y_wall = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(y_wall, float))
        shape = np.broadcast(z_span, x_stream, y_wall).shape
        z_span, x_stream, y_wall = np.broadcast_arrays(z_span, x_stream, y_wall)
        A = np.empty(shape + (3, 3))

        px = self(x_stream + eps, z_span, y_wall, tq)
        mx = self(x_stream - eps, z_span, y_wall, tq)
        pz = self(x_stream, z_span + eps, y_wall, tq)
        mz = self(x_stream, z_span - eps, y_wall, tq)
        for i in range(3):
            A[..., i, 0] = (px[i] - mx[i]) / (2 * eps)     # d/d(streamwise)
            A[..., i, 1] = (pz[i] - mz[i]) / (2 * eps)     # d/d(spanwise)

        # wall-normal term: differentiate the Lagrange stencil analytically
        zc = np.clip(y_wall.ravel(), self.y_nodes[0], self.y_nodes[-1])
        idx, dwts = lagrange_derivative_weights(self.y_nodes, zc,
                                                degree=self.wn_degree)
        k0, k1, s = self._time_bracket(float(tq))
        coords = np.vstack([z_span.ravel() / self.d0,
                            x_stream.ravel() / self.d1])
        need = np.unique(idx)
        col = {p: m for m, p in enumerate(need)}
        n = coords.shape[1]
        for i, comp in enumerate((0, 2, 1)):   # stream, span, wall-normal
            vals = []
            for k in (k0, k1):
                coeff = self._coeffs(k)[comp]
                pl = np.empty((len(need), n))
                for p in need:
                    pl[col[p]] = map_coordinates(coeff[:, :, p], coords, order=3,
                                                 mode="grid-wrap",
                                                 prefilter=False)
                acc = np.zeros(n)
                for m in range(idx.shape[1]):
                    acc += dwts[:, m] * pl[[col[p] for p in idx[:, m]],
                                           np.arange(n)]
                vals.append(acc)
            A[..., i, 2] = WALL_NORMAL_SIGN * (
                (1 - s) * vals[0] + s * vals[1]).reshape(shape)
        return A


def lagrange_derivative_weights(y_nodes, y_wall, degree=2):
    """
    d/d(distance) counterpart of dataset.lagrange_weights.

    Same stencil selection, weights differentiated analytically:
        dL_a/dz = L_a(z) * sum_{b != a} 1/(z - y_b)
    is unstable at nodes, so the product rule is expanded directly instead.
    """
    y = np.asarray(y_nodes, float)
    y_wall = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(y_wall, float))
    idx, _ = lagrange_weights(y, y_wall, degree=degree)
    yy = y[idx]                                     # (nq, m)
    m = idx.shape[1]
    zc = np.clip(y_wall, y[0], y[-1])[:, None]
    dw = np.zeros_like(yy)
    for a in range(m):
        tot = np.zeros(len(y_wall))
        for c in range(m):
            if c == a:
                continue
            term = np.ones(len(y_wall))
            for b in range(m):
                if b == a:
                    continue
                term = term * (np.ones(len(y_wall)) if b == c
                               else (zc[:, 0] - yy[:, b]) / (yy[:, a] - yy[:, b]))
            tot += term / (yy[:, a] - yy[:, c])
        dw[:, a] = tot
    return idx, dw


def _clone_spec(spec, path):
    from dataclasses import replace
    return replace(spec, path=path)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    describe()
    itp = SlabInterpolator()
    k = min(200, itp.Nt - 1)
    tq = float(itp.t[k])
    u, _, _ = itp.spec.load_snapshot(k)
    ii = np.arange(0, itp.Nx, max(1, itp.Nx // 8))
    jj = np.arange(0, itp.Ny, max(1, itp.Ny // 8))
    II, JJ = np.meshgrid(ii, jj, indexing="ij")
    z_span   = II.ravel() * HZ
    x_stream = JJ.ravel() * HX
    p = 1
    ux = itp.at(x=x_stream, z=z_span,
                y=np.full(z_span.size, itp.y_nodes[p]), t=tq).stream
    print(f"\nnode recovery  max|err| = "
          f"{np.abs(ux - u[II.ravel(), JJ.ravel(), p]).max():.3e}")
    f = itp.at(x=x_stream[:50], z=z_span[:50],
               y=np.zeros(50), t=tq)
    a, b, c = f.stream, f.span, f.wall_normal
    print(f"no-slip at z=0 max|u|   = "
          f"{max(np.abs(a).max(), np.abs(b).max(), np.abs(c).max()):.2e}")
dns_fluid.pyConnects interpolated DNS fields to the particle engineSee code ↗

Small conversion helpers keep particle and wall-unit reporting consistent.
