def _check_versions():
    """
    Check that the companion modules expose the interfaces used here.
    """
    import inspect
    from run_dns_study import run_one as _r1
    missing = []
    sig = inspect.signature(_r1).parameters
    for need in ("save_snap", "n_snap", "progress", "inline",
                 "collisions_enabled", "record_target", "progress_every",
                 "stream_dir"):
        if need not in sig:
            missing.append(f"run_dns_study.run_one() has no '{need}'")
    for mod, fn in (("snapshots", "save_frames"), ("progress", "monitor"),
                    ("collective", "poisson_null_angular"),
                    ("figures", "fig_angular")):
        try:
            m = __import__(mod)
            if not hasattr(m, fn):
                missing.append(f"{mod}.py has no {fn}()")
        except ImportError:
            missing.append(f"{mod}.py is missing entirely")
    if missing:
        raise SystemExit(
            "\n".join(["", "OUT-OF-DATE FILES:"] + ["   " + m for m in missing]
                      + ["", "Re-download the modules listed above; they must all",
                         "come from the same set.", ""]))


_check_versions()


def _asnap(v):
    """'all' / 'none' / '' -> None (no clamp); otherwise an int."""
    if v is None:
        return None
    if isinstance(v, str):
        if v.strip().lower() in ("all", "none", ""):
            return None
        v = int(v)
    v = int(v)
    # Negative values use the same "no clamp" convention as run_study. Convert
    # them here before the downstream frame-count clamp.
    return None if v < 0 else v


# Particle cap for pair-based collective and angular figures.
#
# collective.velocity_correlation builds every pair inside r_max, so the pair
# count goes as N^2: at N = 30000 that is 5.9e7 pairs and roughly 5 GB of
# float64 temporaries. The correlation functions are ratios, so a random
# subsample is unbiased; it adds bin-level noise that falls as
# 1/sqrt(pairs) with 4.2e6 pairs still in the average at the default.
#
#     N       pairs     one (M,2) float64    ~6 temporaries
#   30000   5.9e7            899 MiB              5.3 GB
#   12000   9.4e6            144 MiB              0.8 GB
#    8000   4.2e6             64 MiB              0.4 GB
#
# Raise it if you have the memory and want smoother curves; the saved .npz is
# untouched either way, so the full field is always available for re-analysis.
# 0 means no cap. collective.py accumulates pair statistics in blocks, so the
# subsample is mainly a speed tradeoff. Set a positive value only when needed;
# it reduces the pair count, and g(sigma) needs at least 20 pairs
# at contact and subsampling to 8000 from 30000 removes ~93% of them.
CORR_MAX_N = 0


def _subsample_for_pairs(*arrays, n_max=None, seed=12345):
    """
    Take the same random subset from each array.

    One index set for positions and velocities together -- drawing them
    separately would pair each particle's position with another's velocity and
    destroy the correlation the figure is measuring.
    """
    n_max = CORR_MAX_N if n_max is None else int(n_max)
    first = next(x for x in arrays if x is not None)
    N = len(first)
    if n_max <= 0 or N <= n_max:          # 0 = keep everything
        return arrays, N, False
    idx = np.random.default_rng(seed).choice(N, n_max, replace=False)
    idx.sort()
    return tuple(None if x is None else np.asarray(x)[idx]
                 for x in arrays), n_max, True


# Frames averaged for the collective and angular summary. Use 1 for the final
# frame only.
COLLECTIVE_FRAMES = 12

# Keys that are retained rather than averaged across frames.
#
# Most values returned by analyse_collective can be averaged because
# the denominators are frame-independent: g_contact is npair/expected with
# expected fixed by (N, sigma, A), so <g> = <npair>/expected; and
# kernel_ratio_pred_meas divides by n_measured/T, which is already a whole-run
# constant. Those are means of a ratio with a constant denominator, which is
# the ratio of the means.
#
# These are the exceptions:
_NO_AVG = {
    # A power-law exponent fitted per frame. Averaging fitted slopes is not
    # the slope of the pooled data, and a poor per-frame fit biases it. Report
    # the final-frame value and its spread instead of a mean.
    "caustic_tail",
    # Booleans and constants -- carried through, not averaged.
    "g_reliable", "N", "sigma", "n", "strain_used",
    "rate_total_measured",
}


def _avg_dicts(dicts):
    """
    Mean and standard deviation over a list of per-frame result dicts.

    Non-finite entries are dropped per key rather than poisoning the mean, and
    the count that actually contributed is reported as <key>_nframes so a key
    that was only measurable in two of twelve frames cannot masquerade as a
    twelve-frame average.
    """
    out = {}
    keys = set().union(*(d.keys() for d in dicts)) if dicts else set()
    for k in sorted(keys):
        vals = [d[k] for d in dicts if k in d]
        if k in _NO_AVG or not all(isinstance(x, (int, float, np.floating,
                                                  np.integer))
                                   and not isinstance(x, bool) for x in vals):
            out[k] = vals[-1]                       # final frame, unaveraged
            continue
        arr = np.asarray([float(x) for x in vals], float)
        good = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
        if good.size == 0:
            out[k] = np.nan
            continue
        out[k] = float(good.mean())
        out[k + "_sd"] = float(good.std(ddof=1)) if good.size > 1 else 0.0
        out[k + "_nframes"] = int(good.size)
    return out


def _frames_to_average(res, n_avg, skip=0.0):
    """
    Indices of the last n_avg recorded frames at or after `skip`.

    Use the end of the run so the early transient does not bias the summary
    toward the initial condition.
    """
    t = np.asarray(res["event_times"], float)
    keep = np.where(t >= skip)[0]
    if keep.size == 0:
        keep = np.arange(len(t))
    return keep[-max(1, int(n_avg)):]


# One timestamp per module launch, shared by both legs and the tracer. This
# groups one study run while keeping later runs in the same session distinct.
import time as _time
_RUN_STAMP = _time.strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")


def run_case(tau=10, N=N_DEFAULT, a=A_DEFAULT, T=T_MAX, dt=0.02,
             model_study=False, sample_a=None,
             outdir=None, n_snap=50, seed=0, e_ball=1.0, inline=False,
             crop=None, vel_init="fluid", tracer_snap=None,
             collisions_enabled=True, record_target=None,
             run_tracer=True, progress_every=40, stream=False,
             rotation=False, tan_res=False, p_friction=False,
             e_tan=0.0, mu_pp=0.3, spin_init="fluid",
             wall_friction=False, mu_w=0.0, g_eff=0.0, pz_threshold=0.0):
    """
    rotation, tan_res, p_friction, wall_friction, e_tan, mu_pp, mu_w, g_eff,
    spin_init, pz_threshold : contact-model hierarchy from notes_sep26
               (B = rotation, C adds tan_res, D adds p_friction, and E adds
               wall_friction). These are forwarded to run_one for the case;
               the point tracer always uses model A.

    tracer_snap : path to an existing snap_tau0.npz. If given, reuse that file
               instead of running the tracer again.

               The tracer depends on the flow and the configuration, but not on
               tau_p, so one tracer serves every case that shares N, radius,
               dt, T, crop, seed and vel_init. Those are checked against the
               file's metadata before reuse.

    crop     : restrict to a sub-block of the wall-parallel grid, e.g. 64.
               Quarters the area and roughly halves the N needed, at the cost
               of an artificial periodic seam (see run_dns_study.configure).
    vel_init : "fluid" starts particles on the local fluid velocity, so there
               is no initialisation transient and the whole record is usable.
               "random" is the alternative, but since no frames are dropped it
               puts the first 5*tau_p of relaxation into the statistics.
    """
    global L, W
    if crop is not None:
        rds.configure(crop)
    L, W = rds.L, rds.W                 # follow the driver, cropped or not
    outdir = outdir or (f"case_tau{tau:g}"
                        + ("" if crop is None else
                           "_crop%dx%d" % (crop[0], crop[1])))
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
    if T > T_MAX:
        raise ValueError(f"T={T} exceeds the record ({T_MAX})")
    # Check the particle-count window before starting the engine runs; it can
    # be more restrictive than the geometric limit a <= A_MAX.
    area = L * W
    w = particle_window(a, area)
    if not w["feasible"] and not model_study:
        amax = max_feasible_radius(area)
        raise ValueError(
            f"no valid N at a={a:.4e} in this domain (area {area:.3f}): the "
            f"floor {w['N_min']:.0f} exceeds the one-way ceiling "
            f"{w['N_max_oneway']:.0f}. Largest radius admitting any N here: "
            f"{amax:.4e}. The limit scales as sqrt(area), so UNCROPPING helps "
            f"more than shrinking the radius.\n"
            f"If you are deliberately running dense -- a COLLISION-DOMINATED "
            f"regime cannot\nalso be one-way coupled -- pass "
            f"model_study=True and state phi in the write-up.")
    if not w["feasible"] and model_study:
        print(f"  MODEL STUDY: phi = {N * np.pi * a**2 / area:.4f} is "
              f"{N * np.pi * a**2 / area / 1e-3:.0f}x the one-way-coupling "
              f"limit.\n               Two-way momentum feedback is NOT "
              f"modelled. This is a deliberate\n               choice and "
              f"must be stated as one.")
    phi = N * np.pi * a ** 2 / area
    if phi >= 0.5:
        raise ValueError(
            f"phi = {phi:.3g} at N={N}, a={a:.4e}: the disks cannot be placed "
            f"without overlapping. Not a dilute suspension.")
    if T / max(tau, 1e-12) < 10:
        print(f"  WARNING: T/tau_p = {T/max(tau,1e-12):.1f}. Below ~10 "
              f"response times the run\n           cannot reach a steady "
              f"state; 5*tau_p = {5*tau:.1f} is "
              f"{500*tau/T:.0f}% of T. Record allows T = {T_MAX:.1f}.")

    print("=" * 70)
    report_setup(N, a, tau, T, L=L, W=W)
    print(f"  rho_p/rho_f   : {density_ratio(tau, a):.0f}")
    print(f"  window        : full record, nothing discarded"
          f"   (5*tau_p = {transient_time(tau):.2f}"
          f" = {100*transient_time(tau)/T:.0f}% of T, reported only)")
    print(f"  collisions    : "
          + ("ON" if collisions_enabled else
             "OFF -- COLLISIONLESS leg (radius kept at %.4e for seeding, "
             "phi and drag height; contact disabled)" % a))
    _rot, _tr = bool(rotation), bool(rotation and tan_res)
    _pf = bool(_tr and p_friction)
    _wf = bool(_pf and wall_friction)
    model = ("E" if _wf else "D" if _pf else "C" if _tr else "B" if _rot
             else "A")
    print(f"  model         : {model}"
          + {"A": " (smooth, no rotation)",
             "B": " (passive rotation, smooth contact; spins from %s)"
                  % spin_init,
             "C": " (tangential restitution e_tan = %g; spins from %s)"
                  % (float(e_tan), spin_init),
             "D": " (e_tan = %g, Coulomb cap mu_pp = %g; spins from %s)"
                  % (float(e_tan), float(mu_pp), spin_init),
             "E": " (e_tan = %g, mu_pp = %g, wall friction mu_w = %g, "
                  "g_eff = %g; spins from %s)"
                  % (float(e_tan), float(mu_pp), float(mu_w), float(g_eff),
                     spin_init)}[model])
    print(f"  output        : {outdir}/")
    print("=" * 70)

    t0 = time.time()
    results, diags = {}, {}

    reuse = None
    if tracer_snap:
        reuse = load_snapshots(tracer_snap)
        # A tracer is a point particle: archived radius 0, drag height
        # sample_a, never a collision (collisions_on is always 0 for it, so
        # it cannot tell tracers apart); the physical identity is sample_a
        want = dict(N=N, radius=0.0, dt=dt, T=T, e_ball=float(e_ball),
                    sample_a=(float(sample_a) if sample_a is not None
                              else float(a)), seed=int(seed))
        got = dict(reuse.get("meta", {}))
        got.setdefault("e_ball", 1.0)     # files predating this key were e=1
        # Include the recorded contact setting in compatibility checks. Files
        # without this metadata are treated as collisional.
        got.setdefault("collisions_on", 1.0)
        bad = [k for k, v in want.items()
               if k in got and not np.isclose(float(got[k]), float(v), rtol=1e-9)]
        if float(got.get("tau", 0.0)) != 0.0:
            bad.append("tau (not a tracer)")
        if bad:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{tracer_snap} does not match this configuration: {bad}. "
                f"file has {dict(got)}, this run wants {want}. "
                "A tracer is only reusable when N, radius, dt, T, crop, seed "
                "and vel_init all match.")
        print(f"  reusing tracer from {tracer_snap} "
              f"({reuse['positions'].shape[0]} frames, "
              f"{reuse.get('collisions', '?')} collisions) — skipping that run")
        import shutil
        dst = os.path.join(outdir, "snap_tau0.npz")
        if os.path.abspath(tracer_snap) != os.path.abspath(dst):
            shutil.copy(tracer_snap, dst)

    # Select which legs to run.
    #   run_tracer=False   this case only, no tau_p=0 baseline
    #   tracer_snap given  reuse the saved baseline, do not re-run it
    #
    # A tracer is a point particle: tau_p = 0 forces a = 0 through
    # tau_p = (2/9)(rho_p/rho_f) a^2 / nu, so it has no cross-section and
    # cannot collide. It is therefore the same object in the collisional and
    # collisionless legs, which is why it is safe to make it optional -- but
    # also why a tracer carrying a finite radius would be a different object
    # and must not be reused across legs.
    if reuse is not None:
        _legs = (tau,)
    elif run_tracer:
        _legs = (0.0, tau)
    else:
        _legs = (tau,)
        print("  run_tracer=False: no tau_p=0 baseline in this run "
              "(excess-over-tracer diagnostics will be unavailable)")

    for tv in _legs:
        tag = f"tau{tv:g}"
        # tau_p = 0 -> a = 0, from the Stokes relation. A finite radius here
        # would give the baseline a cross-section the definition denies it.
        a_leg = 0.0 if float(tv) == 0.0 else a
        res = run_one(tv, a_leg, N, T, dt, seed=seed, e_ball=e_ball,
                      collisions_enabled=collisions_enabled,
                      # the tracer leg (tau_p = 0) is a point particle: no
                      # spin, no contact law, no wall friction -- model A
                      rotation=(bool(rotation) and float(tv) != 0.0),
                      tan_res=(bool(tan_res) and float(tv) != 0.0),
                      p_friction=(bool(p_friction) and float(tv) != 0.0),
                      wall_friction=(bool(wall_friction) and float(tv) != 0.0),
                      e_tan=float(e_tan), mu_pp=float(mu_pp),
                      spin_init=spin_init, mu_w=float(mu_w),
                      g_eff=float(g_eff), pz_threshold=float(pz_threshold),
                      vel_init=vel_init, progress=True, inline=inline,
                      save_snap=os.path.join(outdir, f"snap_{tag}.npz"),
                      n_snap=n_snap, sample_a=sample_a,
                      record_target=record_target,
                      progress_every=progress_every,
                      # Use a timestamped folder so an earlier Windows memmap
                      # still held by the session cannot block this run.
                      stream_dir=(os.path.join(
                          outdir, f"_stream_tau{tv:g}_{_RUN_STAMP}")
                          if stream else None))
        results[tv] = res
        diags[tv] = diagnose_tail(res, tv)
        diags[tv]["collisions"] = res.get("total_collisions", 0)

    if reuse is not None:
        # Rebuild the tracer entry from the saved frames.
        #
        # Reconstruct tracer velocities from the field because tau_p = 0 gives
        # v_p = u(x_p) at the saved positions.
        _f = dns_slab_fluid(radius=(sample_a if sample_a is not None else a),
                            crop=crop)
        _pos = [np.asarray(p, float) for p in reuse["positions"]]
        _t = [float(x) for x in reuse["times"]]
        _vel = [_f(p, tt) for p, tt in zip(_pos, _t)]
        results[0.0] = {"position_history": _pos,
                        "velocity_history": _vel,
                        "event_times": _t,
                        "total_collisions": int(reuse.get("total_collisions", 0))}
        diags[0.0] = diagnose_tail(results[0.0], 0.0)
        diags[0.0]["collisions"] = int(reuse.get("total_collisions", 0))

    null = poisson_null(N)
    # The angular null includes mean, spread, and percentiles from 200
    # realisations. It is cached by N, domain, and DBSCAN settings.
    _null_N = min(N, 20000)
    if _null_N != N:
        print(f"  NOTE: angular null computed at N={_null_N}, not N={N}. "
              f"The DBSCAN cluster null depends strongly on N -- density "
              f"decides whether random points percolate -- so the "
              f"cluster_nematic comparison below is NOT valid at this N.")
    null_ang = co.poisson_null_angular(_null_N, L, W, verbose=True)

    # ---------------------------------------------------------- summary
    # A run may omit the tracer. Baseline-dependent columns are then omitted
    # explicitly rather than reported as zero.
    has_base = 0.0 in diags
    base = diags[0.0] if has_base else None
    case = diags[tau]
    lines = []
    lines.append(f"CASE  tau_p = {tau:g}   N = {N}   a = {a:.5e}   "
                 f"T = {T}   dt = {dt}")
    # Record the domain so phi, mean free path, and the N bounds can be
    # reconstructed, including for cropped runs.
    _phi = N * np.pi * a ** 2 / (L * W)
    _mfp = (L * W) / (2 * N * a)
    lines.append(f"domain  Lx = {L:.4f}  Lz = {W:.4f}  area = {L*W:.3f}"
                 + ("   (full)" if crop is None else
                    "   (crop %dx%d, %.3f of full)"
                    % (crop[0], crop[1], (L*W) / (DOMAIN["L"]*DOMAIN["W"]))))
    lines.append(f"phi = {_phi:.4e} ({_phi/1e-3:.1f}x the one-way limit)   "
                 f"MFP = {_mfp:.4f} = {_mfp/W:.3f} Lz   "
                 f"r+ = {to_wall_units(a):.4f}   y+ of centre = "
                 f"{to_wall_units(a):.4f}")
    lines.append(f"rho_p/rho_f = {density_ratio(tau, a):.0f}   "
                 f"[label only: St+ = {tau/T_NU:.2f}]")
    lines.append("")
    _KEYS = ("vmr_8", "vmr_16", "vmr_24", "D2", "n_clusters",
             "noise_pct", "largest_pct")
    if has_base:
        lines.append(f"{'diagnostic':>22}{'tracer':>12}{'case':>12}"
                     f"{'null':>12}{'excess':>12}")
        lines.append("-" * 70)
        for k in _KEYS:
            if k in case:
                ex = case[k] - base[k]
                sd = np.hypot(case.get(k + "_sd", 0), base.get(k + "_sd", 0))
                flag = "  *" if abs(ex) > sd else ""
                lines.append(f"{k:>22}{base[k]:>12.4f}{case[k]:>12.4f}"
                             f"{null.get(k, np.nan):>12.4f}{ex:>+12.4f}{flag}")
        lines.append("")
        lines.append(f"{'collisions':>22}{base['collisions']:>12d}"
                     f"{case['collisions']:>12d}")
        lines.append("(* = excess exceeds the frame-to-frame spread)")
    else:
        # Without a tracer baseline, omit the excess column rather than
        # implying a value of zero.
        lines.append(f"{'diagnostic':>22}{'case':>12}{'null':>12}")
        lines.append("-" * 46)
        for k in _KEYS:
            if k in case:
                lines.append(f"{k:>22}{case[k]:>12.4f}"
                             f"{null.get(k, np.nan):>12.4f}")
        lines.append("")
        lines.append(f"{'collisions':>22}{case['collisions']:>12d}")
        lines.append("NO TRACER LEG (tracer='off'): the excess-over-tracer "
                     "column is absent, not zero. 'null' is the Poisson "
                     "reference and is unaffected.")

    # collective + angular, at the final frame
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("COLLECTIVE BEHAVIOUR (final frame)")
    field = dns_slab_fluid(radius=(sample_a if sample_a is not None else a))
    # Iterate only over legs that ran; tracer="off" leaves no 0.0 entry.
    for tv in ((0.0, tau) if has_base else (tau,)):
        # Average the last COLLECTIVE_FRAMES frames. g(sigma) and <|w_r|> are
        # pair statistics at contact,
        # where one frame holds only order 10^3 pairs -- the shot noise on a
        # single frame is larger than the differences between legs that these
        # numbers exist to resolve.
        idx = _frames_to_average(results[tv], COLLECTIVE_FRAMES)
        per_frame = []
        for i in idx:
            pf = np.asarray(results[tv]["position_history"][i])
            vf = np.asarray(results[tv]["velocity_history"][i])
            # Same N^2 pair blow-up as the figures; subsample identically.
            (pf, vf), _n, _ = _subsample_for_pairs(pf, vf)
            per_frame.append(
                co.analyse_collective(pf, vf, L, W, sigma=2 * a, field=field,
                                      t=float(results[tv]["event_times"][i]),
                                      nu=NU,
                                      n_measured=diags[tv]["collisions"], T=T))
        s = _avg_dicts(per_frame)
        _tspan = (float(results[tv]["event_times"][idx[0]]),
                  float(results[tv]["event_times"][idx[-1]]))
        lines.append(f"  tau_p = {tv:g}   "
                     f"mean of {len(idx)} frames, t = {_tspan[0]:.3g}"
                     f"..{_tspan[1]:.3g}"
                     + (f", subsampled to {_n} particles"
                        if _n < len(results[tv]["position_history"][-1])
                        else ""))
        for k in ("g_contact", "w_abs_contact", "kernel_rate_total",
                  "slip_mean", "Re_p_mean", "polarization",
                  "vel_corr_nearest"):
            if k in s and np.isfinite(s[k]):
                sd = s.get(k + "_sd", 0.0)
                lines.append(f"      {k:>24} {s[k]:>12.4e}"
                             f"  +/- {sd:>10.3e}")
        # The rate predicted from the averaged g and w against the rate the
        # engine actually counted. These are the same quantity by two routes;
        # a large gap means the clustering is strongly time-dependent and the
        # frame-averaged g(sigma) still is not representative.
        if np.isfinite(s.get("kernel_rate_total", np.nan)) and T:
            _meas = diags[tv]["collisions"] / float(T)
            lines.append(f"      {'rate: predicted':>24} "
                         f"{s['kernel_rate_total']:>12.4e}")
            lines.append(f"      {'rate: counted':>24} {_meas:>12.4e}"
                         f"   ratio {s['kernel_rate_total']/max(_meas,1e-30):.3f}")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("ANGULAR STRUCTURE (case vs null -- only the excess counts)")
    p = np.asarray(results[tau]["position_history"][-1])
    v = np.asarray(results[tau]["velocity_history"][-1])
    ang = co.analyse_angular(p, v, L, W)
    for k in sorted(ang):
        val = ang[k]
        # The *_Q2 entries are complex. They allow axial angles to be
        # pooled through the complex plane rather than averaged as degrees
        # -- +89 and -89 deg are two degrees apart and average to 0, the
        # ORTHOGONAL axis -- but they are not table rows. Skip anything
        # that is not a real scalar instead of trying to format it.
        if isinstance(val, complex) or not isinstance(
                val, (int, float, np.floating, np.integer)):
            continue
        val = float(val)
        nv = null_ang.get(k, np.nan)
        p95 = np.nan
        if isinstance(nv, dict):                 # the new distribution form
            p95, nv = nv.get("p95", np.nan), nv.get("mean", np.nan)
        try:
            nv = float(nv)
        except (TypeError, ValueError):
            nv = np.nan
        if not np.isfinite(nv):
            lines.append(f"      {k:>26} {val:>10.4f}"
                         f"  null {'--':>8s}  excess {'--':>8s}")
            continue
        # Compare with the upper null percentile as well as the mean; the
        # DBSCAN cluster null can be high in a 3:1 box at eps = 1.8 s.
        mark = ""
        if np.isfinite(p95):
            mark = ("  EXCEEDS null" if val > p95
                    else "  BELOW null -- not evidence")
        lines.append(f"      {k:>26} {val:>10.4f}"
                     f"  null {nv:>8.4f}"
                     f"  excess {val - nv:>+8.4f}{mark}")
    lines.append("")
    lines.append("      the *_axis_deg rows carry NO excess on purpose: an "
                 "axis angle is wrapped, so a difference of two")
    lines.append("      angles is not a difference (+89 and -89 deg are two "
                 "degrees apart). Pool them through *_Q2 instead.")
    lines.append("      vel_polar is dominated by the MEAN FLOW, not by "
                 "collective order; vel_polar_fluct is the informative one.")

    txt = "\n".join(lines)
    with open(os.path.join(outdir, "summary.txt"), "w") as f:
        f.write(txt + "\n")
    print("\n" + txt)

    # ---------------------------------------------------------- figures
    # Release temporary pair arrays before the memory-intensive figure block.
    import gc
    gc.collect()

    print("\nfigures:")
    tag = f"tau{tau:g}"
    s0 = (load_snapshots(os.path.join(outdir, "snap_tau0.npz"))
          if has_base else None)
    sc = load_snapshots(os.path.join(outdir, f"snap_{tag}.npz"))
    skip = 0.0        # full window: no frames dropped
    P = lambda n: os.path.join(outdir, n)

    p = np.asarray(results[tau]["position_history"][-1])
    v = np.asarray(results[tau]["velocity_history"][-1])
    # None when no baseline was run. fig_collective and fig_angular already
    # treat p_tracer=None as "no reference curve", so they degrade on their
    # own once these are None rather than needing a branch here.
    p0 = (np.asarray(results[0.0]["position_history"][-1])
          if has_base else None)
    v0 = (np.asarray(results[0.0]["velocity_history"][-1])
          if has_base else None)

    fg.fig_snapshot(p, L, W, P(f"snapshot_{tag}.png"),
                    title=f"$\\tau_p$ = {tau:g},  t = {T}")
    if has_base:
        fig_snapshot_compare([s0, sc], L, W, P("compare.png"),
                             labels=["tracer ($\\tau_p$=0)",
                                     f"$\\tau_p$ = {tau:g}"])
    else:
        print("  compare.png skipped: no tracer leg to compare against")
    fig_snapshot_series(sc, L, W, P(f"series_{tag}.png"), n_show=8,
                        skip_transient=skip,
                        title=f"$\\tau_p$ = {tau:g}")
    fg.fig_scale_resolved(p, L, W, P(f"scales_{tag}.png"), sigma=2 * a,
                          p_null=np.column_stack([
                              np.random.default_rng(1).uniform(0, L, N),
                              np.random.default_rng(2).uniform(0, W, N)]))
    fg.fig_convergence(results[tau], tau, L, W,
                       P(f"convergence_{tag}.png"))
    # Subsample only the pair-based figures when a cap is configured.
    (_pc, _vc, _p0c, _v0c), _nc, _cut = _subsample_for_pairs(p, v, p0, v0)
    if _cut:
        print(f"  collective/angular: using {_nc} of {len(p)} particles "
              f"(pair count goes as N^2; the full field is in the .npz). "
              f"Set CORR_MAX_N in run_one_case.py to change this.")
    fg.fig_collective(_pc, _vc, L, W, P(f"collective_{tag}.png"), sigma=2 * a,
                      p_tracer=_p0c, v_tracer=_v0c)
    fg.fig_angular(_pc, _vc, L, W, P(f"angular_{tag}.png"),
                   p_tracer=_p0c, v_tracer=_v0c)
    # Render the particle GIF last. PillowWriter retains rendered frames in
    # memory, so free pair arrays first and treat GIF failure as non-fatal.
    import gc as _gc
    del _pc, _vc, _p0c, _v0c
    _gc.collect()
    try:
        animate_snapshots(sc, L, W, P(f"particles_{tag}.gif"), n_frames=40,
                          fps=8, skip_transient=skip)
    except MemoryError:
        print(f"  [particles_{tag}.gif skipped: out of memory during GIF "
              f"encoding. The .npz and all figures are already saved; "
              f"regenerate the GIF later from the snapshot if wanted.]")
        _gc.collect()
    except Exception as _e:
        print(f"  [particles_{tag}.gif skipped: {type(_e).__name__}: {_e}]")

    print(f"\ndone in {(time.time()-t0)/60:.0f} min -> {outdir}/")
    return {"diagnostics": diags, "null": null, "results": results,
            "outdir": outdir}


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import argparse
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="one response time, end to end")
    ap.add_argument("--tau", type=float, default=10,
                    help="particle response time, file time units")
    ap.add_argument("--N", type=int, default=N_DEFAULT)

                    help=f"simulated time, max {T_MAX:.1f}")

    ap.add_argument("--outdir", default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--n-snap", default="50",
                    help="frames written to the .npz, or 'all' to write every "
                         "frame the engine kept (no clamp).")
    ap.add_argument("--record-target", default=None,
                    help="states the ENGINE keeps, or 'all' for every step. "
                         "Default: follow --n-snap, so nothing is computed "
                         "and then discarded.")
    ap.add_argument("--e-ball", type=float, default=1.0)
    ap.add_argument("--tracer", default=None,
                    help="reuse an existing snap_tau0.npz instead of re-running it")
    ap.add_argument("--crop", default=None,
                    help="sub-block as n_stream,n_span (e.g. 64,128). A single "
                         "number is no longer accepted: the two directions "
                         "have different point counts.")
    ap.add_argument("--vel-init", choices=("fluid", "random", "zero"),
                    default="fluid")
    ap.add_argument("--inline", action="store_true",
                    help="overwrite one progress line (terminal only)")
    a = ap.parse_args()
    _crop = None
    if a.crop:
        parts = [int(x) for x in str(a.crop).replace("x", ",").split(",")]
        if len(parts) != 2:
            raise SystemExit("--crop needs two numbers: n_stream,n_span")
        _crop = tuple(parts)
    run_case(tau=a.tau, N=a.N, crop=_crop, vel_init=a.vel_init,
             tracer_snap=a.tracer, T=a.T, dt=a.dt, outdir=a.outdir,
             n_snap=_asnap(a.n_snap), e_ball=a.e_ball, inline=a.inline,
             record_target=_asnap(a.record_target))

04

Diagnostics

Clustering, collective motion, turbulent structures and contact impulses.

clustering.pyPeriodic clustering measures and finite-sample referencesSee code ↗

The variance-to-mean ratio is paired with grid occupancy for context.

"""Particle clustering diagnostics for a periodic two-dimensional domain.

All spatial calculations respect periodic boundaries. The module provides
multiscale variance-to-mean ratios, radial distribution functions,
correlation dimension, periodic Voronoi statistics, and DBSCAN cluster
properties. Velocity correlation, polarization, and pair dispersion describe
collective motion, while conditional density relates concentration to local
flow quantities such as strain, vorticity, or Okubo-Weiss parameter.

``poisson_null`` evaluates the same diagnostics for uniform random points at
the same particle count, providing finite-sample baselines for interpretation.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np
from scipy.spatial import cKDTree, Voronoi
from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
from scipy.special import gamma as _gammafn
from sklearn.cluster import DBSCAN


# --------------------------------------------------------------- utilities
def _wrap(p, L, W):
    q = np.column_stack([np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W)])
    q[q[:, 0] >= L, 0] = 0.0
    q[q[:, 1] >= W, 1] = 0.0
    return q


def _tree(p, L, W):
    return cKDTree(_wrap(p, L, W), boxsize=[L, W])


def mean_spacing(N, L, W):
    """Mean interparticle spacing, used as the natural scale for ``eps``."""
    return np.sqrt(L * W / N)


# ------------------------------------------------------------ box counting
def vmr(p, L, W, nbins):
    """Variance-to-mean ratio and occupancy on an nbins x nbins grid."""
    H, _, _ = np.histogram2d(np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W),
                             bins=nbins, range=[[0, L], [0, W]])
    c = H.ravel()
    return float(c.var() / c.mean()), float(100.0 * np.mean(c > 0))


def vmr_multiscale(p, L, W, nbins_list=(4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48)):
    """
    Evaluate VMR as a function of bin size.
    Returns (bin_size, vmr, occupancy, particles_per_bin).
    """
    out = []
    N = len(p)
    for nb in nbins_list:
        v, o = vmr(p, L, W, nb)
        out.append((np.sqrt(L * W) / nb, v, o, N / nb ** 2))
    a = np.array(out)
    return {"bin_size": a[:, 0], "vmr": a[:, 1], "occupancy": a[:, 2],
            "per_bin": a[:, 3], "nbins": np.array(nbins_list)}


def vmr_noise_floor(nbins):
    """Counting-statistics uncertainty on VMR: sqrt(2/n_bins)."""
    return float(np.sqrt(2.0 / nbins ** 2))


# --------------------------------------------------- radial distribution
def radial_distribution(p, L, W, r_max=None, nbins=40, log=True):
    """
    g(r), normalised by the uniform expectation. g > 1 means particles are
    closer together than random at that separation.
    """
    N = len(p)
    A = L * W
    if r_max is None:
        r_max = 0.25 * min(L, W)
    r_min = 0.5 * mean_spacing(N, L, W) / 20.0
    edges = (np.logspace(np.log10(r_min), np.log10(r_max), nbins + 1) if log
             else np.linspace(0.0, r_max, nbins + 1))
    tree = _tree(p, L, W)
    cum = tree.count_neighbors(tree, edges, cumulative=True).astype(float)
    counts = np.diff(cum)                       # pairs per shell, double counted
    shell = np.pi * (edges[1:] ** 2 - edges[:-1] ** 2)
    expected = N * (N - 1) / A * shell
    g = np.divide(counts, expected, out=np.zeros_like(counts),
                  where=expected > 0)
    return {"r": 0.5 * (edges[1:] + edges[:-1]), "g": g,
            "edges": edges, "counts": counts}


def g_at_contact(p, L, W, sigma):
    """Evaluate ``g(r)`` at the collision diameter relevant to collision rate."""
    N = len(p)
    A = L * W
    tree = _tree(p, L, W)
    n = tree.count_neighbors(tree, sigma) - N       # ordered pairs within sigma
    expected = N * (N - 1) * np.pi * sigma ** 2 / A
    return float(n / expected) if expected > 0 else np.nan


# ------------------------------------------------ correlation dimension
def correlation_dimension(p, L, W, r_lo=None, r_hi=None, npts=24):
    """
    Estimate ``D2`` from ``C(r) ~ r^D2`` and report the fit quality. A low
    ``R^2`` indicates that the selected range is not well described by a
    power law.
    """
    N = len(p)
    tree = _tree(p, L, W)
    r_lo = 0.01 * min(L, W) if r_lo is None else r_lo
    r_hi = 0.20 * min(L, W) if r_hi is None else r_hi
    r = np.logspace(np.log10(r_lo), np.log10(r_hi), npts)
    C = tree.count_neighbors(tree, r, cumulative=True).astype(float) - N
    ok = C > 0
    if ok.sum() < 4:
        return {"D2": np.nan, "r": r, "C": C, "r2": np.nan}
    x, y = np.log(r[ok]), np.log(C[ok])
    slope, icpt = np.polyfit(x, y, 1)
    pred = slope * x + icpt
    ss_res = np.sum((y - pred) ** 2)
    ss_tot = np.sum((y - y.mean()) ** 2)
    return {"D2": float(slope), "r": r, "C": C,
            "r2": float(1 - ss_res / ss_tot) if ss_tot > 0 else np.nan,
            "fit_range": (r_lo, r_hi)}


# ------------------------------------------------------------- Voronoi
def voronoi_analysis(p, L, W, nbins=40):
    """
    Voronoi tessellation with periodic images (Monchaux et al. 2010).

    Returns normalised cell areas V/<V>, the PDF, and sigma/sigma_Poisson.
    For a 2D Poisson process the normalised area follows a Gamma distribution
    with shape 3.5, giving sigma_Poisson = 1/sqrt(3.5) = 0.5345. Values above
    that indicate clustering; the crossings of the measured PDF with the
    Poisson one define cluster/void thresholds parameter-free.
    """
    q = _wrap(p, L, W)
    N = len(q)
    # Tile 3 x 3 so central cells have their periodic neighbours.
    tiles = [q + np.array([i * L, j * W])
             for i in (-1, 0, 1) for j in (-1, 0, 1)]
    big = np.vstack(tiles)
    centre0 = 4 * N                                # index of the (0,0) tile
    vor = Voronoi(big)
    areas = np.full(N, np.nan)
    for k in range(N):
        reg = vor.regions[vor.point_region[centre0 + k]]
        if not reg or -1 in reg:
            continue
        v = vor.vertices[reg]
        x, y = v[:, 0], v[:, 1]
        areas[k] = 0.5 * abs(np.dot(x, np.roll(y, 1)) - np.dot(y, np.roll(x, 1)))
    good = np.isfinite(areas)
    Vn = areas[good] / np.nanmean(areas[good])

    sigma_poisson = 1.0 / np.sqrt(3.5)
    hist, edges = np.histogram(Vn, bins=nbins, range=(0, 4), density=True)
    centres = 0.5 * (edges[1:] + edges[:-1])
    shape = 3.5
    pois = (shape ** shape / _gammafn(shape)) * centres ** (shape - 1) \
        * np.exp(-shape * centres)
    return {"V": Vn, "pdf": hist, "bins": centres, "poisson_pdf": pois,
            "sigma": float(Vn.std()), "sigma_poisson": sigma_poisson,
            "sigma_ratio": float(Vn.std() / sigma_poisson),
            "n_valid": int(good.sum())}


# -------------------------------------------------------------- DBSCAN
def periodic_dbscan(p, L, W, eps, min_samples=10):
    P = _wrap(p, L, W)
    tree = cKDTree(P, boxsize=[L, W])
    pr = tree.sparse_distance_matrix(tree, max_distance=eps,
                                     output_type="coo_matrix")
    G = csr_matrix((pr.data, (pr.row, pr.col)), shape=(len(P),) * 2)
    try:
        from sklearn.neighbors import sort_graph_by_row_values
        G = sort_graph_by_row_values(G, warn_when_not_sorted=False)
    except ImportError:
        pass
    return DBSCAN(eps=eps, min_samples=min_samples,
                  metric="precomputed").fit(G).labels_


def dbscan_clusters(p, L, W, eps_factor=1.8, min_samples=10):
    """
    Cluster statistics with periodic minimum-image distances.

    Elongation is computed on minimum-image coordinates relative to each
    cluster's first member, so filaments crossing the box edge are handled.
    ``percolates`` flags a cluster spanning the domain; in that regime, the
    cluster count primarily describes connectivity.
    """
    N = len(p)
    eps = eps_factor * mean_spacing(N, L, W)
    lab = periodic_dbscan(p, L, W, eps, min_samples)
    ids, counts = np.unique(lab[lab >= 0], return_counts=True)
    order = np.argsort(counts)[::-1]
    ids, counts = ids[order], counts[order]

    elong, perc = [], []
    P = _wrap(p, L, W)
    for cid in ids:
        Q = P[lab == cid]
        d = Q - Q[0]
        d[:, 0] -= L * np.round(d[:, 0] / L)
        d[:, 1] -= W * np.round(d[:, 1] / W)
        span = d.max(0) - d.min(0)
        perc.append(bool(span[0] >= 0.99 * L or span[1] >= 0.99 * W))
        if len(Q) >= 10:
            ev = np.linalg.eigvalsh(np.cov(d.T))
            elong.append(float(np.sqrt(max(ev[1], 0) / max(ev[0], 1e-15))))
    return {"labels": lab, "eps": eps, "ids": ids, "sizes": counts,
            "n_clusters": int(len(ids)),
            "noise_pct": float(100.0 * np.mean(lab == -1)),
            "largest_pct": float(100.0 * counts[0] / N) if len(counts) else 0.0,
            "elongation": np.array(elong),
            "median_elongation": float(np.median(elong)) if elong else np.nan,
            "percolates": bool(perc[0]) if perc else False,
            "n_percolating": int(sum(perc))}


# ------------------------------------------------ collective behaviour
def velocity_correlation(p, v, L, W, r_max=None, nbins=20):
    """
    Longitudinal velocity correlation <v_i . v_j> / <|v|^2> vs separation.
    This distinguishes coherent motion from spatial proximity alone.
    """
    N = len(p)
    if r_max is None:
        r_max = 0.25 * min(L, W)
    tree = _tree(p, L, W)
    pairs = tree.query_pairs(r_max, output_type="ndarray")
    if len(pairs) == 0:
        return {"r": np.array([]), "corr": np.array([])}
    d = _wrap(p, L, W)[pairs[:, 0]] - _wrap(p, L, W)[pairs[:, 1]]
    d[:, 0] -= L * np.round(d[:, 0] / L)
    d[:, 1] -= W * np.round(d[:, 1] / W)
    r = np.hypot(d[:, 0], d[:, 1])
    dot = np.sum(v[pairs[:, 0]] * v[pairs[:, 1]], axis=1)
    norm = np.mean(np.sum(v * v, axis=1))
    edges = np.linspace(0, r_max, nbins + 1)
    idx = np.digitize(r, edges) - 1
    corr = np.array([dot[idx == k].mean() / norm if np.any(idx == k) else np.nan
                     for k in range(nbins)])
    return {"r": 0.5 * (edges[1:] + edges[:-1]), "corr": corr}


def polarization(v):
    """Return ``|<v/|v|>|``: 0 for disorder and 1 for perfect alignment."""
    n = np.linalg.norm(v, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    u = np.divide(v, n, out=np.zeros_like(v), where=n > 0)
    return float(np.linalg.norm(u.mean(axis=0)))


def pair_dispersion(p0, p1, L, W, r0_bins=(0.0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2)):
    """
    Mean square separation growth of pairs initially within each r0 band.
    Tests whether particles that start together stay together.
    """
    def sep(a, b):
        d = a - b
        d[:, 0] -= L * np.round(d[:, 0] / L)
        d[:, 1] -= W * np.round(d[:, 1] / W)
        return np.hypot(d[:, 0], d[:, 1])
    tree = _tree(p0, L, W)
    pr = tree.query_pairs(max(r0_bins), output_type="ndarray")
    if len(pr) == 0:
        return {}
    r_ini = sep(_wrap(p0, L, W)[pr[:, 0]], _wrap(p0, L, W)[pr[:, 1]])
    r_fin = sep(_wrap(p1, L, W)[pr[:, 0]], _wrap(p1, L, W)[pr[:, 1]])
    out = {}
    for lo, hi in zip(r0_bins[:-1], r0_bins[1:]):
        m = (r_ini >= lo) & (r_ini < hi)
        if m.sum() > 10:
            out[f"{lo:.3f}-{hi:.3f}"] = {
                "n_pairs": int(m.sum()),
                "r0_mean": float(r_ini[m].mean()),
                "r2_growth": float((r_fin[m] ** 2).mean()
                                   / (r_ini[m] ** 2).mean())}
    return out


# ------------------------------------------------------- conditioning
def conditional_density(p, L, W, field, nbins=12, quantiles=8):
    """
    Particle number density conditioned on a local flow quantity.

    field : callable(x, y) -> value at those points, e.g. strain magnitude,
            vorticity, or the Okubo-Weiss parameter.

    Dependence on strain or vorticity helps relate preferential concentration
    to the local flow structure.
    """
    gx = (np.arange(nbins) + 0.5) * L / nbins
    gy = (np.arange(nbins) + 0.5) * W / nbins
    X, Y = np.meshgrid(gx, gy, indexing="ij")
    F = np.asarray(field(X.ravel(), Y.ravel()), float)
    H, _, _ = np.histogram2d(np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W),
                             bins=nbins, range=[[0, L], [0, W]])
    n = H.ravel() / H.mean()
    qs = np.quantile(F, np.linspace(0, 1, quantiles + 1))
    cen, dens, err = [], [], []
    for lo, hi in zip(qs[:-1], qs[1:]):
        m = (F >= lo) & (F <= hi)
        if m.sum() > 2:
            cen.append(0.5 * (lo + hi))
            dens.append(float(n[m].mean()))
            err.append(float(n[m].std() / np.sqrt(m.sum())))
    return {"field": np.array(cen), "density": np.array(dens),
            "err": np.array(err)}


# ------------------------------------------------------------- controls
def analyse(p, L, W, sigma=None, v=None, eps_factor=1.8, min_samples=10,
            nbins_list=(8, 16, 24)):
    """Full diagnostic set for one configuration."""
    out = {"N": len(p)}
    for nb in nbins_list:
        out[f"vmr_{nb}"], out[f"occ_{nb}"] = vmr(p, L, W, nb)
    cd = correlation_dimension(p, L, W)
    out["D2"], out["D2_r2"] = cd["D2"], cd["r2"]
    db = dbscan_clusters(p, L, W, eps_factor, min_samples)
    out.update(n_clusters=db["n_clusters"], noise_pct=db["noise_pct"],
               largest_pct=db["largest_pct"],
               median_elongation=db["median_elongation"],
               percolates=db["percolates"])
    try:
        vo = voronoi_analysis(p, L, W)
        out["voronoi_sigma_ratio"] = vo["sigma_ratio"]
    except Exception:
        out["voronoi_sigma_ratio"] = np.nan
    if sigma is not None:
        out["g_contact"] = g_at_contact(p, L, W, sigma)
    if v is not None:
        out["polarization"] = polarization(v)
    return out


def poisson_null(N, L, W, reps=5, seed=0, **kw):
    """Evaluate diagnostics on uniform random points as a finite-sample baseline."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    acc = [analyse(np.column_stack([rng.uniform(0, L, N), rng.uniform(0, W, N)]),
                   L, W, **kw) for _ in range(reps)]
    out = {}
    for k in acc[0]:
        vals = [a[k] for a in acc]
        if isinstance(vals[0], (int, float, np.floating)):
            out[k] = float(np.nanmean(vals))
            out[k + "_sd"] = float(np.nanstd(vals))
        else:
            out[k] = vals[0]
    return out
collective.pyPair motion, collision transport and orientational statisticsSee code ↗

The contact-scale diagnostic separates mean relative speed from approaching motion.

"""Collective-motion diagnostics for the inertial-particle study.

The routines here complement the spatial clustering measures in
``clustering.py``.  They describe relative motion, collision transport,
particle--fluid slip, velocity coherence, pair dispersion and orientational
order.

For hard disks in two dimensions, the collision kernel is written as

    Gamma = n * sigma * g(sigma) * <|w_r|>_sigma,

where ``g(sigma)`` measures accumulation at contact and ``<|w_r|>`` measures
the relative transport into contact.  Keeping these terms separate helps
distinguish changes in spatial concentration from changes in approach speed.

The caustic statistic is intended for comparisons with the tracer run.  Its
absolute threshold-crossing fraction also responds to the broad strain-rate
distribution of a smooth flow, whereas the tracer-to-inertial difference and
the tail behaviour isolate the inertial contribution.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np
from scipy.spatial import cKDTree


# --------------------------------------------------------------- utilities
def _wrap(p, L, W):
    q = np.column_stack([np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W)])
    q[q[:, 0] >= L, 0] = 0.0
    q[q[:, 1] >= W, 1] = 0.0
    return q


def _pairs(p, L, W, r_max):
    """Periodic pairs within r_max, with separation vectors and distances."""
    q = _wrap(p, L, W)
    tree = cKDTree(q, boxsize=[L, W])
    ij = tree.query_pairs(r_max, output_type="ndarray")
    if len(ij) == 0:
        return ij, np.empty((0, 2)), np.empty(0)
    d = q[ij[:, 0]] - q[ij[:, 1]]
    d[:, 0] -= L * np.round(d[:, 0] / L)
    d[:, 1] -= W * np.round(d[:, 1] / W)
    r = np.hypot(d[:, 0], d[:, 1])
    return ij, d, r


# ------------------------------------------------ blocked pair iteration
#
# _pairs() materialises all pairs inside r_max at once. The count grows as
# N^2, which makes the large-r_max diagnostics memory intensive:
#
#     N = 30000, Lx x Lz = 12.57 x 8.38
#     velocity_correlation     r_max 2.094   5.9e7 pairs   899 MiB per (M,2)
#     relative_velocity_stats  r_max 1.676   3.8e7 pairs   575 MiB per (M,2)
#     caustic_indicator        r_max 0.018   4.2e3 pairs     0 MiB
#
# Several float64 temporaries can otherwise require multiple gigabytes.
# iter_pairs() visits the same pairs in blocks, reducing peak allocation
# without subsampling the data.
PAIR_BLOCK = 2_000_000          # pairs per block; ~30 MiB per (M,2) float64


def iter_pairs(p, L, W, r_max, block=None):
    """
    Yield (ij, d, r) for periodic pairs within r_max, a block at a time.

    The output follows the same ``i < j`` convention and minimum-image
    separation as :func:`_pairs`, but the full pair array is not held in
    memory.
    """
    block = PAIR_BLOCK if block is None else int(block)
    q = _wrap(p, L, W)
    tree = cKDTree(q, boxsize=[L, W])
    N = len(q)
    # Neighbours per particle, estimated from the mean density, sets how many
    # source particles fit in one block without overshooting it.
    dens = N / (L * W)
    per_pt = max(1.0, np.pi * r_max ** 2 * dens)
    step = max(1, int(block / per_pt))

    for lo in range(0, N, step):
        hi = min(lo + step, N)
        nb = tree.query_ball_point(q[lo:hi], r_max)
        ii = np.repeat(np.arange(lo, hi), [len(x) for x in nb])
        if ii.size == 0:
            continue
        jj = np.fromiter((j for x in nb for j in x), dtype=np.int64,
                         count=ii.size)
        keep = ii < jj                      # i<j once, and drops self-pairs
        ii, jj = ii[keep], jj[keep]
        if ii.size == 0:
            continue
        ij = np.column_stack([ii, jj])
        d = q[ii] - q[jj]
        d[:, 0] -= L * np.round(d[:, 0] / L)
        d[:, 1] -= W * np.round(d[:, 1] / W)
        r = np.hypot(d[:, 0], d[:, 1])
        yield ij, d, r


# ------------------------------------------------- relative velocity
def relative_velocity_stats(p, v, L, W, r_max=None, nbins=24, log=True):
    """
    Radial relative velocity w_r = (v_i - v_j) . r_hat as a function of pair
    separation.

    Returns per-bin means of w_r, |w_r|, and the approaching part w_r^- (the
    piece that actually causes collisions), plus the fraction approaching.

    For tracers in a smooth flow, <|w_r|> ~ S*r, i.e. it vanishes linearly as
    r -> 0. Inertial particles retain a finite relative velocity at contact,
    which is exactly what raises the collision rate.
    """
    N = len(p)
    if r_max is None:
        r_max = 0.2 * min(L, W)
    # Accumulate in blocks so the result does not require one full pair array.
    if log:
        r_lo = np.inf
        for _, _, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, r_max):
            _pos = r[r > 0]           # coincident pairs must not set the
            if len(_pos):             # log-edge floor to the 1e-12 clamp
                r_lo = min(r_lo, float(_pos.min()))
        if not np.isfinite(r_lo):
            return {"r": np.array([]), "w_abs": np.array([])}
        edges = np.logspace(np.log10(max(r_lo, 1e-12)), np.log10(r_max),
                            nbins + 1)
    else:
        edges = np.linspace(0, r_max, nbins + 1)

    s_w = np.zeros(nbins)          # sum w_r
    s_abs = np.zeros(nbins)        # sum |w_r|
    s_neg = np.zeros(nbins)        # sum w_r over approaching pairs only
    c_neg = np.zeros(nbins)        # count of approaching pairs
    cnt = np.zeros(nbins, dtype=np.int64)
    any_pair = False
    for ij, d, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, r_max):
        any_pair = True
        _ok = r > 0                  # coincident pairs (r = 0):
        if not _ok.all():             # no direction -> excluded
            ij, d, r = ij[_ok], d[_ok], r[_ok]
            if len(r) == 0:
                continue
        rhat = d / r[:, None]
        wr = np.sum((v[ij[:, 0]] - v[ij[:, 1]]) * rhat, axis=1)
        idx = np.digitize(r, edges) - 1
        ok = (idx >= 0) & (idx < nbins)
        i_ok, w_ok = idx[ok], wr[ok]
        s_w += np.bincount(i_ok, weights=w_ok, minlength=nbins)
        s_abs += np.bincount(i_ok, weights=np.abs(w_ok), minlength=nbins)
        neg = w_ok < 0
        if neg.any():
            s_neg += np.bincount(i_ok[neg], weights=w_ok[neg],
                                 minlength=nbins)
            c_neg += np.bincount(i_ok[neg], minlength=nbins)
        cnt += np.bincount(i_ok, minlength=nbins)

    if not any_pair:
        return {"r": np.array([]), "w_abs": np.array([])}

    out = {k: np.full(nbins, np.nan) for k in
           ("r", "w_mean", "w_abs", "w_minus", "frac_approach", "n_pairs")}
    for k in range(nbins):
        if cnt[k] < 5:
            continue
        out["r"][k] = 0.5 * (edges[k] + edges[k + 1])
        out["w_mean"][k] = s_w[k] / cnt[k]
        out["w_abs"][k] = s_abs[k] / cnt[k]
        out["w_minus"][k] = (-s_neg[k] / c_neg[k]) if c_neg[k] else 0.0
        out["frac_approach"][k] = c_neg[k] / cnt[k]
        out["n_pairs"][k] = cnt[k]
    out["edges"] = edges
    return out


def pair_statistics(p, v, L, W, r_max_rv=None, r_max_vc=None,
                    nbins_rv=24, nbins_vc=20, z_frac=0.05):
    """
    Compute relative velocity, log-binned velocity correlation and the
    stretching-rate sample in a shared pair traversal.

    The first pass finds the common lower log-bin edge.  The second pass uses
    the largest requested radius and accumulates each diagnostic only over its
    own range, avoiding repeated scans of the same pairs.

    Returns {"rv": <relative_velocity_stats dict>,
             "vc": <velocity_correlation dict>,
             "z":  log10(|w_r|/r) samples within z_frac * min(L, W)}.
    """
    if r_max_rv is None:
        r_max_rv = 0.2 * min(L, W)
    if r_max_vc is None:
        r_max_vc = 0.25 * min(L, W)
    z_rmax = z_frac * min(L, W)
    R = max(r_max_rv, r_max_vc, z_rmax)

    # pass 1: minimum separation (shared by both log-edge constructions)
    r_lo = np.inf
    for _, _, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, R):
        _pos = r[r > 0]               # exclude coincident pairs from the
        if len(_pos):                 # log-edge floor (see the loop guard)
            r_lo = min(r_lo, float(_pos.min()))
    if not np.isfinite(r_lo):
        empty = {"r": np.array([]), "w_abs": np.array([])}
        return {"rv": dict(empty), "vc": {"r": np.array([]),
                "corr": np.array([])}, "z": np.array([])}

    e_rv = np.logspace(np.log10(max(r_lo, 1e-12)), np.log10(r_max_rv),
                       nbins_rv + 1)
    e_vc = np.logspace(np.log10(max(r_lo, 1e-12)), np.log10(r_max_vc),
                       nbins_vc + 1)
    norm = float(np.mean(np.sum(v * v, axis=1)))

    s_w = np.zeros(nbins_rv); s_abs = np.zeros(nbins_rv)
    s_neg = np.zeros(nbins_rv); c_neg = np.zeros(nbins_rv)
    cnt_rv = np.zeros(nbins_rv, dtype=np.int64)
    s_dot = np.zeros(nbins_vc); s_lon = np.zeros(nbins_vc)
    cnt_vc = np.zeros(nbins_vc, dtype=np.int64)
    zs = []

    # pass 2: everything, one sweep
    for ij, d, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, R):
        _ok = r > 0                  # coincident pairs (r = 0):
        if not _ok.all():             # no direction -> excluded
            ij, d, r = ij[_ok], d[_ok], r[_ok]
            if len(r) == 0:
                continue
        rhat = d / r[:, None]
        vi, vj = v[ij[:, 0]], v[ij[:, 1]]
        dv = vi - vj
        wr = np.sum(dv * rhat, axis=1)

        m = r <= r_max_rv
        if m.any():
            idx = np.digitize(r[m], e_rv) - 1
            ok = (idx >= 0) & (idx < nbins_rv)
            i_ok, w_ok = idx[ok], wr[m][ok]
            s_w += np.bincount(i_ok, weights=w_ok, minlength=nbins_rv)
            s_abs += np.bincount(i_ok, weights=np.abs(w_ok),
                                 minlength=nbins_rv)
            neg = w_ok < 0
            if neg.any():
                s_neg += np.bincount(i_ok[neg], weights=w_ok[neg],
                                     minlength=nbins_rv)
                c_neg += np.bincount(i_ok[neg], minlength=nbins_rv)
            cnt_rv += np.bincount(i_ok, minlength=nbins_rv)

        m = r <= r_max_vc
        if m.any():
            dot = np.sum(vi[m] * vj[m], axis=1)
            li = np.sum(vi[m] * rhat[m], axis=1)
            lj = np.sum(vj[m] * rhat[m], axis=1)
            idx = np.digitize(r[m], e_vc) - 1
            ok = (idx >= 0) & (idx < nbins_vc)
            s_dot += np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=dot[ok],
                                 minlength=nbins_vc)
            s_lon += np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=(li * lj)[ok],
                                 minlength=nbins_vc)
            cnt_vc += np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=nbins_vc)

        m = r <= z_rmax
        if m.any():
            z = np.abs(wr[m]) / np.maximum(r[m], 1e-15)
            zs.append(np.log10(z[z > 0]))

    rv = {k: np.full(nbins_rv, np.nan) for k in
          ("r", "w_mean", "w_abs", "w_minus", "frac_approach", "n_pairs")}
    for k in range(nbins_rv):
        if cnt_rv[k] < 5:
            continue
        rv["r"][k] = 0.5 * (e_rv[k] + e_rv[k + 1])
        rv["w_mean"][k] = s_w[k] / cnt_rv[k]
        rv["w_abs"][k] = s_abs[k] / cnt_rv[k]
        rv["w_minus"][k] = (-s_neg[k] / c_neg[k]) if c_neg[k] else 0.0
        rv["frac_approach"][k] = c_neg[k] / cnt_rv[k]
        rv["n_pairs"][k] = cnt_rv[k]
    rv["edges"] = e_rv

    rc, c, cl = [], [], []
    for k in range(nbins_vc):
        if cnt_vc[k] < 5:
            continue
        rc.append(0.5 * (e_vc[k] + e_vc[k + 1]))
        c.append(s_dot[k] / cnt_vc[k] / norm)
        cl.append(s_lon[k] / cnt_vc[k] / (norm / 2))
    vc = ({"r": np.array(rc), "corr": np.array(c),
           "corr_longitudinal": np.array(cl), "n_pairs": int(cnt_vc.sum())}
          if rc else {"r": np.array([]), "corr": np.array([])})

    return {"rv": rv, "vc": vc,
            "z": (np.concatenate(zs) if zs else np.array([]))}


def w_at_contact(p, v, L, W, sigma, width=1.5):
    """
    Mean |w_r| and mean approaching w_r^- for pairs within `width`*sigma.
    This is the transport factor of the collision kernel.
    """
    ij, d, r = _pairs(p, L, W, width * sigma)
    _ok = r > 0                      # coincident pairs: no direction
    ij, d, r = ij[_ok], d[_ok], r[_ok]
    if len(ij) == 0:
        return {"w_abs": np.nan, "w_minus": np.nan,
                "frac_approach": np.nan, "n_pairs": 0}
    rhat = d / r[:, None]
    wr = np.sum((v[ij[:, 0]] - v[ij[:, 1]]) * rhat, axis=1)
    return {"w_abs": float(np.abs(wr).mean()),
            "w_minus": float(-wr[wr < 0].mean()) if np.any(wr < 0) else 0.0,
            "frac_approach": float(np.mean(wr < 0)),
            "n_pairs": int(len(wr))}


# --------------------------------------------------- collision kernel
def collision_kernel(p, v, L, W, sigma, n_measured=None, T=None):
    """
    Sundaram & Collins decomposition of the 2D collision rate:

        Gamma = n * sigma * g(sigma) * <|w_r|>

    Returns the two factors separately -- accumulation g(sigma) and transport
    <|w_r|> -- so a change in the collision rate can be attributed.

    If `n_measured` (total collisions) and `T` (duration) are given, the
    predicted and observed rates are compared. Agreement validates the
    picture; a large discrepancy usually means the clustering is strongly
    time-dependent, so the instantaneous g(sigma) is not representative.
    """
    N = len(p)
    A = L * W
    n = N / A
    q = _wrap(p, L, W)
    tree = cKDTree(q, boxsize=[L, W])
    npair = tree.count_neighbors(tree, sigma) - N          # ordered pairs
    expected = N * (N - 1) * np.pi * sigma ** 2 / A
    g = float(npair / expected) if expected > 0 else np.nan
    # g(sigma) needs pairs AT contact. With a sub-viscous particle that
    # requires large N: at N = 4000 the expected count is < 1 and g comes back
    # as 0, which means "not measurable here", not "no clustering".
    g_reliable = npair >= 20
    w = w_at_contact(p, v, L, W, sigma)

    Gamma = n * sigma * g * w["w_abs"]                      # per particle
    out = {"n": n, "sigma": sigma, "g_contact": g,
           "g_reliable": bool(g_reliable), "n_contact_pairs": int(npair),
           "w_abs": w["w_abs"], "w_minus": w["w_minus"],
           "rate_per_particle": float(Gamma),
           "rate_total": float(0.5 * N * Gamma),
           "g_x_w": float(g * w["w_abs"])}
    if n_measured is not None and T:
        out["rate_total_measured"] = float(n_measured / T)
        out["ratio_pred_over_meas"] = float(0.5 * N * Gamma /
                                            max(n_measured / T, 1e-30))
    return out


# ------------------------------------------------------------ caustics
def caustic_indicator(p, v, L, W, r_probe=None, strain=None, n_sigma=3.0):
    """
    Caustics (sling events) are folds of the particle velocity field: two
    particles nearly co-located with very different velocities.

    Smooth advection bounds the relative velocity by |w_r| <~ S*r, with S the
    local strain rate. This routine reports:
        frac_exceed : fraction of close pairs with |w_r| > n_sigma * S * r
        tail_exponent : power-law slope of the |w_r|/r distribution tail
                        (a heavy tail is the caustic signature)
        w_over_Sr    : median |w_r| / (S*r), which is O(1) without caustics

    `strain` may be given (rms strain rate of the carrier); if omitted it is
    estimated from the particles' own velocity differences at larger
    separation, where the motion is smooth.
    """
    if r_probe is None:
        r_probe = 3.0 * np.sqrt(L * W / len(p)) / 10.0
    ij, d, r = _pairs(p, L, W, r_probe)
    _ok = r > 0                      # coincident pairs: no direction
    ij, d, r = ij[_ok], d[_ok], r[_ok]
    if len(ij) < 20:
        return {"frac_exceed": np.nan, "tail_exponent": np.nan,
                "w_over_Sr": np.nan, "n_pairs": int(len(ij))}
    rhat = d / r[:, None]
    wr = np.abs(np.sum((v[ij[:, 0]] - v[ij[:, 1]]) * rhat, axis=1))

    if strain is None:                    # estimate from the smooth range
        ij2, d2, r2 = _pairs(p, L, W, 10.0 * r_probe)
        if len(ij2) > 50:
            far = r2 > 3.0 * r_probe
            if far.sum() > 20:
                rh2 = d2[far] / r2[far][:, None]
                w2 = np.abs(np.sum((v[ij2[far, 0]] - v[ij2[far, 1]]) * rh2,
                                   axis=1))
                strain = float(np.median(w2 / r2[far]))
            else:
                strain = float(np.median(wr / r))
        else:
            strain = float(np.median(wr / r))

    z = wr / np.maximum(r, 1e-15)          # stretching rate
    frac = float(np.mean(wr > n_sigma * strain * r))
    zs = np.sort(z[z > 0])
    tail = zs[int(0.9 * len(zs)):] if len(zs) > 50 else zs
    if len(tail) > 10:
        rank = np.arange(len(tail), 0, -1) / len(z)
        sl = np.polyfit(np.log(tail), np.log(rank), 1)[0]
    else:
        sl = np.nan
    return {"frac_exceed": frac, "tail_exponent": float(sl),
            "w_over_Sr": float(np.median(z) / strain) if strain > 0 else np.nan,
            "strain_used": float(strain), "n_pairs": int(len(ij))}


# ----------------------------------------------------------- slip
def slip_velocity_stats(p, v, field, t, nu=None, d=None):
    """
    Particle-fluid slip |v_p - u(x_p)|, the quantity that drives the drag and
    sets Re_p. Its growth with Stokes number is the most direct measure of
    decoupling from the flow.
    """
    u = field(p, t)
    s = v - u
    mag = np.hypot(s[:, 0], s[:, 1])
    out = {"slip_mean": float(mag.mean()), "slip_rms": float(mag.std()),
           "slip_max": float(mag.max()),
           "u_rms": float(np.hypot(u[:, 0], u[:, 1]).std()),
           "slip_over_u": float(mag.mean() /
                                max(np.hypot(u[:, 0], u[:, 1]).std(), 1e-30))}
    if nu and d:
        out["Re_p_mean"] = float(mag.mean() * d / nu)
        out["Re_p_max"] = float(mag.max() * d / nu)
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------- spatial coherence
def velocity_correlation(p, v, L, W, r_max=None, nbins=20, log=False):
    """
    Normalised velocity correlation <v_i . v_j> / <|v|^2> vs separation, split
    into longitudinal and transverse parts. Answers whether neighbours move
    together, which is independent of whether they are close together.
    """
    if r_max is None:
        r_max = 0.25 * min(L, W)
    norm = float(np.mean(np.sum(v * v, axis=1)))

    # Accumulate pair contributions in blocks to limit peak memory use.
    if log:
        # log edges need the global minimum separation, which is only known
        # after a pass. One extra sweep is far cheaper than the memory.
        r_lo = np.inf
        for _, _, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, r_max):
            _pos = r[r > 0]           # coincident pairs must not set the
            if len(_pos):             # log-edge floor to the 1e-12 clamp
                r_lo = min(r_lo, float(_pos.min()))
        if not np.isfinite(r_lo):
            return {"r": np.array([]), "corr": np.array([])}
        edges = np.logspace(np.log10(max(r_lo, 1e-12)), np.log10(r_max),
                            nbins + 1)
    else:
        edges = np.linspace(0, r_max, nbins + 1)

    s_dot = np.zeros(nbins)
    s_lon = np.zeros(nbins)
    cnt = np.zeros(nbins, dtype=np.int64)
    for ij, d, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, r_max):
        _ok = r > 0                  # coincident pairs (r = 0):
        if not _ok.all():             # no direction -> excluded
            ij, d, r = ij[_ok], d[_ok], r[_ok]
            if len(r) == 0:
                continue
        rhat = d / r[:, None]
        vi, vj = v[ij[:, 0]], v[ij[:, 1]]
        dot = np.sum(vi * vj, axis=1)
        li = np.sum(vi * rhat, axis=1)
        lj = np.sum(vj * rhat, axis=1)
        idx = np.digitize(r, edges) - 1
        ok = (idx >= 0) & (idx < nbins)
        s_dot += np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=dot[ok], minlength=nbins)
        s_lon += np.bincount(idx[ok], weights=(li * lj)[ok], minlength=nbins)
        cnt += np.bincount(idx[ok], minlength=nbins)

    rc, c, cl = [], [], []
    for k in range(nbins):
        if cnt[k] < 5:                      # same threshold as before
            continue
        rc.append(0.5 * (edges[k] + edges[k + 1]))
        c.append(s_dot[k] / cnt[k] / norm)
        cl.append(s_lon[k] / cnt[k] / (norm / 2))
    if not rc:
        return {"r": np.array([]), "corr": np.array([])}
    return {"r": np.array(rc), "corr": np.array(c),
            "corr_longitudinal": np.array(cl),
            "n_pairs": int(cnt.sum())}


def polarization(v):
    """|<v/|v|>| : 0 = disordered, 1 = all particles moving the same way."""
    n = np.linalg.norm(v, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    u = np.divide(v, n, out=np.zeros_like(v), where=n > 0)
    return float(np.linalg.norm(u.mean(axis=0)))


def alignment_with_strain(p, v, L, W, gradient, t):
    """
    Cosine between the particle velocity and the local compressive strain
    eigenvector of the carrier flow.

    gradient : callable(p, t) -> (N, 2, 2) in-plane velocity-gradient tensor.

    Particles converging onto strain-dominated regions tend to align with the
    compressive direction; this is a direct mechanism test rather than a
    statistical one.
    """
    A = np.asarray(gradient(p, t), float)[..., :2, :2]
    S = 0.5 * (A + np.swapaxes(A, -1, -2))
    w, V = np.linalg.eigh(S)                   # ascending: w[0] compressive
    e = V[..., :, 0]
    vn = v / np.maximum(np.linalg.norm(v, axis=1, keepdims=True), 1e-30)
    cos = np.abs(np.sum(vn * e, axis=1))
    return {"cos_mean": float(cos.mean()), "cos_median": float(np.median(cos)),
            "isotropic_value": 2.0 / np.pi}     # <|cos|> for random 2D angles


# ------------------------------------------------------- pair dispersion
def pair_dispersion(p0, p1, L, W, dt, r0_bins=(1e-3, 3e-3, 1e-2, 3e-2, 1e-1)):
    """
    Mean-square separation growth for pairs that started within each r0 band.

    Reports the growth ratio and an effective exponent from <r^2> ~ t^alpha:
    alpha ~ 2 is ballistic (particles remember their initial relative
    velocity), alpha ~ 1 is diffusive.
    """
    def sep(a, b):
        d = a - b
        d[:, 0] -= L * np.round(d[:, 0] / L)
        d[:, 1] -= W * np.round(d[:, 1] / W)
        return np.hypot(d[:, 0], d[:, 1])

    q0, q1 = _wrap(p0, L, W), _wrap(p1, L, W)
    tree = cKDTree(q0, boxsize=[L, W])
    ij = tree.query_pairs(max(r0_bins), output_type="ndarray")
    if len(ij) == 0:
        return {}
    r_i = sep(q0[ij[:, 0]], q0[ij[:, 1]])
    r_f = sep(q1[ij[:, 0]], q1[ij[:, 1]])
    out = {}
    for lo, hi in zip(r0_bins[:-1], r0_bins[1:]):
        m = (r_i >= lo) & (r_i < hi)
        if m.sum() < 10:
            continue
        g = float((r_f[m] ** 2).mean() / (r_i[m] ** 2).mean())
        out[f"{lo:.1e}-{hi:.1e}"] = {
            "n_pairs": int(m.sum()), "r0_mean": float(r_i[m].mean()),
            "r2_growth": g,
            "alpha": float(np.log(max(g, 1e-12)) / np.log(max(dt, 1e-12)))
            if dt > 1 else np.nan}
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------- clustering rate
def clustering_timescale(res, L, W, nbins=16, n_skip=0):
    """
    How fast clustering builds from the initial condition.

    Fits VMR(t) - VMR_inf = (VMR_0 - VMR_inf) exp(-t/tau_c) over the history,
    returning tau_c and the saturation level. If the record is shorter than a
    few tau_c the run has NOT converged, and this is the number that says so.
    """
    times = np.asarray(res["event_times"], float)
    hist = res["position_history"]
    sel = np.arange(n_skip, len(times))
    if len(sel) < 8:
        return {}
    idx = sel[np.linspace(0, len(sel) - 1, min(50, len(sel))).astype(int)]
    tv, vv = [], []
    for i in idx:
        p = np.asarray(hist[i])
        H, _, _ = np.histogram2d(np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W),
                                 bins=nbins, range=[[0, L], [0, W]])
        c = H.ravel()
        tv.append(times[i]); vv.append(c.var() / c.mean())
    tv, vv = np.array(tv), np.array(vv)
    v_inf = float(np.mean(vv[-max(3, len(vv) // 4):]))
    dvv = vv - v_inf
    ok = dvv > 0
    if ok.sum() < 5:
        return {"vmr_final": v_inf, "tau_c": np.nan, "t": tv, "vmr": vv}
    sl = np.polyfit(tv[ok] - tv[0], np.log(dvv[ok]), 1)[0]
    tau = -1.0 / sl if sl < 0 else np.nan
    T = tv[-1] - tv[0]
    return {"t": tv, "vmr": vv, "vmr_final": v_inf, "tau_c": float(tau),
            "record_over_tau": float(T / tau) if tau and tau > 0 else np.nan,
            "converged": bool(tau and tau > 0 and T / tau > 3)}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- summary
def analyse_collective(p, v, L, W, sigma=None, field=None, t=None,
                       nu=None, n_measured=None, T=None):
    """One-call summary of the collective-behaviour diagnostics."""
    out = {"N": len(p), "polarization": polarization(v)}
    if sigma:
        w = w_at_contact(p, v, L, W, sigma)
        out.update(w_abs_contact=w["w_abs"], w_minus_contact=w["w_minus"],
                   frac_approach=w["frac_approach"])
        ck = collision_kernel(p, v, L, W, sigma, n_measured, T)
        out.update(g_contact=ck["g_contact"],
                   kernel_rate_total=ck["rate_total"],
                   accumulation_x_transport=ck["g_x_w"])
        if "ratio_pred_over_meas" in ck:
            out["kernel_ratio_pred_meas"] = ck["ratio_pred_over_meas"]
    ca = caustic_indicator(p, v, L, W)
    out.update(caustic_fraction=ca["frac_exceed"],
               caustic_tail=ca["tail_exponent"])
    if field is not None and t is not None:
        sl = slip_velocity_stats(p, v, field, t, nu=nu,
                                 d=2 * sigma / 2 if sigma else None)
        out.update({k: sl[k] for k in sl})
    vc = velocity_correlation(p, v, L, W)
    if len(vc["r"]):
        out["vel_corr_nearest"] = float(vc["corr"][0])
    return out


# ==========================================================================
#  Angular structure
# ==========================================================================
"""
Near-wall structures are streamwise, so pairs should be preferentially
aligned with x, and clusters should be filaments pointing downstream. The
routines below measure that.

The natural 2D order parameters are the circular moments. For a set of angles
theta:
    polar order    P = |<exp(i theta)>|    directionality (0 = no direction)
    nematic order  S = |<exp(2i theta)>|   alignment along an axis
Nematic is the right one for pair separations and cluster axes, because a pair
at angle theta and one at theta+pi are the same configuration.

Three details matter when these quantities are combined:

1. Polar order is not meaningful for a folded axial angle. Once theta has been
   folded to [0, pi), a perfectly isotropic set has <sin theta> = 2/pi, so
   P = 0.6366 under isotropy. ``_circular_orders(..., axial=True)`` therefore
   omits the polar result.

2. S is nonzero under finite-sample isotropy. |<exp(2 i theta)>| is the
   length of an n-step random walk of unit vectors: Rayleigh, mean
   0.886/sqrt(n). Each result includes the corresponding ``S_null95`` floor.

3. Axial angles should not be averaged arithmetically. +89 deg and -89 deg are
   degrees apart, not 178, and their arithmetic mean (0 deg) is orthogonal to
   both. Directions are combined through Q2 = <exp(2 i theta)> by
   ``combine_Q2()``.
"""

_LN20 = 2.9957322735539909          # -ln(0.05)


def nematic_noise_floor(n, level=0.95):
    """
    The value S = |<exp(2 i theta)>| reaches by chance on n independent
    isotropic angles.

    Q2 is a mean of n unit vectors, so its real and imaginary parts are each
    ~N(0, 1/(2n)) and |Q2| is Rayleigh with scale 1/sqrt(2n):

        <S>       = sqrt(pi)/(2 sqrt(n))   = 0.886 / sqrt(n)
        P(S > x)  = exp(-n x^2)
        S_0.95    = sqrt(-ln 0.05 / n)     = 1.731 / sqrt(n)

    A measured S below S_0.95 is not evidence of alignment.

    Pairs that share a particle are correlated, so the pair count can
    overstate the effective sample size.  For marginal cases,
    :func:`poisson_null_angular` provides an empirical reference.
    """
    n = float(n)
    if not np.isfinite(n) or n < 1:
        return np.nan
    if level is None:
        return float(np.sqrt(np.pi) / (2.0 * np.sqrt(n)))       # the mean
    return float(np.sqrt(-np.log(1.0 - level) / n))


def _circular_orders(theta, weights=None, axial=False):
    """
    Circular order parameters, with their preferred angles.

    axial=True  -- theta has been folded to [0, pi) and denotes an axis. Only
                   the second-order (nematic) quantities are returned; the
                   polar moment of a folded angle is not a physical quantity
                   (isotropy gives P = 2/pi, not 0).
    axial=False -- theta is a true direction on (-pi, pi]; both orders come
                   back.

    Non-finite angles, non-finite weights and non-positive weights are dropped;
    `n_dropped` records how many. With nothing left, every value is NaN rather
    than the 0.0 that an empty sum would otherwise produce and that reads as
    perfect isotropy.
    """
    th = np.asarray(theta, float).ravel()
    w = (np.ones_like(th) if weights is None
         else np.asarray(weights, float).ravel())
    if w.shape != th.shape:
        raise ValueError("weights and theta must have the same length")
    ok = np.isfinite(th) & np.isfinite(w) & (w > 0)
    n_drop = int(th.size - ok.sum())
    th, w = th[ok], w[ok]
    tot = float(w.sum()) if th.size else 0.0

    nan_c = complex(np.nan, np.nan)
    if th.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(tot) or tot <= 0:
        out = {"nematic": np.nan, "nematic_angle": np.nan, "Q2": nan_c,
               "n": 0, "n_eff": 0.0, "n_dropped": n_drop, "S_null95": np.nan}
        if not axial:
            out.update({"polar": np.nan, "polar_angle": np.nan, "Q1": nan_c})
        return out

    w = w / tot
    Q2 = complex(float(np.sum(w * np.cos(2 * th))),
                 float(np.sum(w * np.sin(2 * th))))
    n_eff = float(1.0 / np.sum(w ** 2))          # Kish effective sample size
    out = {"nematic": float(abs(Q2)),
           "nematic_angle": float(0.5 * np.arctan2(Q2.imag, Q2.real)),
           "Q2": Q2, "n": int(th.size), "n_eff": n_eff, "n_dropped": n_drop,
           "S_null95": nematic_noise_floor(n_eff)}
    if not axial:
        Q1 = complex(float(np.sum(w * np.cos(th))),
                     float(np.sum(w * np.sin(th))))
        out.update({"polar": float(abs(Q1)),
                    "polar_angle": float(np.arctan2(Q1.imag, Q1.real)),
                    "Q1": Q1})
    return out


def combine_Q2(dicts, weight_key="n_pairs", q_key="Q2"):
    """
    Combine per-frame nematic moments into a persistent estimate.

        Q2_bar = sum_t n_t Q2_t / sum_t n_t
        S_persistent = |Q2_bar|,  Theta_persistent = 0.5 arg Q2_bar

    Pooling the complex moment respects the wrapped axial angle.  A director
    that rotates between frames consequently lowers the pooled magnitude.

    S_persistent <= mean_t S_t, and the gap reflects rotation. Report
    both: the mean of |Q2_t| says how ordered a typical frame is, |mean Q2_t|
    says whether that order points the same way twice.
    """
    q, n = [], []
    for d in dicts:
        if d is None:
            continue
        z, m = d.get(q_key), d.get(weight_key, 1.0)
        if z is None or m is None:
            continue
        z = complex(z)
        m = float(m)
        if np.isfinite(z.real) and np.isfinite(z.imag) and m > 0:
            q.append(z)
            n.append(m)
    if not q:
        return {"Q2": complex(np.nan, np.nan), "S_persistent": np.nan,
                "angle_persistent": np.nan, "S_frame_mean": np.nan,
                "n_frames": 0, "n_total": 0.0}
    q = np.asarray(q)
    n = np.asarray(n, float)
    Qbar = complex(np.sum(n * q) / n.sum())
    return {"Q2": Qbar,
            "S_persistent": float(abs(Qbar)),
            "angle_persistent": float(0.5 * np.arctan2(Qbar.imag, Qbar.real)),
            "S_frame_mean": float(np.mean(np.abs(q))),
            "n_frames": int(len(q)), "n_total": float(n.sum())}


# ------------------------------------------------------- pair-axis geometry
def angular_pair_distribution(p, L, W, r_lo=None, r_hi=None, nbins=36,
                              max_pairs=None, seed=0):
    """
    Angular distribution of pair-separation axes inside the shell
    [r_lo, r_hi]. This is a local measurement, not a whole-domain one, and the
    shell must be quoted with the number.

        default r_hi = 0.05 min(L, W)
        default r_lo = 0.2 r_hi = 0.01 min(L, W)

    The histogram is normalised by its own mean, so g(theta) = 1 is the exact
    isotropic reference: for pairs drawn from an annulus the area element
    r dr dtheta carries no theta dependence, so isotropy is flat in theta.

    S = |<exp(2 i theta)>| is computed from the raw angles, independently of
    the binning, and is returned with its finite-sample noise floor
    `S_null95`. `Q2` is the complex moment; pool frames with combine_Q2(),
    never by averaging S or the angle.

    max_pairs thins the sample by an unbiased Bernoulli draw when the shell is
    large enough to make an exact sweep expensive. Q2 stays unbiased; the
    noise floor rises with the thinned count, which the returned S_null95
    already reflects.
    """
    N = len(p)
    if r_hi is None:
        r_hi = 0.05 * min(L, W)
    if r_lo is None:
        r_lo = 0.2 * r_hi
    r_lo, r_hi = float(r_lo), float(r_hi)
    if not (0.0 <= r_lo < r_hi):
        raise ValueError(f"need 0 <= r_lo < r_hi, got {r_lo}, {r_hi}")

    empty = {"theta": np.array([]), "g": np.array([]), "nematic": np.nan,
             "nematic_angle": np.nan, "Q2": complex(np.nan, np.nan),
             "n_pairs": 0, "S_null95": np.nan, "r_range": (r_lo, r_hi),
             "counts": np.zeros(nbins, dtype=np.int64), "keep_p": 1.0,
             "r_mid": float(np.sqrt(r_lo * r_hi)) if r_lo > 0 else r_hi / 2}

    # Set the thinning probability from the exact shell count. count_neighbors
    # avoids materialising the pair array and adapts to clustered frames.
    keep_p = 1.0
