    if max_pairs is not None and N > 1:
        _t = cKDTree(_wrap(p, L, W), boxsize=[L, W])
        n_hi, n_lo = _t.count_neighbors(_t, np.array([r_hi, r_lo]))
        n_shell = max(0.0, float(n_hi - n_lo) / 2.0)
        if n_shell > max_pairs:
            keep_p = float(max_pairs / n_shell)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Accumulate angular moments and histogram counts in blocks; the largest
    # shells can contain tens of millions of pairs.
    edges = np.linspace(0, np.pi, nbins + 1)
    hist = np.zeros(nbins, dtype=np.int64)
    c2 = s2 = 0.0
    npair = 0
    for _ij, d, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, r_hi):
        m = (r >= r_lo) & (r > 0)         # coincident pairs have no axis
        if not m.any():
            continue
        dm = d[m]
        if keep_p < 1.0:
            sel = rng.random(len(dm)) < keep_p
            if not sel.any():
                continue
            dm = dm[sel]
        theta = np.arctan2(dm[:, 1], dm[:, 0])       # 0 = streamwise
        th = np.mod(theta, np.pi)                    # axis -> fold to [0, pi)
        c2 += float(np.cos(2 * th).sum())
        s2 += float(np.sin(2 * th).sum())
        hist += np.histogram(th, bins=edges)[0]
        npair += int(len(dm))

    if npair < 20:
        return empty
    Q2 = complex(c2 / npair, s2 / npair)
    g = hist / hist.mean() if hist.mean() > 0 else hist.astype(float)
    # The polar moment is omitted because th is folded to [0, pi).
    return {"theta": 0.5 * (edges[1:] + edges[:-1]), "g": g,
            "counts": hist,
            "nematic": float(abs(Q2)),
            "nematic_angle": float(0.5 * np.arctan2(Q2.imag, Q2.real)),
            "Q2": Q2,
            "S_null95": nematic_noise_floor(npair),
            "n_pairs": npair, "r_range": (r_lo, r_hi),
            "r_mid": float(np.sqrt(r_lo * r_hi)),
            "keep_p": keep_p}


def anisotropy_vs_scale(p, L, W, r_list=None, nbins=36, max_pairs=None,
                        seed=0):
    """
    Nematic order of the pair separation axes as a function of separation,
    measured on the contiguous shells [r/2, r].

    S(r) is the scale over which pair-axis orientation remains coherent.  For
    a field of parallel filaments it is
    largest at the smallest resolved separation -- inside one filament every
    pair is collinear -- and decays as r grows past the structure.  It is not
    expected to peak at the streak spacing.

    Returned coordinates:
        r      upper shell edge (what the shell is named by)
        r_mid  geometric shell centre sqrt(r_lo r_hi) = r/sqrt(2)
        r_lo   lower shell edge
        r_over_s   r_mid in units of the mean interparticle spacing
                   s = sqrt(LW/N)
        S_null95   per-shell finite-sample noise floor
    """
    # Beyond half the short side, the periodic box truncates separations in the
    # narrow direction, so only streamwise pairs survive and the measurement is
    # geometrically biased. Measured on a uniform null: S = 0.0008 at r = 1.5
    # but S = 0.25 at r = 3.0, purely from the 3:1 box. Stay below Lz/2.
    r_safe = 0.5 * min(L, W)
    s_spacing = float(np.sqrt(L * W / max(len(p), 1)))
    if r_list is None:
        r_list = s_spacing * np.array([1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32], float)
    r_list = np.asarray(r_list, float)
    r_list = r_list[(r_list > 0) & (r_list <= r_safe)]
    out = {k: [] for k in ("r", "r_mid", "r_lo", "r_over_s", "nematic",
                           "angle", "n_pairs", "S_null95")}
    q2 = []
    for i_sh, rh in enumerate(r_list):
        # seed offset per shell: one seed would reuse the same thinning draw
        # on the pairs the shells share, correlating the errors across r
        a = angular_pair_distribution(p, L, W, r_lo=0.5 * rh, r_hi=rh,
                                      nbins=nbins, max_pairs=max_pairs,
                                      seed=seed + 1000 * i_sh)
        if not np.isfinite(a.get("nematic", np.nan)):
            continue
        out["r"].append(rh)
        out["r_mid"].append(a["r_mid"])
        out["r_lo"].append(0.5 * rh)
        out["r_over_s"].append(a["r_mid"] / s_spacing)
        out["nematic"].append(a["nematic"])
        out["angle"].append(a["nematic_angle"])
        out["n_pairs"].append(a["n_pairs"])
        out["S_null95"].append(a["S_null95"])
        q2.append(a["Q2"])
    res = {k: np.array(v) for k, v in out.items()}
    res["Q2"] = np.array(q2, dtype=complex)
    res["spacing"] = s_spacing
    return res


# --------------------------------------------------------- cluster geometry
def _unwrap_component(Q, L, W, eps):
    """
    Periodic unwrapping of one connected component along its neighbour graph.

    Reference-point unwrapping (every member minimum-imaged against Q[0]) is
    correct only while the component is shorter than half the box. For a
    longer, curved or percolating one it folds the far end back on itself and
    manufactures an extent of exactly L/2 -- which in a 3:1 domain reads as a
    long streamwise filament whether or not one is there.

    Here the offsets are propagated along a BFS tree of the eps-graph, and
    then every edge, not just the tree edges, is checked for consistency. An
    edge that disagrees means the component closes on itself around the torus:
    it percolates, no unwrapping exists, and no principal axis is defined.

    Returns (X, wrapped, reached), where `reached` masks the members the BFS
    actually reached. Anything outside it has NO valid offset -- the caller
    must drop those points rather than mix raw wrapped coordinates into the
    covariance. A DBSCAN cluster is connected through its core points, so this
    is normally everything; it is guarded because a clustering routine that
    labels by something other than the eps-graph could break that.
    """
    from scipy.sparse import coo_matrix
    from scipy.sparse.csgraph import breadth_first_order

    n = len(Q)
    box = np.array([L, W], float)
    if n < 2:
        return Q.copy(), False, np.ones(n, bool)

    tree = cKDTree(Q, boxsize=[L, W])
    ij = tree.query_pairs(eps, output_type="ndarray")
    if len(ij) == 0:
        return Q.copy(), False, np.zeros(n, bool)

    G = coo_matrix((np.ones(len(ij)), (ij[:, 0], ij[:, 1])),
                   shape=(n, n)).tocsr()
    order, pred = breadth_first_order(G, 0, directed=False,
                                      return_predecessors=True)
    reached = np.zeros(n, bool)
    reached[order] = True

    off = np.zeros((n, 2))
    for k in order[1:]:
        i = pred[k]
        dq = Q[k] - Q[i]
        off[k] = off[i] - box * np.round(dq / box)

    # Consistency over ALL edges, vectorised. Every discrepancy is an exact
    # integer multiple of the box, so anything above half the short side is a
    # genuine wrap and nothing else can land there.
    a, b = ij[:, 0], ij[:, 1]
    dq = Q[b] - Q[a]
    mi = dq - box * np.round(dq / box)
    resid = (Q[b] + off[b]) - (Q[a] + off[a]) - mi
    reach = reached[a] & reached[b]
    wrapped = bool(np.any(np.abs(resid[reach]) > 0.5 * box.min()))
    return Q + off, wrapped, reached


def cluster_orientation(p, L, W, eps_factor=1.8, min_samples=10, min_size=10,
                        min_anisotropy=0.0, exclude_wrapped=True, eps=None,
                        v=None):
    """
    Orientation of each DBSCAN cluster's principal axis, and the nematic order
    of those orientations.

    Compare this result with the corresponding Poisson null.  In a 3:1 domain a
    chance grouping has more room to extend streamwise, and at eps = 1.8 s the
    expected count inside eps is pi*1.8^2 = 10.2, right at min_samples = 10 --
    so uniform random points may percolate and DBSCAN can return domain-
    spanning components whose major axis follows the box.  Use
    :func:`poisson_null_angular` to establish the reference distribution.

    Periodic unwrapping follows each component's neighbour graph. Components
    that close around the torus are excluded by default because they do not
    have a unique principal axis.  The axis and its reliability come from the
    deviatoric part of the covariance tensor,

           A exp(2 i theta) = (Cxx - Cyy + 2 i Cxy) / (Cxx + Cyy),

    which is equivalent to the eigen-decomposition with
    A = (l1-l2)/(l1+l2).  A nearly round cluster therefore contributes little
    directional weight.

    Returns both weightings:
        nematic        weight N_c            (every clustered particle votes)
        nematic_shape  weight N_c * A_c      (and votes only as far as its
                                              cluster has an axis at all)

    When ``v`` is supplied, each cluster also reports its mean velocity
    direction as an angle from the x axis:

        vel_angles   phi_c = atan2(<v_y>_c, <v_x>_c)  on (-pi, pi]
        vel_speeds   |<v>_c|
        vel_polar    |sum_c N_c exp(i phi_c)| / sum_c N_c

    This is a direction rather than an axis, so the first-order polar moment is the
    right one and no folding happens -- a cluster drifting downstream and one
    drifting upstream are not the same state, unlike a pair axis at theta and
    theta+pi. In a mean flow phi_c clusters near 0 for every cluster; what the
    quantity describes the spread about it, and whether that spread
    narrows as N_c grows (a large cluster averaging over more of the
    fluctuating field should move more nearly with the mean).
    """
    import clustering as cl
    N = len(p)
    db = cl.dbscan_clusters(p, L, W, eps_factor, min_samples)
    lab = np.asarray(db["labels"])
    q = _wrap(p, L, W)
    if eps is None:
        eps = float(db.get("eps", eps_factor * np.sqrt(L * W / max(N, 1))))

    ang, elo, siz, aniso = [], [], [], []
    vang, vspd = [], []
    if v is not None:
        v = np.asarray(v, float)
        if len(v) != N:
            raise ValueError(f"v has {len(v)} rows for {N} particles")
    n_wrapped = n_split = n_small = n_round = 0
    for cid in db["ids"]:
        sel = lab == cid
        Q = q[sel]
        if len(Q) < min_size:
            n_small += 1
            continue
        X, wrapped, reached = _unwrap_component(Q, L, W, eps)
        if wrapped:
            n_wrapped += 1
            if exclude_wrapped:
                continue
        if not reached.all():
            # members the eps-graph never reached have no valid offset; using
            # their raw wrapped coordinates would inject a spurious box-scale
            # extent into the covariance, so they are dropped outright
            n_split += 1
            X = X[reached]
            if len(X) < min_size:
                continue
        C = np.cov(X.T)
        tr = C[0, 0] + C[1, 1]
        if not np.isfinite(tr) or tr <= 0:
            continue
        z = complex(C[0, 0] - C[1, 1], 2 * C[0, 1]) / tr   # = A exp(2 i th)
        A = float(abs(z))
        th = float(0.5 * np.arctan2(z.imag, z.real))
        if A < min_anisotropy:
            n_round += 1
            continue
        ang.append(th)
        aniso.append(A)
        if v is not None:
            vm = v[sel].mean(axis=0)
            vang.append(float(np.arctan2(vm[1], vm[0])))
            vspd.append(float(np.hypot(vm[0], vm[1])))
        # elongation l1/l2 from the same two numbers, no eigensolve needed
        elo.append(float(np.sqrt((1 + A) / max(1 - A, 1e-15))))
        siz.append(len(Q))

    n_noise = int(np.sum(lab < 0))
    base = {"n_wrapped": n_wrapped, "n_disconnected": n_split,
            "n_below_min_size": n_small, "n_below_min_anisotropy": n_round,
            "eps": float(eps),
            "n_noise": n_noise,
            "noise_fraction": float(n_noise / N) if N else np.nan}
    if not ang:
        return {"angles": np.array([]), "sizes": np.array([]),
                "vel_angles": np.array([]), "vel_speeds": np.array([]),
                "vel_polar": np.nan, "vel_angle_mean": np.nan,
                "Q1_vel": complex(np.nan, np.nan),
                "anisotropy": np.array([]), "elongation": np.array([]),
                "nematic": np.nan, "nematic_angle": np.nan,
                "nematic_shape": np.nan, "nematic_unweighted": np.nan,
                "nematic_unweighted_angle": np.nan,
                "S_null95_unweighted": np.nan,
                "Q2": complex(np.nan, np.nan),
                "n_clusters": 0, "n_clustered": 0,
                "median_elongation": np.nan, "weight_top": np.nan,
                "n_eff_clusters": np.nan, **base}

    ang = np.mod(np.asarray(ang), np.pi)
    aniso = np.asarray(aniso, float)
    elo = np.asarray(elo, float)
    siz = np.asarray(siz, float)

    o = _circular_orders(ang, weights=siz, axial=True)
    o_shape = _circular_orders(ang, weights=siz * aniso, axial=True)
    # Keep both weightings: size weighting gives each clustered particle a
    # vote, while the unweighted result describes the typical cluster.
    o_unw = _circular_orders(ang, weights=None, axial=True)
    wnorm = siz / siz.sum()
    vel = {}
    if v is not None and vang:
        # axial=False: a mean velocity is a direction, so the polar moment is
        # the meaningful one here and the angles are never folded
        ov = _circular_orders(np.asarray(vang), weights=siz, axial=False)
        vel = {"vel_angles": np.asarray(vang), "vel_speeds": np.asarray(vspd),
               "vel_polar": ov["polar"], "vel_angle_mean": ov["polar_angle"],
               "Q1_vel": ov["Q1"], "vel_nematic": ov["nematic"]}
    return {"angles": ang, "sizes": siz, "anisotropy": aniso,
            "elongation": elo, **vel,
            "n_clusters": int(len(ang)),
            "n_clustered": int(siz.sum()),
            "clustered_fraction": float(siz.sum() / N) if N else np.nan,
            "median_elongation": float(np.median(elo)),
            "median_anisotropy": float(np.median(aniso)),
            "nematic_shape": o_shape["nematic"],
            "nematic_shape_angle": o_shape["nematic_angle"],
            "nematic_unweighted": o_unw["nematic"],
            "nematic_unweighted_angle": o_unw["nematic_angle"],
            "S_null95_unweighted": o_unw["S_null95"],
            "weight_top": float(wnorm.max()),
            "n_eff_clusters": float(1.0 / np.sum(wnorm ** 2)),
            **base, **o}


def cluster_anisotropy_split(p, L, W, eps_factor=1.8, min_samples=10,
                             min_size=10, max_pairs_per_cluster=4000,
                             seed=0):
    """
    Separate anisotropy within clusters from anisotropy in their arrangement.

    Two fields with identical global pair-axis order can be built two ways:
    elongated streamwise clusters scattered isotropically, or round clusters
    laid out along streamwise lines. The global S cannot distinguish them: the
    first describes particle organisation within a streak, while the second
    describes the arrangement of the clusters.

        S_within   nematic order of pair axes within one cluster,
                   pooled over clusters through the complex moment
        S_between  nematic order of cluster-centroid separation axes

    Centroids on a torus are taken by the circular mean per coordinate
    (map x to an angle 2 pi x / L, average the unit vectors, map back) --
    the arithmetic mean of wrapped coordinates puts a cluster straddling the
    seam in the middle of the box, which is nowhere near it.

    Within-cluster pairs are minimum-imaged, which is exact for any pair
    closer than half the box; a cluster large enough to violate that is
    percolating and has already been excluded upstream by the wrapping test
    in cluster_orientation. Pairs are capped per cluster so one giant cluster
    cannot monopolise S_within; the cap is a uniform subsample, and the
    per-cluster Q2 values are pooled with their actual pair counts as weights.
    """
    import clustering as cl
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    N = len(p)
    db = cl.dbscan_clusters(p, L, W, eps_factor, min_samples)
    lab = np.asarray(db["labels"])
    q = _wrap(p, L, W)
    box = np.array([L, W], float)

    rows_within, cents, cent_w = [], [], []
    for cid in db["ids"]:
        sel = lab == cid
        Q = q[sel]
        n = len(Q)
        if n < min_size:
            continue
        # circular-mean centroid, per coordinate
        cx = (np.angle(np.exp(2j * np.pi * Q[:, 0] / L).mean())
              * L / (2 * np.pi))
        cz = (np.angle(np.exp(2j * np.pi * Q[:, 1] / W).mean())
              * W / (2 * np.pi))
        cents.append([cx % L, cz % W])
        cent_w.append(n)
        # within-cluster pair axes
        npair = n * (n - 1) // 2
        if npair < 1:
            continue
        ii, jj = np.triu_indices(n, 1)
        if len(ii) > max_pairs_per_cluster:
            k = rng.choice(len(ii), max_pairs_per_cluster, replace=False)
            ii, jj = ii[k], jj[k]
        d = Q[ii] - Q[jj]
        d -= box * np.round(d / box)
        th = np.mod(np.arctan2(d[:, 1], d[:, 0]), np.pi)
        Q2 = complex(np.cos(2 * th).mean(), np.sin(2 * th).mean())
        rows_within.append({"Q2": Q2, "n_pairs": npair})

    out = {"n_clusters": len(cents)}
    cw = combine_Q2(rows_within)
    out["S_within"] = cw["S_persistent"]
    out["S_within_angle"] = cw["angle_persistent"]
    out["n_pairs_within"] = cw["n_total"]
    out["S_within_null95"] = (nematic_noise_floor(len(rows_within))
                              if rows_within else np.nan)

    if len(cents) >= 3:
        C = np.asarray(cents, float)
        ii, jj = np.triu_indices(len(C), 1)
        d = C[ii] - C[jj]
        d -= box * np.round(d / box)
        th = np.mod(np.arctan2(d[:, 1], d[:, 0]), np.pi)
        ob = _circular_orders(th, axial=True)
        out["S_between"] = ob["nematic"]
        out["S_between_angle"] = ob["nematic_angle"]
        out["n_pairs_between"] = ob["n"]
        # centroid pairs share centroids, so the independent count is the
        # number of clusters, not pairs, so the floor uses that count
        out["S_between_null95"] = nematic_noise_floor(len(C))
    else:
        out.update({"S_between": np.nan, "S_between_angle": np.nan,
                    "n_pairs_between": 0, "S_between_null95": np.nan})
    return out


# ------------------------------------------------------- velocity direction
def velocity_angle_stats(v, v_tol=None):
    """
    Polar and nematic order of the particle velocity directions.

    In a mean shear the polar order is high and points streamwise; that reflects
    the mean flow rather than collective behaviour, and P = 0.99 says only
    that the particles are going downstream. The informative quantity is the
    order of the fluctuation directions, ``polar_fluct``.

    Particles slower than v_tol are excluded: atan2(0, 0) returns 0, which
    would file a motionless particle as perfectly streamwise and inflate P.
    Default v_tol = 1e-9 x the rms speed, i.e. exact zeros only.

    The fluctuation subtracts ONE global mean. That is right for this study --
    every particle sits in the same wall-parallel plane, so there is only one
    mean to subtract -- and would be wrong for particles spread over y, where
    v - u_p(y) is the fluctuation and v - <v> mostly measures the profile.
    """
    v = np.asarray(v, float)
    sp = np.hypot(v[:, 0], v[:, 1])
    rms = float(np.sqrt(np.nanmean(sp ** 2))) if len(sp) else 0.0
    tol = (1e-9 * rms) if v_tol is None else float(v_tol)
    keep = np.isfinite(sp) & (sp > tol)
    n_drop = int(len(sp) - keep.sum())

    th = np.arctan2(v[keep, 1], v[keep, 0])
    tot = _circular_orders(th)

    vf = (v - v[keep].mean(axis=0)) if keep.any() else v
    spf = np.hypot(vf[:, 0], vf[:, 1])
    rmsf = float(np.sqrt(np.nanmean(spf ** 2))) if len(spf) else 0.0
    tolf = (1e-9 * rmsf) if v_tol is None else float(v_tol)
    keepf = np.isfinite(spf) & (spf > tolf)
    thf = np.arctan2(vf[keepf, 1], vf[keepf, 0])
    fl = _circular_orders(thf)

    return {"polar": tot["polar"], "polar_angle": tot["polar_angle"],
            "nematic": tot["nematic"], "Q1": tot["Q1"], "Q2": tot["Q2"],
            "polar_fluct": fl["polar"], "polar_fluct_angle": fl["polar_angle"],
            "nematic_fluct": fl["nematic"],
            "nematic_fluct_angle": fl["nematic_angle"],
            "Q1_fluct": fl["Q1"], "Q2_fluct": fl["Q2"],
            "n_used": int(keep.sum()), "n_zero_speed": n_drop,
            "n_used_fluct": int(keepf.sum()),
            "mean_velocity": tuple(float(x) for x in v[keep].mean(axis=0))
            if keep.any() else (np.nan, np.nan)}


# ------------------------------------------- radial motion vs pair direction
def angular_relative_velocity(p, v, L, W, r_lo=None, r_hi=None, nbins=18,
                              min_count=20):
    """
    Radial relative velocity resolved by the angle of the separation axis,
    inside the shell [r_lo, r_hi] (same defaults as
    angular_pair_distribution: 0.01 to 0.05 min(L, W)).

        w_r = (v_i - v_j) . r_hat = d r_ij / dt      w_r < 0 = approaching

    ``w_mean`` is a signed mean rather than an approach rate. A bin holding
    violent approach and violent recession in equal measure averages to zero.
    For collision physics use the inward decomposition, which is what feeds the
    Sundaram-Collins kernel:

        frac_approach  P_-(theta) = Pr(w_r < 0 | theta)      how often
        w_minus        S_-(theta) = <-w_r | w_r < 0, theta>  how fast
        inward_flux    A_-(theta) = P_- S_-                  the product

    (Same names and the same sign convention as relative_velocity_stats, so
    the two are directly comparable.)

    For tracers in an in-plane linear shear du_x/dy = G,
    v_i - v_j = (G d_y, 0), so

        <w_r | theta> = (G/2) <r | theta> sin 2 theta

    -- extensional for theta in (0, 90), compressive in (90, 180), extrema at
    45 and 135 deg. Where that applies the sinusoid is the null, not a result,
    and ``shear_reference`` draws it; the signal is the departure from it.

    This reference does not apply to a wall-parallel slab. If the axes are
    streamwise and spanwise -- as in this study --
    "spanwise" axis labels in compare_legs -- then the wall-normal direction
    is OUT of the plane, the mean flow U(y) is uniform across the plane, and
    the in-plane mean gradient is zero. Fitting G from the particles returns
    ~0 (measured 0.037 against a wall gradient of 19.74), which is the correct
    answer, not a units problem. Leave `shear` unset for a slab.

    That makes a nonzero <w_r|theta> the interesting quantity rather than an
    artefact: with no mean in-plane gradient, uniformly distributed particles
    would average the fluctuating strain to zero. It is non-zero only because
    the particles preferentially occupy particular strain regions, so this
    panel measures the strain field sampled by the particles.

    As a symmetry check, spanwise reflection z -> -z implies
    channel flow, so a converged slab measurement must satisfy

        <w_r | theta> = <w_r | 180 deg - theta>

    i.e. symmetry about 90 deg (~ cos 2 theta), not antisymmetry. An
    antisymmetric component is either unconverged statistics or an index/sign
    error, and is worth chasing down before the curve is interpreted.

    Blocked accumulation, so peak memory is one block rather than every pair at
    once (6.5e6 pairs at N = 50000 in the default shell).
    """
    if r_hi is None:
        r_hi = 0.05 * min(L, W)
    if r_lo is None:
        r_lo = 0.2 * r_hi
    r_lo, r_hi = float(r_lo), float(r_hi)
    if not (0.0 <= r_lo < r_hi):
        raise ValueError(f"need 0 <= r_lo < r_hi, got {r_lo}, {r_hi}")

    edges = np.linspace(0, np.pi, nbins + 1)
    z = lambda: np.zeros(nbins)                                  # noqa: E731
    s_w, s_w2, s_abs, s_r = z(), z(), z(), z()
    s_neg, c_neg = z(), z()
    cnt = np.zeros(nbins, dtype=np.int64)

    for ij, d, r in iter_pairs(p, L, W, r_hi):
        m = (r >= r_lo) & (r > 0)
        if not m.any():
            continue
        ij_, d_, r_ = ij[m], d[m], r[m]
        rhat = d_ / r_[:, None]
        wr = np.sum((v[ij_[:, 0]] - v[ij_[:, 1]]) * rhat, axis=1)
        th = np.mod(np.arctan2(d_[:, 1], d_[:, 0]), np.pi)
        idx = np.clip(np.digitize(th, edges) - 1, 0, nbins - 1)
        s_w += np.bincount(idx, weights=wr, minlength=nbins)
        s_w2 += np.bincount(idx, weights=wr ** 2, minlength=nbins)
        s_abs += np.bincount(idx, weights=np.abs(wr), minlength=nbins)
        s_r += np.bincount(idx, weights=r_, minlength=nbins)
        cnt += np.bincount(idx, minlength=nbins)
        neg = wr < 0
        if neg.any():
            s_neg += np.bincount(idx[neg], weights=wr[neg], minlength=nbins)
            c_neg += np.bincount(idx[neg], minlength=nbins)

    keys = ("w_mean", "w_sd", "w_sem", "w_abs", "w_minus", "frac_approach",
            "inward_flux", "r_mean")
    out = {k: np.full(nbins, np.nan) for k in keys}
    ok = cnt >= int(min_count)
    if ok.any():
        n = cnt[ok].astype(float)
        mu = s_w[ok] / n
        var = np.maximum(s_w2[ok] / n - mu ** 2, 0.0)
        out["w_mean"][ok] = mu
        out["w_sd"][ok] = np.sqrt(var * n / np.maximum(n - 1, 1))
        # w_sem treats pairs as independent. They are not -- pairs sharing a
        # particle are correlated, so it is a lower bound on the true error.
        out["w_sem"][ok] = out["w_sd"][ok] / np.sqrt(n)
        out["w_abs"][ok] = s_abs[ok] / n
        out["r_mean"][ok] = s_r[ok] / n
        out["frac_approach"][ok] = c_neg[ok] / n
        with np.errstate(invalid="ignore", divide="ignore"):
            _cn = np.maximum(c_neg[ok], 1)
            out["w_minus"][ok] = np.where(c_neg[ok] > 0,
                                          -s_neg[ok] / _cn, 0.0)
        out["inward_flux"][ok] = out["frac_approach"][ok] * out["w_minus"][ok]

    out.update({"theta": 0.5 * (edges[1:] + edges[:-1]), "edges": edges,
                "n_pairs": cnt, "ok": ok, "min_count": int(min_count),
                "r_range": (r_lo, r_hi), "n_total": int(cnt.sum())})
    return out


def shear_reference(res, shear):
    """
    <w_r | theta> for tracers in an in-plane linear shear du_x/dy = ``shear``,
    evaluated at the measured <r | theta> of `res` (the output of
    angular_relative_velocity). Where it applies, data sitting on this curve
    is the mean flow and only the departure is particle physics.

    This applies only to a plane containing the wall-normal direction. On a wall-parallel
    (streamwise, spanwise) slab the in-plane mean gradient is zero and this
    reference is meaningless -- see angular_relative_velocity. Fit it from the
    data before trusting a remembered number:

        m = res["ok"]
        b = res["r_mean"][m] * np.sin(2 * res["theta"][m])
        G = 2 * np.dot(res["w_mean"][m], b) / np.dot(b, b)

    If G comes back near zero, the plane has no in-plane shear and this
    function has nothing to say about it.
    """
    th = np.asarray(res["theta"], float)
    rm = np.asarray(res["r_mean"], float)
    return 0.5 * float(shear) * rm * np.sin(2 * th)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------- nulls
_NULL_CACHE = {}


def poisson_null_angular(N, L, W, reps=200, seed=0, cluster=True,
                         eps_factor=1.8, min_samples=10, min_size=10,
                         cache=True, verbose=False):
    """
    Estimate the angular null distributions from uniform random points.

    At eps = 1.8 s the DBSCAN neighbourhood
    holds 10.2 points against min_samples = 10, so uniform noise percolates and
    the raw cluster nematic can exceed 0.9 without physical structure.  The
    resulting distribution therefore provides the reference for the cluster
    panel.

    Two hundred repetitions are used by default so that upper percentiles are
    reasonably resolved.

    Axial angles are pooled through their complex moments rather than by
    arithmetic averaging.

    Returns {quantity: {mean, sd, p05, p50, p95, n}} for the magnitudes, plus
    `*_axis_persistent_deg` from the pooled Q2, plus the raw per-rep arrays
    under "samples" so a caller can form any other percentile.

    The result depends only on (N, L, W, DBSCAN settings), not on the data,
    so it is cached; pass cache=False to force a recompute.
    """
    key = (int(N), round(float(L), 9), round(float(W), 9), int(reps),
           int(seed), bool(cluster), float(eps_factor), int(min_samples),
           int(min_size))
    if cache and key in _NULL_CACHE:
        return _NULL_CACHE[key]

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    mags = {}
    q2 = {"pair": [], "cluster": []}
    cluster_keys = ("cluster_nematic", "cluster_nematic_shape",
                    "cluster_median_elongation", "cluster_n",
                    "cluster_wrapped", "clustered_fraction")
    cluster_err = None
    for i in range(int(reps)):
        if verbose and (i % 25 == 0):
            print(f"    poisson_null_angular {i}/{reps}", end="\r", flush=True)
        q = np.column_stack([rng.uniform(0, L, N), rng.uniform(0, W, N)])
        vv = rng.normal(0, 1, (N, 2))
        ap = angular_pair_distribution(q, L, W)
        va = velocity_angle_stats(vv)
        rec = {"pair_nematic": ap["nematic"],
               "vel_polar": va["polar"],
               "vel_polar_fluct": va["polar_fluct"],
               "vel_nematic_fluct": va["nematic_fluct"]}
        q2["pair"].append((ap["Q2"], ap["n_pairs"]))
        if cluster:
            try:
                co_ = cluster_orientation(q, L, W, eps_factor, min_samples,
                                          min_size)
                rec.update({"cluster_nematic": co_["nematic"],
                            "cluster_nematic_shape": co_["nematic_shape"],
                            "cluster_median_elongation":
                                co_["median_elongation"],
                            "cluster_n": co_["n_clusters"],
                            "cluster_wrapped": co_["n_wrapped"],
                            "clustered_fraction":
                                co_.get("clustered_fraction", np.nan)})
                q2["cluster"].append((co_["Q2"], co_.get("n_clustered", 0)))
            except Exception as e:                        # pragma: no cover
                # NaN rather than a missing key, so every series keeps the
                # same length and the failure is visible in `n` instead of
                # silently shortening the null.
                cluster_err = repr(e)
                rec.update({k: np.nan for k in cluster_keys})
        for k, val in rec.items():
            mags.setdefault(k, []).append(float(val))
    if verbose:
        print(" " * 44, end="\r")

    out = {}
    for k, arr in mags.items():
        a = np.asarray(arr, float)
        f = a[np.isfinite(a)]
        out[k] = {"mean": float(np.mean(f)) if f.size else np.nan,
                  "sd": float(np.std(f, ddof=1)) if f.size > 1 else np.nan,
                  "p05": float(np.percentile(f, 5)) if f.size else np.nan,
                  "p50": float(np.percentile(f, 50)) if f.size else np.nan,
                  "p95": float(np.percentile(f, 95)) if f.size else np.nan,
                  "n": int(f.size)}
    for name, lst in q2.items():
        if not lst:
            continue
        pooled = combine_Q2([{"Q2": z, "n_pairs": n} for z, n in lst])
        out[f"{name}_axis_persistent_deg"] = float(
            np.degrees(pooled["angle_persistent"]))
        out[f"{name}_S_persistent"] = pooled["S_persistent"]
    out["samples"] = {k: np.asarray(v, float) for k, v in mags.items()}
    out["reps"] = int(reps)
    out["cluster_error"] = cluster_err
    if cache:
        _NULL_CACHE[key] = out
    return out


def excess_over_null(value, null_entry):
    """
    Compare a measured order parameter with its null distribution.

    `passes` is True only when the value exceeds the null's 95th percentile.
    Values below the null mean have a negative z score and ``passes=False``.
    """
    if null_entry is None or not np.isfinite(value):
        return {"value": value, "excess": np.nan, "z": np.nan, "passes": False}
    mu, sd = null_entry.get("mean", np.nan), null_entry.get("sd", np.nan)
    p95 = null_entry.get("p95", np.nan)
    z = (value - mu) / sd if np.isfinite(sd) and sd > 0 else np.nan
    return {"value": float(value), "null_mean": mu, "null_sd": sd,
            "null_p95": p95, "excess": float(value - mu),
            "z": float(z) if np.isfinite(z) else np.nan,
            "passes": bool(np.isfinite(p95) and value > p95)}


# ------------------------------------------------------- temporal inference
def block_bootstrap(series, n_boot=2000, block=None, seed=0, ci=95.0,
                    tau_ref=None):
    """
    Moving-block bootstrap mean and interval for a per-frame series.

    Frames from one run are correlated, so neither sd nor sd/sqrt(n) is an
    uncertainty on the mean: sd overstates it and sd/sqrt(n) understates it,
    by the factor sqrt(2 tau_int) that the correlation puts there. The block
    length is taken as max(1, 2 tau_int) with tau_int measured by a Sokal
    window.

    The internal correlation-time estimate has a finite-window ceiling.  For a
    mean-centred series the sample
    autocorrelations sum to -1/2, so 0.5 + sum_k rho_k is identically
    zero and the value of tau_int is set entirely by where the window closes.
    The window `k >= 6 tau` can only be reached while k <= n-1, so this
    estimator cannot return tau_int greater than (n-1)/6 -- 4.0 frames on a
    25-frame window -- no matter how correlated the frames actually are. A
    short strongly-correlated window therefore reports a small tau, a short
    block, and a comfortable n_blocks_eff: measured coverage of the nominal
    95% interval at n = 25 is 54% when the true tau_int is 4.5 frames and 39%
    when it is 9.5, with no warning fired because n_blocks_eff reads 12.

    A short window may not resolve its own correlation time. Measure tau_int on
    the full record (all 10001 saved frames) and pass it here as `tau_ref`,
    in units of the frames of this series -- if the series is strided by k,
    divide the full-record tau_int by k. `tau_resolved` is False whenever the
    internal estimate is sitting against its own ceiling, which is the signal
    that tau_ref is required rather than optional.

    ``n_blocks_eff`` is the effective sample size. Below about 10 the interval is
    indicative only, and the returned dict says so in `warning`.
    """
    x = np.asarray(series, float)
    x = x[np.isfinite(x)]
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return {"mean": np.nan, "lo": np.nan, "hi": np.nan, "n": 0,
                "tau_int": np.nan, "block": 0, "n_blocks_eff": 0.0,
                "warning": "no finite values"}
    if n < 4:
        return {"mean": float(x.mean()), "lo": np.nan, "hi": np.nan, "n": n,
                "tau_int": np.nan, "block": n, "n_blocks_eff": 1.0,
                "warning": f"only {n} frames; no interval"}

    xc = x - x.mean()
    denom = float(np.dot(xc, xc))
    tau = 0.5
    if denom > 0:
        for k in range(1, n):
            rho = float(np.dot(xc[:-k], xc[k:])) / denom
            if k >= 6 * tau:                 # Sokal self-consistent window
                break
            tau += rho
    tau_meas = max(0.5, float(tau))
    # The largest tau the Sokal window can express on n points (see docstring).
    tau_ceiling = max(0.5, (n - 1) / 6.0)
    # Calibrated against measured coverage: this flags n=25/rho>=0.5,
    # n=100/rho>=0.8 and n=400/rho=0.95 (coverage 74%, 76%, 70%) while
    # leaving n=25 white noise and n=400/rho=0.5 unflagged (92%, 87%).
    # A hard floor on n as well: at n = 25 the ceiling is 4.0, so the
    # largest tau that would still leave 10 blocks is ~1.25 -- the window
    # cannot certify anything useful, and in ~10% of strongly correlated
    # draws the sample autocorrelation comes out near zero and would
    # otherwise pass silently. Below n = 50 the answer is always "not
    # certified", which is the honest one.
    tau_resolved = bool(n >= 50
                        and tau_meas < 0.5 * tau_ceiling
                        and n >= 40.0 * tau_meas)
    if tau_ref is not None and np.isfinite(float(tau_ref)):
        tau = max(0.5, float(tau_ref))
        tau_source = "supplied (tau_ref)"
    else:
        tau = tau_meas
        tau_source = "measured on this window"
    b = int(block) if block else max(1, min(n // 2, int(round(2 * tau))))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n_blk = int(np.ceil(n / b))
    starts = rng.integers(0, n - b + 1, size=(n_boot, n_blk))
    # Build bootstrap indices in chunks to keep memory use bounded.
    off = np.arange(b)[None, None, :]
    means = np.empty(n_boot)
    step = max(1, int(4_000_000 // max(n_blk * b, 1)))
    for s0 in range(0, n_boot, step):
        st = starts[s0:s0 + step]
        idx = (st[:, :, None] + off).reshape(len(st), -1)[:, :n]
        means[s0:s0 + len(st)] = x[idx].mean(axis=1)
    lo, hi = np.percentile(means, [(100 - ci) / 2, 100 - (100 - ci) / 2])
    n_eff = n / b
    warn = []
    if not tau_resolved and tau_ref is None and block is None:
        warn.append(f"tau_int={tau_meas:.2f} is at this window's resolution "
                    f"ceiling ((n-1)/6={tau_ceiling:.2f}); the window cannot "
                    f"measure its own correlation time -- measure it on the "
                    f"full record and pass tau_ref=. The interval below is "
                    f"NOT trustworthy")
    if n_eff < 10:
        warn.append(f"only {n_eff:.1f} independent blocks (want >=10); "
                    f"interval is indicative")
    return {"mean": float(x.mean()), "lo": float(lo), "hi": float(hi),
            "n": n, "tau_int": tau, "block": b, "n_blocks_eff": float(n_eff),
            "sd": float(np.std(x, ddof=1)),
            "tau_meas": float(tau_meas), "tau_ceiling": float(tau_ceiling),
            "tau_resolved": tau_resolved, "tau_source": tau_source,
            "warning": "; ".join(warn)}


def block_bootstrap_diff(a, b, n_boot=2000, block=None, seed=0, ci=95.0,
                         tau_ref=None):
    """
    Paired moving-block bootstrap for the difference between two legs.

    Paired because the legs share the DNS realisation at the same instants:
    one block draw is applied to both series, so a carrier-flow excursion
    cancels in the difference instead of being resampled into its error bar.
    Resampling the legs independently inflates the interval severalfold.

    Significance is `excludes_zero`, and it is the only basis on which a
    difference like Delta S = 0.005 may be called an effect.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, float)
    b = np.asarray(b, float)
    n = min(a.size, b.size)
    a, b = a[:n], b[:n]
    d = a - b
    keep = np.isfinite(d)
    if keep.sum() < 4:
        return {"diff": float(np.nanmean(d)) if keep.any() else np.nan,
                "lo": np.nan, "hi": np.nan, "excludes_zero": False,
                "n": int(keep.sum()), "warning": "too few paired frames"}
    r = block_bootstrap(d[keep], n_boot=n_boot, block=block, seed=seed, ci=ci,
                        tau_ref=tau_ref)
    r["diff"] = r.pop("mean")
    # A zero-width interval at zero does not exclude zero. Identical legs give
    # diff = 0 with lo = hi = 0, and "(lo > 0) == (hi > 0)" is True for that
    # -- a zero-width interval AT zero read as excluding it. The same rule is
    # also boundary-asymmetric: [-0.2, 0] passes while [0, +0.2] does not.
    # Require an open interval lying on one side of zero.
    r["excludes_zero"] = bool(
        np.isfinite(r["lo"]) and np.isfinite(r["hi"])
        and (r["lo"] > 0 or r["hi"] < 0))
    r["mean_a"] = float(np.nanmean(a))
    r["mean_b"] = float(np.nanmean(b))
    return r


def block_bootstrap_diff_multi(A, B, n_boot=2000, block=None, seed=0,
                               ci=95.0, tau_ref=None):
    """
    Paired moving-block bootstrap across several columns, with pointwise and
    simultaneous (sup-t) bands.

    A and B are (n_frames, n_col), with one column per shell, scale or bin. One block
    draw is applied to every column of both legs, which is what makes the
    band simultaneous and what keeps the columns' shared frames shared.

    The simultaneous band supports claims about the shape of a curve -- "the
    legs cross", "the sign changes with scale", "the effect is confined to
    small r" -- is a claim about many columns at once. Reading pointwise 95%
    intervals across K columns and reporting only those that exclude zero
    ignores multiplicity. Use `excludes_zero` for one named column chosen
    in advance, and `excludes_zero_sim` for any claim about the curve.

    Returns diff, lo/hi (pointwise), lo_sim/hi_sim, se, the sup-t critical
    value, and the resolution diagnostics of the underlying block bootstrap.
    """
    A = np.atleast_2d(np.asarray(A, float))
    B = np.atleast_2d(np.asarray(B, float))
    n = min(A.shape[0], B.shape[0])
    k = min(A.shape[1], B.shape[1])
    D = A[:n, :k] - B[:n, :k]
    col_ok = np.isfinite(D).all(axis=0)
    out = {"diff": np.full(k, np.nan), "lo": np.full(k, np.nan),
           "hi": np.full(k, np.nan), "lo_sim": np.full(k, np.nan),
           "hi_sim": np.full(k, np.nan), "se": np.full(k, np.nan),
           "excludes_zero": np.zeros(k, bool),
           "excludes_zero_sim": np.zeros(k, bool),
           "n": n, "n_col_used": int(col_ok.sum()), "crit_sim": np.nan,
           "crit_pointwise": np.nan, "warning": ""}
    if n < 4 or not col_ok.any():
        out["warning"] = "too few paired frames or no finite column"
        return out

    # block length from the pooled column-mean series, so every column shares
    # one block structure (a per-column block length would break the pairing)
    ref = block_bootstrap(np.nanmean(D[:, col_ok], axis=1), n_boot=16,
                          block=block, seed=seed, tau_ref=tau_ref)
    b = int(ref["block"])
    for key in ("tau_int", "tau_meas", "tau_ceiling", "tau_resolved",
                "tau_source", "n_blocks_eff"):
        out[key] = ref[key]
    out["block"] = b
    out["warning"] = ref["warning"]

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n_blk = int(np.ceil(n / b))
    off = np.arange(b)[None, None, :]
    starts = rng.integers(0, n - b + 1, size=(n_boot, n_blk))
    draws = np.empty((n_boot, k))
    step = max(1, int(4_000_000 // max(n_blk * b * max(k, 1), 1)))
    for s0 in range(0, n_boot, step):
        st = starts[s0:s0 + step]
        idx = (st[:, :, None] + off).reshape(len(st), -1)[:, :n]
        draws[s0:s0 + len(st)] = D[idx].mean(axis=1)

    d_hat = D.mean(axis=0)
    se = draws.std(axis=0, ddof=1)
    alpha = (100.0 - ci) / 100.0
    lo = np.percentile(draws, 100 * alpha / 2, axis=0)
    hi = np.percentile(draws, 100 * (1 - alpha / 2), axis=0)
    # sup-t: the largest standardised deviation across columns per replicate
    good = col_ok & (se > 0)
    if good.any():
        t = np.abs(draws[:, good] - d_hat[good]) / se[good]
        crit = float(np.percentile(np.nanmax(t, axis=1), 100 * (1 - alpha)))
    else:
        crit = np.nan
    out["diff"], out["lo"], out["hi"], out["se"] = d_hat, lo, hi, se
    out["lo_sim"] = d_hat - crit * se
    out["hi_sim"] = d_hat + crit * se
    out["crit_sim"] = crit
    out["crit_pointwise"] = float(
        np.percentile(np.abs(rng.standard_normal(20000)), 100 * (1 - alpha)))
    fin = np.isfinite(lo) & np.isfinite(hi) & good
    out["excludes_zero"] = fin & ((lo > 0) | (hi < 0))
    fs = np.isfinite(out["lo_sim"]) & np.isfinite(out["hi_sim"]) & good
    out["excludes_zero_sim"] = fs & ((out["lo_sim"] > 0) |
                                     (out["hi_sim"] < 0))
    return out


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ summary
def analyse_angular(p, v, L, W, cluster=True):
    """
    One-call summary of the angular structure.

    Axes are reported in degrees for display. Do not average them, do not
    difference them between legs, and do not feed them to a null: pool the
    `*_Q2` complex moments with combine_Q2() instead.
    """
    ap = angular_pair_distribution(p, L, W)
    va = velocity_angle_stats(v)
    out = {"pair_nematic": ap.get("nematic", np.nan),
           "pair_nematic_null95": ap.get("S_null95", np.nan),
           "pair_axis_deg": np.degrees(ap.get("nematic_angle", np.nan)),
           "pair_n_pairs": ap.get("n_pairs", 0),
           "pair_Q2": ap.get("Q2", complex(np.nan, np.nan)),
           "vel_polar": va["polar"],
           "vel_polar_fluct": va["polar_fluct"],
           "vel_nematic_fluct": va["nematic_fluct"],
           "vel_n_zero_speed": va["n_zero_speed"]}
    if cluster:
        co_ = cluster_orientation(p, L, W)
        out.update({"cluster_nematic": co_.get("nematic", np.nan),
                    "cluster_nematic_shape": co_.get("nematic_shape", np.nan),
                    # One-cluster, one-vote result from cluster_orientation.
                    "cluster_nematic_unweighted":
                        co_.get("nematic_unweighted", np.nan),
                    "cluster_axis_deg":
                        np.degrees(co_.get("nematic_angle", np.nan)),
                    "cluster_median_elongation":
                        co_.get("median_elongation", np.nan),
                    "cluster_n": co_.get("n_clusters", 0),
                    "cluster_n_wrapped": co_.get("n_wrapped", 0),
                    "cluster_weight_top": co_.get("weight_top", np.nan),
                    "cluster_Q2": co_.get("Q2", complex(np.nan, np.nan))})
        # Separate within-cluster anisotropy from the cluster arrangement.
        try:
            spl = cluster_anisotropy_split(p, L, W)
            out.update({"cluster_S_within": spl.get("S_within", np.nan),
                        "cluster_S_between": spl.get("S_between", np.nan)})
        except Exception:                                 # pragma: no cover
            out.update({"cluster_S_within": np.nan,
                        "cluster_S_between": np.nan})
    return out
structures.pyFlow gradients, streaks and particle–structure relationshipsSee code ↗

Periodic directions use a spectral derivative on the DNS grid.

"""Turbulent-structure diagnostics for a near-wall DNS slab.

The component order is streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal, denoted
``(x, z, y)``, while canonical arrays use ``(z, x, y)`` axes. Wall-normal
``y`` is distance from the no-slip surface. Wall-parallel derivatives are
spectral, and wall-normal derivatives use local Lagrange stencils on the
physical node positions.

The diagnostics include streamwise-velocity streaks, in-plane strain and
vorticity, and slice divergence ``d_x u + d_z w``. For incompressible
three-dimensional flow, slice divergence equals ``-d_y v`` and need not
vanish on a wall-parallel plane. Negative particle-density correlation with
slice divergence indicates preferential sampling of convergent regions.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np

import slab_interp as si
from slab_interp import (HX, HZ, LX, LZ, Y_NODES, NU, N_PLANES, N_SPAN,
                         N_STREAM, WALL_NORMAL_SIGN, WN_DEGREE,
                         lagrange_derivative_weights)

PLANE_TOP = N_PLANES - 1      # the plane the particles sit closest to

# Shared snapshot count for the ensemble-averaged streak correlation.
LAMBDA_SNAPS = 1000


def _spec(spec=None):
    return si._SPEC if spec is None else spec


# --------------------------------------------------------------- gradients
def d_spectral(F, axis, h):
    """Exact derivative of a periodic field along `axis` with spacing `h`."""
    n = F.shape[axis]
    k = np.fft.fftfreq(n, d=h / (2 * np.pi))
    sh = [1, 1]
    sh[axis] = n
    return np.real(np.fft.ifft(1j * k.reshape(sh) * np.fft.fft(F, axis=axis),
                               axis=axis))


def d_wall_normal(F, at=1, y=None, degree=None):
    """
    Evaluate ``dF/dy`` at plane or height ``at`` from the stored planes.

    F : (n_span, n_stream, n_planes). Local Lagrange derivative of degree
    `degree` on the true node positions, with the sign that converts
    d/d(wall_distance) into d/dy_channel.

    ``at`` must match the location used for the in-plane derivatives. Evaluating
    the wall-normal term at one node while taking d/dx and d/dz at another
    mixes two locations, and continuity fails by tens of percent rather than
    the small residual it should give.
    """
    y = Y_NODES if y is None else np.asarray(y, float)
    degree = WN_DEGREE if degree is None else int(degree)
    if F.shape[-1] != len(y):
        raise ValueError(f"F has {F.shape[-1]} planes but y has {len(y)}")
    # An integer selects a node; a noninteger float specifies a physical height.
    y_at = (float(at) if isinstance(at, float)
            and not float(at).is_integer() else float(y[int(at)]))
    idx, dw = lagrange_derivative_weights(y, np.atleast_1d(y_at),
                                          degree=degree)
    out = np.zeros(F.shape[:2])
    for m in range(idx.shape[1]):
        out += F[:, :, idx[0, m]] * dw[0, m]
    return WALL_NORMAL_SIGN * out


def plane_weights_at(a, y=None, degree=None):
    """
    Value-interpolation weights at wall-normal height y = a.

    Particle forcing is evaluated at the centre height ``y = a``, which need
    not coincide with a grid node. Comparisons with flow fields therefore use
    the same local Lagrange interpolation as the engine.
    """
    y = Y_NODES if y is None else np.asarray(y, float)
    degree = WN_DEGREE if degree is None else int(degree)
    a = float(a)
    if not (y[0] - 1e-12 <= a <= y[-1] + 1e-12):
        raise ValueError(f"height a = {a:.6e} outside the slab "
                         f"[{y[0]:.3e}, {y[-1]:.3e}]")
    n = degree + 1
    j = int(np.clip(np.searchsorted(y, a) - (n // 2), 0, len(y) - n))
    idx = np.arange(j, j + n)
    w = np.ones(n)
    for i in range(n):
        for m in range(n):
            if i != m:
                w[i] *= (a - y[idx[m]]) / (y[idx[i]] - y[idx[m]])
    return idx, w / w.sum()


def _at_height(F3, a):
    """Interpolate a (span, stream, planes) stack to wall-normal y = a."""
    idx, w = plane_weights_at(a)
    return np.tensordot(F3[:, :, idx], w, axes=([2], [0]))


def load_plane(k, plane=PLANE_TOP, spec=None, f=None):
    """
    Velocity components at one snapshot and plane, plus the full plane stack.

    ``plane`` may be an integer node index or a wall-normal height ``y = a`` in
    wall-distance units -- pass the run's own radius to sample the fields
    where that run's particles actually are. The float form interpolates the
    stack with the engine's local Lagrange scheme (see plane_weights_at).

    Pass ``spec`` to override the active dataset specification.
    """
    sp = _spec(spec)
    U, V, W = sp.load_snapshot(k, f=f)              # canonical (span, stream, p)
    if isinstance(plane, float) and not float(plane).is_integer():
        return {"u": _at_height(U, plane), "v": _at_height(V, plane),
                "w": _at_height(W, plane), "U3": U, "V3": V, "W3": W,
                "height": float(plane)}
    plane = int(plane)
    return {"u": U[:, :, plane], "v": V[:, :, plane], "w": W[:, :, plane],
            "U3": U, "V3": V, "W3": W, "height": float(Y_NODES[plane])}


# ------------------------------------------------------------- structures
def fluctuations(u):
    """u' = u - <u>, the mean taken over the whole plane."""
    return u - u.mean()


def velocity_gradient(fld, plane=PLANE_TOP):
    """
    In-plane velocity-gradient tensor components, plus the wall-normal term.

    Array axis 0 is spanwise (z), axis 1 is streamwise (x), so
        d/dx -> axis 1 with spacing HX
        d/dz -> axis 0 with spacing HZ
    """
    u, w = fld["u"], fld["w"]
    return {
        "dudx": d_spectral(u, 1, HX), "dudz": d_spectral(u, 0, HZ),
        "dwdx": d_spectral(w, 1, HX), "dwdz": d_spectral(w, 0, HZ),
        "dvdy": d_wall_normal(fld["V3"], at=plane),
    }


def strain_vorticity(g):
    """
    In-plane strain magnitude, vorticity, Q and the Okubo-Weiss parameter.

        S_xx = dudx,  S_zz = dwdz,  S_xz = (dudz + dwdx)/2
        omega_y = dwdx - dudz
        Q     = (Omega_ij Omega_ij - S_ij S_ij)/2 ;  Q > 0 rotation
        W_OW  = s_n^2 + s_s^2 - omega^2 = -4 Q  ;  W > 0 strain
        with  s_n = dudx - dwdz,  s_s = dudz + dwdx.

    A single tensor convention is used throughout, with ``Q = -W_OW/4``;
    therefore ``Q > 0`` denotes rotation and ``W_OW > 0`` denotes strain.
    """
    Sxx, Szz = g["dudx"], g["dwdz"]
    Sxz = 0.5 * (g["dudz"] + g["dwdx"])
    omega = g["dwdx"] - g["dudz"]
    SS = Sxx ** 2 + Szz ** 2 + 2 * Sxz ** 2              # S_ij S_ij
    OO = 0.5 * omega ** 2                                 # Omega_ij Omega_ij
    Q = 0.5 * (OO - SS)
    W_ow = (Sxx - Szz) ** 2 + (2.0 * Sxz) ** 2 - omega ** 2   # = -4 Q
    return {"S_mag": np.sqrt(2.0 * SS), "omega": omega, "Q": Q,
            "okubo_weiss": W_ow, "Sxx": Sxx, "Szz": Szz, "Sxz": Sxz}


def slice_divergence(g):
    """
    d_x u + d_z w on the wall-parallel cut.

    Equals ``-d_y v`` for incompressible 3D flow, so it is generally nonzero.
    This is the field that concentrates the particles: convergence (negative)
    collects them, divergence (positive) thins them out.

    ``residual_rel`` measures continuity closure and is sensitive to the
    component mapping, geometry, and ``WALL_NORMAL_SIGN``.
    """
    div2 = g["dudx"] + g["dwdz"]
    dvdy = g["dvdy"]
    resid = div2 + dvdy
    scale = np.sqrt(np.mean(g["dudx"] ** 2) + np.mean(g["dwdz"] ** 2)
                    + np.mean(dvdy ** 2))
    return {"div2d": div2, "dvdy": dvdy, "residual": resid,
            "residual_rel": float(resid.std() / max(scale, 1e-30))}


def streak_spacing(u, max_lag=None):
    """
    Spanwise two-point correlation for one snapshot and the spacing implied
    by its first local minimum.

    A single-frame correlation is noisy after it decays toward zero. Use
    ``streak_spacing_mean`` for quantitative estimates of this ensemble
    statistic.
    """
    up = fluctuations(u)
    n = up.shape[0]
    max_lag = n // 2 if max_lag is None else max_lag
    c = np.array([float((up * np.roll(up, m, axis=0)).mean() / (up * up).mean())
                  for m in range(max_lag)])
    return {"lags": np.arange(max_lag), "corr": c, **_first_min(c)}


def _first_min(c):
    """First local minimum of a correlation curve, with sub-lag refinement."""
    d = np.diff(c)
    up_ = np.where(d > 0)[0]
    lag = int(up_[0]) if len(up_) else int(np.argmin(c))
    if 0 < lag < len(c) - 1:                       # parabolic refinement
        a, b, cc = c[lag - 1], c[lag], c[lag + 1]
        den = a - 2 * b + cc
        lag_f = lag + (0.5 * (a - cc) / den if den != 0 else 0.0)
    else:
        lag_f = float(lag)
    # The estimate lambda = 2 x (first minimum) treats the minimum as the
    # anti-correlated neighbouring streak at half a spacing. Report its depth
    # to distinguish a resolved dip from a noise-induced turnover. Near-wall
    # streaks may decorrelate before a secondary peak at Delta z = lambda.
    depth = float(c[lag]) if lag < len(c) else np.nan
    i_lam = int(round(2 * lag_f))
    peak = float(c[i_lam]) if 0 < i_lam < len(c) else np.nan
    return {"lag_min": lag, "lag_min_refined": lag_f,
            "lambda": 2 * lag_f * HZ, "lambda_cells": 2 * lag_f,
            "R_min": depth, "R_at_lambda": peak}


def streak_spacing_mean(plane=PLANE_TOP, k0=0, k1=None,
                        n=LAMBDA_SNAPS, max_lag=None,
                        spec=None, n_groups=4):
    """
    Streak spacing from the ensemble-averaged spanwise correlation.

    Average over ``n`` snapshots before locating the first minimum.

    The spacing is dataset dependent and should be recomputed for each record.
    """
    import h5py
    sp = _spec(spec)
    g = sp.read_geometry()
    k1 = g["n_t"] - 1 if k1 is None else k1
    ks = np.linspace(k0, k1, min(n, k1 - k0 + 1)).astype(int)
    acc = None
    with h5py.File(sp.path, "r") as f:
        for k in ks:
            u, _, _ = sp.load_plane_stack(int(k), plane, f=f)
            up = u - u.mean()
            ml = up.shape[0] // 2 if max_lag is None else max_lag
            c = np.array([float((up * np.roll(up, m, axis=0)).mean()
                                / (up * up).mean()) for m in range(ml)])
            acc = c if acc is None else acc + c
    c = acc / len(ks)
    out = {"lags": np.arange(len(c)), "corr": c, "n_snapshots": len(ks),
            **_first_min(c)}

    # Estimate uncertainty by splitting snapshots into independent groups and
    # measuring lambda in each. The group spread captures sensitivity to a
    # shallow or noisy first minimum.
    if n_groups > 1 and len(ks) >= 2 * n_groups:
        lam = []
        for g in range(n_groups):
            sub = ks[g::n_groups]
            acc_g = None
            with h5py.File(sp.path, "r") as f:
                for k in sub:
                    u, _, _ = sp.load_plane_stack(int(k), plane, f=f)
                    up = u - u.mean()
                    ml = up.shape[0] // 2 if max_lag is None else max_lag
                    cg = np.array([float((up * np.roll(up, m, axis=0)).mean()
                                         / (up * up).mean())
                                   for m in range(ml)])
                    acc_g = cg if acc_g is None else acc_g + cg
            lam.append(_first_min(acc_g / len(sub))["lambda"])
        lam = np.array(lam)
        out["lambda_groups"] = lam
        out["lambda_sd"] = float(lam.std(ddof=1))
        out["lambda_sem"] = float(lam.std(ddof=1) / np.sqrt(n_groups))
    return out


def identify_streaks(u, threshold=1.0):
    """Label low- and high-speed streaks by thresholding u' at `threshold` rms."""
    up = fluctuations(u)
    s = up.std()
    low, high = up < -threshold * s, up > threshold * s
    return {"u_fluct": up, "low": low, "high": high,
            "frac_low": float(low.mean()), "frac_high": float(high.mean()),
            "rms": float(s)}


# ---------------------------------------------------- particles vs structure
def bin_particles(p, nb_x, nb_z, L=LX, W=LZ):
    """Particle number density on an (nb_x, nb_z) grid, normalised to mean 1."""
    H, _, _ = np.histogram2d(np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W),
                             bins=[nb_x, nb_z], range=[[0, L], [0, W]])
    return H / H.mean()


def coarsen(F, nb_x, nb_z):
    """
    Area-average a (spanwise, streamwise) field onto an (nb_x, nb_z) grid.

    Each requested bin count must divide its source axis so that cells contain
    equal numbers of input points. The two directions may have different
    lengths, so use
    nearest_divisor(N_STREAM, ...) and nearest_divisor(N_SPAN, ...)
    separately.
    """
    nz, nx = F.shape
    if nx % nb_x or nz % nb_z:
        raise ValueError(
            f"coarsen: {nb_x} must divide {nx} and {nb_z} must divide {nz}. "
            f"Otherwise the output cells hold unequal numbers of input points, "
            f"which biases both the mean and the per-cell noise. "
            f"Valid streamwise: {divisors(nx)}; spanwise: {divisors(nz)}. "
            f"Use nearest_divisor() to pick one near a target scale.")
    fz, fx = nz // nb_z, nx // nb_x
    return F.reshape(nb_z, fz, nb_x, fx).mean((1, 3)).T


def divisors(n):
    """Grid sizes that coarsen() accepts for an axis of length n."""
    return [d for d in range(1, n + 1) if n % d == 0]


def nearest_divisor(n, target):
    """The divisor of n closest to `target`. Use this to hit a physical scale."""
    d = np.array(divisors(n))
    return int(d[np.argmin(np.abs(d - target))])


def time_averaged_divergence(k_end, window=None, plane=PLANE_TOP,
                             n_sample=60, crop=None, spec=None):
    """
    Slice divergence averaged over the `window` snapshots ending at k_end.

    The average represents sustained convergence over the selected window,
    which is more relevant to accumulated particle density than a single
    instantaneous field.

    window : number of snapshots ending at ``k_end``; defaults to the record.
    """
    import h5py
    sp = _spec(spec)
    window = sp.read_geometry()["n_t"] if window is None else window
    ks = np.linspace(max(0, k_end - window + 1), k_end,
                     min(window, n_sample)).astype(int)
    acc = None
    with h5py.File(sp.path, "r") as f:
        for kk in ks:
            fld = load_plane(int(kk), plane, spec=sp, f=f)
            g = velocity_gradient(fld, plane)
            d = g["dudx"] + g["dwdz"]
            acc = d if acc is None else acc + d
    D = acc / len(ks)
    if crop is None:
        return D
    n_stream, n_span = (crop, crop) if np.isscalar(crop) else crop
    return D[:n_span, :n_stream]


def correlate_particles_with_field(p, field, nb_x=24, nb_z=8, L=LX, W=LZ):
    """
    Correlate the particle density with a flow field on a common coarse grid.

    A negative correlation with slice divergence indicates preferential
    particle concentration in convergent regions of the two-dimensional cut.
    """
    n = bin_particles(p, nb_x, nb_z, L, W)
    f = coarsen(field, nb_x, nb_z)
    a, b = n.ravel() - n.mean(), f.ravel() - f.mean()
    denom = np.sqrt((a * a).sum() * (b * b).sum())
    r = float((a * b).sum() / denom) if denom > 0 else np.nan
    return {"corr": r, "density": n, "field_coarse": f, "n_bins": nb_x * nb_z}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- summary
def analyse_structures(k=200, plane=PLANE_TOP, spec=None):
    """Every structure diagnostic for one snapshot."""
    fld = load_plane(k, plane, spec=spec)
    g = velocity_gradient(fld, plane)
    sv = strain_vorticity(g)
    dv = slice_divergence(g)
    st = streak_spacing(fld["u"])
    sk = identify_streaks(fld["u"])
    return {
        "field": fld, "grad": g, "strain": sv, "div": dv,
        "streaks": {**st, **sk},
        "dissipation": float(NU * (sv["S_mag"] ** 2).mean()),
        "rms_strain": float(sv["S_mag"].std()),
        "rms_vorticity": float(sv["omega"].std()),
        "rms_div2d": float(dv["div2d"].std()),
        "div_over_strain": float(dv["div2d"].std() / sv["S_mag"].std()),
        "continuity_residual": dv["residual_rel"],
        "frac_rotation": float((sv["Q"] > 0).mean()),
        "lambda": st["lambda"],
    }


def report(k=200, plane=PLANE_TOP, spec=None):
    a = analyse_structures(k, plane, spec=spec)
    print(f"TURBULENT STRUCTURES   snapshot {k}, plane {plane} of "
          f"{N_PLANES}  (y = {Y_NODES[plane]:.4e}, "
          f"y+ = {si.to_wall_units(Y_NODES[plane]):.3f})\n")
    print(f"  streak spacing lambda    = {a['lambda']:.4f}"
          f"   ({a['streaks']['lambda_cells']:.1f} spanwise cells)")
    _rm = a["streaks"].get("R_min", np.nan)
    print(f"  R_uu at lambda/2         = {_rm:+.3f}"
          + ("   <- shallow: the first minimum is barely above the noise, "
             "so lambda is poorly determined" if _rm > -0.10 else
             "   (a clear anti-correlated neighbour)"))
    print(f"  u' rms                   = {a['streaks']['rms']:.4e}")
    print(f"  low-speed streak area    = {100*a['streaks']['frac_low']:.1f} %"
          f"   (|u'| > 1 rms)")
    print(f"  rms in-plane strain      = {a['rms_strain']:.4e}")
    print(f"  rms in-plane vorticity   = {a['rms_vorticity']:.4e}")
    print(f"  rotation-dominated area  = {100*a['frac_rotation']:.1f} %  (Q > 0)")
    print(f"\n  rms SLICE divergence     = {a['rms_div2d']:.4e}")
    print(f"  div2d / strain           = {a['div_over_strain']:.3f}"
          "   <- nonzero: the cut is compressible")
    print(f"  continuity residual      = {100*a['continuity_residual']:.2f} %"
          f"   (degree-{WN_DEGREE} wall-normal on {N_PLANES} planes)")
    if a["continuity_residual"] > 0.5:
        print("    !! that is far too large. Check WALL_NORMAL_SIGN and the "
              "component/axis pairing before reading anything else here.")
    return a
vertical_impulse.pyPost-processing of diagnostic contact impulsesSee code ↗

Archived slip components allow a diagnostic impulse to be re-evaluated for another contact law.

"""Analyse the diagnostic vertical impulse recorded at particle contacts.

The dynamics apply only planar impulses.  For each rough contact, the engine
also records the vertical component that an unconstrained three-dimensional
law would produce from the full tangential slip, including in-plane spin:

    U~_c,ij   = U_j - U_i - r (omega_i + omega_j) x n
    U~_T      = U~_c,ij - (U~_c,ij . n) n            in the plane (t, e_y)
    Pz~       = P~_T . e_y,     P~_T = -(m/7)(1 - e_par) U~_T            (C)
                                P~_T = -min((m/7)(1 - e_par)|U~_T|,
                                            mu_pp P_perp) U~_T/|U~_T|      (D, E)

Pz~ is the hypothetical vertical impulse on particle j, with -Pz~ on i.  It
and its torque are recorded for analysis but are not applied.  Each contact
stores Pz~, the vertical slip U~_y, horizontal slip w_t and normal impulse
P_perp, allowing Pz~ to be recomputed for other (e_par, mu_pp) values.

For one or more archives of the same run, the script reports:

  1. the distribution of |Pz~|: PDF, and the complementary CDF
     P(|Pz~| > Lambda)  (m = 1, so Lambda is a vertical velocity jump)
  2. the exceedance-rate curve: contacts with |Pz~| > Lambda per particle
     per unit time, as a function of Lambda
  3. the time series of that rate for a chosen --threshold (windowed), and
     the fraction of contacts above it
  4. where the vertical slip comes from: PDF of |U~_y| / |U~_T| (1 = the
     slip is purely vertical, i.e. driven by the in-plane spins)
  5. rates re-evaluated for other contact laws from the archived slips
  6. if the run carried g_eff (model E), a physical reference threshold:
     the impulse whose ballistic hop height |Pz~|^2 / (2 g_eff) equals one
     radius, Lambda_a = sqrt(2 g_eff a), marked on the curves

    python vertical_impulse.py case_tau0.5_r2.3_mC_et0
    python vertical_impulse.py case_tau0.5_r2.3_mC_et0 case_tau0.5_r2.3_mD_et0_mu0.3 \\
                               --threshold 0.05 --out sec8.png

The command writes ``<out>.png`` and a companion text table without rerunning
the simulation.
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import argparse
import glob
import os
import sys

import numpy as np

try:
    import housestyle  # noqa: F401
except Exception:
    pass
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import thesisfig as tf

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
sys.path.insert(0, HERE)
from snapshots import load_snapshots            # noqa: E402

plt.rcParams.update({"text.color": "#111111", "axes.labelcolor": "#111111",
                     "xtick.color": "#111111", "ytick.color": "#111111",
                     "axes.edgecolor": "#111111", "font.size": 12,
                     "axes.labelsize": 13, "legend.fontsize": 10.5})
NEAR_BLACK = "#111111"


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ loading
def _load(folder):
    cands = [c for c in sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(folder, "snap_tau*.npz")))
             if not c.endswith("snap_tau0.npz")]
    if not cands:
        sys.exit(f"no case archive in {folder}")
    d = load_snapshots(cands[0])
    m = d["meta"]
    d["folder"] = folder
    d["model"] = str(m.get("model", "A"))
    d["e_tan"] = float(m.get("e_tan", 1.0))
    d["mu_pp"] = float(m.get("mu_pp", -1.0))
    d["g_eff"] = float(m.get("g_eff", 0.0)) if float(m.get("wall_friction", 0)) else 0.0
    d["a"] = float(m.get("radius", np.nan))
    d["N"] = int(d["positions"].shape[1])
    d["T"] = float(d["times"][-1] - d["times"][0])
    d["label"] = (d["model"]
                  + (f" e_tan={d['e_tan']:g}" if d["model"] in ("C", "D", "E") else "")
                  + (f" mu={d['mu_pp']:g}" if d["mu_pp"] >= 0 else "")
                  + (f" g={d['g_eff']:g}" if d["g_eff"] > 0 else ""))
    for k in ("collision_Pz_diag", "collision_Uy_diag", "collision_wt", "collision_Pn",
              "collision_t"):
        if k not in d:
            sys.exit(f"{cands[0]} has no {k}: this archive predates the sec. 8 "
                     f"columns or is model A/B -- rerun with the new engine, model C/D/E")
    if len(d["collision_t"]) == 0:
        sys.exit(f"{cands[0]} holds no contacts (collisionless leg?): nothing to analyse")
    # A smooth-law archive stores Pz~ = 0 but still contains the slips needed
    # to evaluate a rough-law prediction on the same contacts. This is a
    # first-order estimate because the trajectories do not include feedback
    # from the rough law.
    d["predicted"] = False
    if np.all(d["collision_Pz_diag"] == 0.0):
        d["predicted"] = True
        d["collision_Pz_diag"] = pz_for_law(d, 0.0, -1.0).astype(np.float32)
        d["label"] += "  [Pz~ PREDICTED from the smooth-law slips for e_tan=0, no cap]"
    return d


def pz_for_law(d, e_tan, mu_pp, m=1.0):
    """Re-evaluate Pz~ from the archived slips for another (e_tan, mu_pp)."""
    wt = d["collision_wt"].astype(float)
    uy = d["collision_Uy_diag"].astype(float)
    Pn = d["collision_Pn"].astype(float)
    uT = np.hypot(wt, uy)
    trial = (m / 7.0) * (1.0 - e_tan) * uT
    if mu_pp >= 0:
        trial = np.minimum(trial, mu_pp * Pn)
    with np.errstate(divide="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
        pz = np.where(uT > 0, -trial * uy / uT, 0.0)
    return pz


# --------------------------------------------------------------- statistics
def exceedance_rate(pz_abs, N, T, lambdas):
    """contacts with |Pz~| > Lambda, per particle per unit time."""
    s = np.sort(pz_abs)
    n_above = len(s) - np.searchsorted(s, lambdas, side="right")
    return n_above / (N * T)


def windowed_rate(t, pz_abs, thr, N, t0, t1, n_win=25):
    edges = np.linspace(t0, t1, n_win + 1)
    hit = t[pz_abs > thr]
    counts, _ = np.histogram(hit, bins=edges)
    return 0.5 * (edges[1:] + edges[:-1]), counts / (N * np.diff(edges))


def summary(d, thr, f):
    if d.get("predicted"):
        note = ("  NOTE: this archive has a SMOOTH contact law (model B): its recorded Pz~ is 0 at every\n"
                "  contact. The statistics below use Pz~ RE-EVALUATED from the archived slips for the\n"
                "  reference rough law e_tan = 0 without cap; the what-if table gives the other laws.\n"
                "  The trajectories are those of the smooth run, so this is a prediction, not a measurement.")
        print(note); f.write(note + "\n")
    pz = d["collision_Pz_diag"].astype(float)
    uy = d["collision_Uy_diag"].astype(float)
    wt = d["collision_wt"].astype(float)
    Pn = d["collision_Pn"].astype(float)
    uT = np.hypot(wt, uy)
    n = len(pz); N, T = d["N"], d["T"]
    rough = uT > 0
    lines = [f"\nsec. 8 diagnostic vertical impulse -- {d['label']}  ({d['folder']})",
             "-" * 72,
             f"  contacts {n}  over T = {T:g}, N = {N}:  {n / (N * T):.4e} per particle per time",
             f"  |Pz~| (per unit mass = vertical velocity jump on the particle that gets the upward side):",
             f"     mean {np.mean(np.abs(pz)):.4e}   median {np.median(np.abs(pz)):.4e}   "
             f"p90 {np.percentile(np.abs(pz), 90):.4e}   p99 {np.percentile(np.abs(pz), 99):.4e}   "
             f"max {np.max(np.abs(pz)):.4e}",
             f"  |Pz~| / P_perp:  median {np.median(np.abs(pz) / np.maximum(Pn, 1e-30)):.3f}   "
             f"p95 {np.percentile(np.abs(pz) / np.maximum(Pn, 1e-30), 95):.3f}",
             f"  vertical share of the slip |U~_y|/|U~_T|:  median "
             f"{np.median(np.abs(uy[rough]) / uT[rough]) if rough.any() else np.nan:.3f}   "
             f"(1 = slip purely from the in-plane spins)"]
    if d["mu_pp"] >= 0:
        capped = np.mean((d["mu_pp"] * Pn) < (1 - d["e_tan"]) / 7.0 * uT - 1e-12) if n else 0.0
        lines.append(f"  3-D cap active on {capped:.1%} of contacts (mu_pp = {d['mu_pp']:g})")
    if thr is not None:
        lines.append(f"  threshold Lambda = {thr:g}:  {np.mean(np.abs(pz) > thr):.2%} of contacts, "
                     f"rate {np.sum(np.abs(pz) > thr) / (N * T):.4e} per particle per time")
    if d["g_eff"] > 0 and np.isfinite(d["a"]):
        lam_a = np.sqrt(2 * d["g_eff"] * d["a"])
        lines.append(f"  reference Lambda_a = sqrt(2 g_eff a) = {lam_a:.4e} (hop of one radius): "
                     f"{np.mean(np.abs(pz) > lam_a):.2%} of contacts, rate "
                     f"{np.sum(np.abs(pz) > lam_a) / (N * T):.4e} per particle per time")
    # what-if table over the contact law
    lines.append("  rate above Lambda for other contact laws, from the archived slips "
                 f"(Lambda = {thr if thr is not None else np.percentile(np.abs(pz), 90):.3e}):")
    lam = thr if thr is not None else float(np.percentile(np.abs(pz), 90))
    es = (-1.0, -0.5, 0.0, 0.5)
    mus = (-1.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5)
    lines.append("     e_par \\ mu_pp   " + "".join(f"{('none' if m_ < 0 else f'{m_:g}'):>12}" for m_ in mus))
    for e in es:
        row = [f"{exceedance_rate(np.abs(pz_for_law(d, e, m_)), N, T, np.array([lam]))[0]:12.3e}"
               for m_ in mus]
        lines.append(f"     {e:>+6.1f}          " + "".join(row))
    txt = "\n".join(lines)
    print(txt)
    f.write(txt + "\n")


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ figure
def _lam_default(cases, thr):
    if thr is not None:
        return float(thr)
    pz = np.abs(cases[0]["collision_Pz_diag"].astype(float))
    return float(np.percentile(pz[pz > 0], 90)) if np.any(pz > 0) else 0.0


def _caption(cases, thr_c):
    d = cases[0]
    n = len(d["collision_t"]); N, T = d["N"], d["T"]
    pz = np.abs(d["collision_Pz_diag"].astype(float))
    Pn = d["collision_Pn"].astype(float)
    ratio = np.median(pz[Pn > 0] / Pn[Pn > 0]) if np.any(Pn > 0) else np.nan
    rate = exceedance_rate(pz, N, T, np.array([thr_c]))[0]
    law = (f"model {d['model']}" + (rf", $e_\parallel = {d['e_tan']:g}$" if d["model"] in ("C", "D", "E") else "")
           + (rf", $\mu_{{pp}} = {d['mu_pp']:g}$" if d["mu_pp"] >= 0 else ""))
    pred = (r" The archive has a smooth contact law, so $\tilde P_z$ is re-evaluated from the stored "
            r"slips for the reference law $e_\parallel = 0$ without cap and is a prediction, not a measurement."
            if d.get("predicted") else "")
    return (r"\caption{Diagnostic vertical impulse of the collisions (\S 8 of the notes), " + law
            + rf", $N = {N:,}$, {n:,} contacts over $T = {T:g}$. "
            + r"(a) Probability that the hypothetical vertical velocity jump $|\tilde P_z|/m$ exceeds $\Lambda$; "
            + r"(b) the rate of contacts exceeding $\Lambda$, per particle and unit time; "
            + rf"(c) that rate against time for $\Lambda = {tf.fmt(thr_c)}$ (mean {tf.fmt(rate)}); "
            + r"(d) the fraction of contacts whose ratio $|\tilde P_z|/P_\perp$ exceeds $r$ (median ratio "
            + (f"{ratio:.2f}" if np.isfinite(ratio) else "--") + r"); the dashed line marks equality with the normal "
            + r"impulse and the dotted line the Coulomb cap $\mu_{pp}$ where one is present. "
            + r"$\tilde P_z$ is recorded at every contact and never applied." + pred + "}\n")


def _mathlabel(d):
    lab = f"model {d['model']}"
    if d["model"] in ("C", "D", "E"):
        lab += rf", $e_\parallel$ = {d['e_tan']:g}"
    if d["mu_pp"] >= 0:
        lab += rf", $\mu_{{pp}}$ = {d['mu_pp']:g}"
    if d["g_eff"] > 0:
        lab += rf", $\mu_w g_{{\rm eff}}$ = {float(d['meta'].get('mu_w', 0)) * d['g_eff']:g}"
    return lab


def figure(cases, thr, out):
    import thesisfig as tf_
    from matplotlib.ticker import NullFormatter, LogLocator
    fig, (ax_a, ax_b, ax_c, ax_d) = tf_.figure_2x2()
    thr_c = _lam_default(cases, thr)
    first = cases[0]
    rate_floor = 0.5 / (first["N"] * first["T"])          # half a contact
    for c, d in enumerate(cases):
        col = tf_.CYCLE[c % len(tf_.CYCLE)]
        lab = _mathlabel(d)
        pz = np.abs(d["collision_Pz_diag"].astype(float))
        Pn = d["collision_Pn"].astype(float)
        N, T = d["N"], d["T"]
        pos = pz[pz > 0]
        if pos.size == 0:
            continue
        lo, hi = np.percentile(pos, 0.5), pos.max()
        lam = np.logspace(np.log10(lo), np.log10(hi), 160)
        # (a) complementary distribution
        ax_a.loglog(lam, 1.0 - np.searchsorted(np.sort(pz), lam, side="right") / len(pz),
                    color=col, lw=2.0, zorder=4, label=lab)
        # (b) exceedance rate against the threshold
        ax_b.loglog(lam, np.maximum(exceedance_rate(pz, N, T, lam), rate_floor),
                    color=col, lw=2.0, zorder=4, label=lab)
        # (c) rate above the chosen threshold against time
        t = d["collision_t"].astype(float)
        tc, rate = windowed_rate(t, pz, thr_c, N, float(d["times"][0]), float(d["times"][-1]))
        ax_c.plot(tc, rate, color=col, lw=2.0, zorder=4, label=lab)
        ax_c.axhline(rate.mean(), color=col, ls=":", lw=1.0, zorder=3)
        # (d) hypothetical vertical impulse relative to the normal impulse:
        #     fraction of contacts with |Pz~|/P_perp above r (a delta at the
        #     cap becomes a vertical drop, not a spike)
        ok = (Pn > 0) & (pz > 0)
        if np.any(ok):
            ratio = np.sort(pz[ok] / Pn[ok])
            rr = np.logspace(np.log10(ratio[0]) - 0.05, np.log10(max(ratio[-1], 1.5)) + 0.05, 200)
            ax_d.semilogx(rr, 1.0 - np.searchsorted(ratio, rr, side="right") / len(ratio),
                          color=col, lw=2.0, zorder=4, label=lab)
        if d["g_eff"] > 0 and np.isfinite(d["a"]):
            lam_a = np.sqrt(2 * d["g_eff"] * d["a"])
            for a_ in (ax_a, ax_b):
                a_.axvline(lam_a, color=col, ls=":", lw=1.0, zorder=2,
                           label=r"$\Lambda_a = \sqrt{2 g_{\rm eff} a}$" if c == 0 else None)
        if d["mu_pp"] >= 0:
            ax_d.axvline(d["mu_pp"], color=col, ls=":", lw=1.0, zorder=2)
            tf_.note(ax_d, 0.03, 0.06 + 0.08 * c, rf"dotted: cap $\mu_{{pp}}$ = {d['mu_pp']:g}",
                     ha="left", va="bottom", color=col)
    for a_ in (ax_a, ax_b):
        a_.axvline(thr_c, color=tf_.BLUE, ls="-.", lw=1.1, zorder=2,
                   label=rf"$\Lambda$ = {thr_c:.3g}")
    ax_d.axvline(1.0, color=tf_.REF, ls="--", lw=1.0, zorder=2)
    tf_.note(ax_d, 0.97, 0.9, r"dashed: $|\tilde P_z| = P_\perp$", ha="right", va="top")
    ax_d.set_ylim(0, 1.05)

    tf_.title(ax_a, "Distribution of the vertical impulse")
    tf_.labels(ax_a, r"threshold  $\Lambda$", r"$P\,(|\tilde P_z|/m > \Lambda)$")
    tf_.legend_below(ax_a, ncol=3)
    tf_.title(ax_b, "Exceedance rate against the threshold")
    tf_.labels(ax_b, r"threshold  $\Lambda$", "rate above $\\Lambda$\n[per particle and time]")
    ax_b.set_ylim(bottom=rate_floor)
    for a_ in (ax_a, ax_b):                       # clean log axes: majors only
        a_.xaxis.set_major_locator(LogLocator(base=10, numticks=6))
        a_.xaxis.set_minor_formatter(NullFormatter())
        a_.yaxis.set_minor_formatter(NullFormatter())
    tf_.title(ax_c, "Exceedance rate against time")
    tf_.labels(ax_c, r"time  $t$", rf"rate above $\Lambda$ = {thr_c:.3g}")
    ax_c.set_ylim(bottom=0)
    tf_.title(ax_d, "Ratio to the normal impulse")
    tf_.labels(ax_d, r"$r = |\tilde P_z| / P_\perp$", r"fraction of contacts with ratio $> r$")
    ax_d.xaxis.set_minor_formatter(NullFormatter())
    for k, a_ in enumerate((ax_a, ax_b, ax_c, ax_d)):
        tf_.tag(a_, "abcd"[k])
    tau = first["meta"].get("tau", "?") if isinstance(first.get("meta"), dict) else first.get("tau", "?")
    law = (_mathlabel(first) + (r";  predicted from the smooth-law slips for $e_\parallel = 0$, no cap"
                                if first.get("predicted") else ""))
