    tf_.heading(fig, rf"Diagnostic vertical impulse of collisions    ($\tau_p$ = {tau})",
                rf"$\tilde P_z = \tilde{{\mathbf{{P}}}}_T\cdot\mathbf{{e}}_y$, recorded at every contact, never applied"
                rf"   ({law};  $N$ = {first['N']:,},  {len(first['collision_t']):,} contacts)")
    tf_.save(fig, out, caption=_caption(cases, thr_c))
    plt.close(fig)


def main(argv=None):
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__,
                                 formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument("cases", nargs="+")
    ap.add_argument("--threshold", type=float, default=None,
                    help="Lambda for the time series and the what-if table "
                         "(default: the 90th percentile of |Pz~|)")
    ap.add_argument("--out")
    a = ap.parse_args(argv)
    cases = [_load(c) for c in a.cases]
    out = a.out or f"vertical_impulse_{os.path.basename(a.cases[0])}.png"
    with open(os.path.splitext(out)[0] + ".txt", "w") as f:
        for d in cases:
            summary(d, a.threshold, f)
    figure(cases, a.threshold, out)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _rc = main()
    if _rc:            # non-zero only on failure; no SystemExit noise under Spyder
        sys.exit(_rc)

05

Figures and outputs

Snapshots, analysis figures, consistent styling and specialised plots.

figures.pyComparison figures for clustering and collective behaviourSee code ↗

The collision kernel is displayed beside its accumulation and transport factors.

"""Figures for the DNS slab clustering study.

The comparison plots include a Poisson reference, the tracer baseline and
frame-to-frame uncertainty over the steady portion of each run.  This keeps
the inertial signal separate from the clustering introduced by the slice
itself.

Typical use::

    from figures import *
    fig_snapshot(p, L, W, "snapshot.png")
    fig_stokes_sweep(rows, null, "sweep.png")
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from matplotlib.gridspec import GridSpec

import clustering as cl

NAVY = "#1F3864"
# Keep the palette and Matplotlib settings in the shared style module.
import figstyle as fs

BLUE, RED, GREEN, GREY, ORANGE = fs.BLUE, fs.RED, fs.GREEN, fs.GREY, fs.ORANGE
NAVY = fs.NAVY


def _save(fig, path):
    """Vector PDF plus PNG -- see figstyle.save."""
    if path:
        fs.save(fig, path)
    return fig


# ------------------------------------------------------------- snapshot
def fig_snapshot(p, L, W, path=None, eps_factor=1.8, min_samples=10,
                 title=None, max_show=40000):
    """Particle positions, raw and DBSCAN-segmented, with the diagnostics."""
    db = cl.dbscan_clusters(p, L, W, eps_factor, min_samples)
    lab = db["labels"]
    q = np.column_stack([np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W)])
    s = max(0.4, min(4.0, 4000.0 / max(len(p), 1)))

    # Derive the height from the domain aspect ratio.
    fig = plt.figure(figsize=fs.panel_figsize(L, W, ncols=2, side_ratio=0.72,
                                              extra_h=0.65))
    gs = GridSpec(1, 3, width_ratios=[1, 1, 0.72], wspace=0.30)

    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[0])
    ax.scatter(q[:, 0], q[:, 1], s=s, c="k", linewidths=0)
    ax.set_title("particle positions")

    ax2 = fig.add_subplot(gs[1])
    m = lab == -1
    ax2.scatter(q[m, 0], q[m, 1], s=s, c="#B0B0B0", linewidths=0)
    cmap = plt.get_cmap("tab10")
    for i, cid in enumerate(db["ids"][:10]):
        k = lab == cid
        ax2.scatter(q[k, 0], q[k, 1], s=s * 1.6, color=cmap(i % 10), linewidths=0)
    for i, cid in enumerate(db["ids"][10:]):
        k = lab == cid
        ax2.scatter(q[k, 0], q[k, 1], s=s * 1.2, color="#666666", linewidths=0)
    ax2.set_title(f"DBSCAN: {db['n_clusters']} clusters, "
                  f"{db['noise_pct']:.0f}% noise")

    for a in (ax, ax2):
        a.set_xlim(0, L); a.set_ylim(0, W); a.set_aspect("equal")
        a.set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["x"]); a.set_ylabel(fs.SYMBOL["z"])

    ax3 = fig.add_subplot(gs[2]); ax3.axis("off")
    v16, o16 = cl.vmr(p, L, W, 16)
    cd = cl.correlation_dimension(p, L, W)
    # Use opposing alignment for labels and values so long entries stay clear.
    lines = [("N", f"{len(p):,}"),
             (r"$\varepsilon$", f"{db['eps']:.4g}"),
             (r"VMR$_{16}$", f"{v16:.3f}"),
             ("occupancy", f"{o16:.1f}%"),
             (r"$D_2$", f"{cd['D2']:.3f}"),
             (r"   $R^2$", f"{cd['r2']:.2f}"),
             ("clusters", f"{db['n_clusters']}"),
             ("noise", f"{db['noise_pct']:.1f}%"),
             ("largest", f"{db['largest_pct']:.1f}%"),
             ("elongation", f"{db['median_elongation']:.2f}:1"),
             ("percolates", "YES" if db["percolates"] else "no")]
    ax3.text(0, 1.0, "DIAGNOSTICS", fontsize=9.5, weight="bold", va="top")
    dy = 0.085
    for i, (k, val) in enumerate(lines):
        y = 0.90 - dy * i
        ax3.text(0.0, y, k, fontsize=8.5, color="#333333", va="top",
                 ha="left")
        ax3.text(1.0, y, val, fontsize=8.5, weight="bold", va="top",
                 ha="right")
    ax3.set_xlim(0, 1); ax3.set_ylim(0, 1)
    if db["percolates"]:
        ax3.text(0, 0.90 - dy * len(lines) - 0.03,
                 "cluster count is\nconnectivity, not\nclustering",
                 fontsize=7, color=RED, style="italic", va="top",
                 ha="left", wrap=True)
    if title:
        fig.suptitle(title, fontsize=10, y=0.99)
    return _save(fig, path)


# --------------------------------------------------------- Stokes sweep
def fig_tau_sweep(rows, null, path=None, key="vmr_16"):
    """VMR, D2, clusters and collisions against Stokes number."""
    st = np.array([r["tau"] for r in rows])
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(fs.WIDE, 5.0))

    def band(a, k, lbl, col):
        y = np.array([r[k] for r in rows])
        e = np.array([r.get(k + "_sd", 0.0) for r in rows])
        a.errorbar(st, y, yerr=e, marker="o", color=col, capsize=3, lw=1.6)
        if k in null:
            a.axhline(null[k], color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2,
                      label="Poisson null")
        base = next((r for r in rows if r["tau"] == 0), None)
        if base is not None:
            a.axhline(base[k], color=GREEN, ls=":", lw=1.4,
                      label="tracer baseline")
        a.set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["tau"]); a.set_ylabel(fs.sym(key, lbl))
        a.set_xscale("symlog", linthresh=0.05)
        a.legend(frameon=False, fontsize=8)

    band(ax[0, 0], key, "VMR (16$\\times$16)", BLUE)
    band(ax[0, 1], "D2", "correlation dimension $D_2$", RED)
    band(ax[1, 0], "n_clusters", "DBSCAN clusters", NAVY)
    coll = np.array([r.get("collisions", 0) for r in rows], float)
    ax[1, 1].plot(st, np.maximum(coll, 0.5), "o-", color=ORANGE, lw=1.6)
    ax[1, 1].set_yscale("log"); ax[1, 1].set_xscale("symlog", linthresh=0.05)
    ax[1, 1].set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["tau"])
    ax[1, 1].set_ylabel("collisions")

    fs.panels(ax)
    fig.align_ylabels(ax)
    fig.tight_layout()
    return _save(fig, path)


def fig_excess(rows, path=None, key="vmr_16"):
    """
    The inertial signal: excess over the tracer baseline. This is the figure
    that separates inertial preferential concentration from the clustering the
    2D slice produces on its own.
    """
    base = next((r for r in rows if r["tau"] == 0), None)
    if base is None:
        raise ValueError("no tau = 0 tracer run in `rows`")
    st = np.array([r["tau"] for r in rows])
    y = np.array([r[key] - base[key] for r in rows])
    e = np.array([np.hypot(r.get(key + "_sd", 0), base.get(key + "_sd", 0))
                  for r in rows])
    fig, a = plt.subplots(figsize=(fs.COL, fs.COL * 0.78))
    a.axhline(0, color=GREY, lw=1.2)
    a.errorbar(st, y, yerr=e, marker="o", color=BLUE, capsize=3, lw=1.8)
    a.fill_between(st, -e, e, color=GREY, alpha=0.15,
                   label="not significant")
    a.set_xscale("symlog", linthresh=0.05)
    a.set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["tau"])
    a.set_ylabel(f"$\\Delta$ {key} over tracer baseline")
    a.set_title("excess over tracer")
    a.legend(frameon=False)
    fig.tight_layout()
    return _save(fig, path)


# ----------------------------------------------------- scale-resolved
def fig_scale_resolved(p, L, W, path=None, sigma=None, p_null=None):
    """g(r), D2 scaling, VMR vs bin size, and the Voronoi PDF."""
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(fs.WIDE, 5.0))

    rd = cl.radial_distribution(p, L, W)
    ax[0, 0].loglog(rd["r"], np.maximum(rd["g"], 1e-3), color=BLUE, lw=1.8)
    ax[0, 0].axhline(1.0, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2, label="uniform")
    if sigma:
        ax[0, 0].axvline(sigma, color=RED, ls=":", lw=1.4,
                         label="contact $\\sigma$")
    ax[0, 0].set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["r"]); ax[0, 0].set_ylabel("$g(r)$")
    ax[0, 0].set_title("radial distribution"); ax[0, 0].legend(frameon=False)

    cd = cl.correlation_dimension(p, L, W)
    ok = cd["C"] > 0
    ax[0, 1].loglog(cd["r"][ok], cd["C"][ok], "o", ms=4, color=RED)
    if np.isfinite(cd["D2"]):
        rr = cd["r"][ok]
        ax[0, 1].loglog(rr, cd["C"][ok][0] * (rr / rr[0]) ** cd["D2"],
                        "-", color=NAVY, lw=1.4,
                        label=f"$D_2$={cd['D2']:.2f} ($R^2$={cd['r2']:.2f})")
        ax[0, 1].loglog(rr, cd["C"][ok][0] * (rr / rr[0]) ** 2, "--",
                        color=GREY, lw=1.2, label="$D_2=2$ (uniform)")
    ax[0, 1].set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["r"]); ax[0, 1].set_ylabel("$C(r)$")
    ax[0, 1].set_title("correlation integral"); ax[0, 1].legend(frameon=False)

    ms = cl.vmr_multiscale(p, L, W)
    ax[1, 0].semilogx(ms["bin_size"], ms["vmr"], "o-", color=BLUE, lw=1.7)
    ax[1, 0].axhline(1.0, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2, label="Poisson")
    if p_null is not None:
        mn = cl.vmr_multiscale(p_null, L, W)
        ax[1, 0].semilogx(mn["bin_size"], mn["vmr"], "s--", color=GREY,
                          lw=1.3, ms=4, label="random null")
    ax[1, 0].set_xlabel("bin size")
    ax[1, 0].set_ylabel("variance-to-mean ratio")
    ax[1, 0].set_title("VMR vs scale"); ax[1, 0].legend(frameon=False)

    try:
        vo = cl.voronoi_analysis(p, L, W)
        ax[1, 1].semilogy(vo["bins"], np.maximum(vo["pdf"], 1e-4),
                          color=BLUE, lw=1.8, label="measured")
        ax[1, 1].semilogy(vo["bins"], np.maximum(vo["poisson_pdf"], 1e-4),
                          "--", color=GREY, lw=1.4, label="Poisson (Gamma 3.5)")
        ax[1, 1].set_title(f"Voronoi cell areas  "
                           f"$\\sigma/\\sigma_P$ = {vo['sigma_ratio']:.2f}")
        ax[1, 1].set_xlabel("$V/\\langle V\\rangle$")
        ax[1, 1].set_ylabel("PDF"); ax[1, 1].set_ylim(1e-3, 3)
        ax[1, 1].legend(frameon=False)
    except Exception as e:
        ax[1, 1].text(0.5, 0.5, f"Voronoi unavailable\n{e}", ha="center",
                      transform=ax[1, 1].transAxes, fontsize=9)

    fs.panels(ax)
    fig.align_ylabels(ax)
    fig.tight_layout()
    return _save(fig, path)


# ------------------------------------------------------- convergence
def fig_convergence(res, tau, L, W, path=None, n_tau=0.0, max_frames=60):
    """
    Diagnostics over the complete time window.

    n_tau > 0 shades the first n_tau*tau_p as an initialisation transient, for
    reference only; the curve itself always covers every frame. This is the
    figure that shows whether the run reached a steady state, so quote nothing
    without it.
    """
    times = np.asarray(res["event_times"], float)
    hist = res["position_history"]
    sel = np.linspace(0, len(times) - 1,
                      min(max_frames, len(times))).astype(int)
    tv = times[sel]
    v16 = [cl.vmr(np.asarray(hist[i]), L, W, 16)[0] for i in sel]
    d2 = [cl.correlation_dimension(np.asarray(hist[i]), L, W)["D2"] for i in sel]

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(2, 1, figsize=(fs.COL * 1.5, 3.6), sharex=True)
    tr = n_tau * float(tau)
    for a, y, lbl, col in ((ax[0], v16, "VMR (16$\\times$16)", BLUE),
                           (ax[1], d2, "$D_2$", RED)):
        a.plot(tv, y, "-", color=col, lw=1.5)
        if tr > 0:
            a.axvspan(times[0], min(tr, times[-1]), color=GREY, alpha=0.18)
        a.set_ylabel(lbl)
    ax[1].axhline(2.0, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2)
    ax[1].set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["t"])
    ax[0].set_title(f"convergence, $\\tau_p$ = {tau:g}   (full window)"
                    + ("   shaded: 5$\\tau_p$" if tr > 0 else ""),
                    fontsize=11, color=NAVY)
    # last third vs middle third
    n = len(tv)
    mid, last = slice(n // 3, 2 * n // 3), slice(2 * n // 3, None)
    ax[0].text(0.98, 0.05,
               f"middle third {np.nanmean(np.array(v16)[mid]):.2f} | "
               f"last third {np.nanmean(np.array(v16)[last]):.2f}",
               transform=ax[0].transAxes, ha="right", fontsize=9, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout()
    return _save(fig, path)


# ---------------------------------------------------------- mechanism
def fig_conditional(p, L, W, fields, path=None):
    """
    Density conditioned on local flow quantities. `fields` is a dict of
    name -> callable(x, y). Rising density with strain, or falling with
    |vorticity|, is the preferential-concentration signature.
    """
    n = len(fields)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, n, figsize=(2.4 * n, 2.3), squeeze=False)
    for i, (name, fn) in enumerate(fields.items()):
        c = cl.conditional_density(p, L, W, fn)
        a = ax[0, i]
        a.errorbar(c["field"], c["density"], yerr=c["err"], marker="o",
                   color=BLUE, capsize=3, lw=1.6)
        a.axhline(1.0, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2, label="uniform")
        a.set_xlabel(name); a.set_ylabel("$n/\\langle n\\rangle$")
        a.legend(frameon=False)
    fs.panels(ax[0])
    fig.suptitle("",
                 fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout()
    return _save(fig, path)


# ------------------------------------------------------- regime table
def fig_regime_table(table, path=None):
    """
    2x2 inertia x collisions as a grouped bar chart.
    `table` : dict {(inertia_label, collision_label): {"vmr_16":..,"D2":..}}
    """
    labels = list(table.keys())
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(11, 4.4))
    x = np.arange(len(labels))
    for a, k, lbl, col in ((ax[0], "vmr_16", "VMR (16$\\times$16)", BLUE),
                           (ax[1], "D2", "$D_2$", RED)):
        a.bar(x, [table[l][k] for l in labels], color=col, alpha=0.85)
        a.set_xticks(x)
        a.set_xticklabels(["\n".join(l) if isinstance(l, tuple) else str(l)
                           for l in labels], fontsize=8.5)
        a.set_ylabel(lbl)
        a.axhline(1.0 if k == "vmr_16" else 2.0, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2,
                  label="random")
        a.legend(frameon=False)
    fig.suptitle("inertia $\\times$ collisions regime table",
                 fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=(0, 0, 1, 0.94))
    return _save(fig, path)


# ------------------------------------------------- collective behaviour
def fig_collective(p, v, L, W, path=None, sigma=None, p_tracer=None,
                   v_tracer=None, ref_label="tracer"):
    """
    Collective-behaviour panel: relative velocity vs separation, velocity
    correlation, the caustic indicator, and pair-approach statistics.

    Pass the tracer run (p_tracer, v_tracer) as the reference. Several of these
    diagnostics only mean something as a difference from it -- the tracer is
    caustic-free by construction and its relative velocity vanishes linearly
    with separation, providing the natural baseline.
    """
    import collective as co
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(fs.WIDE, 5.0))

    rv = co.relative_velocity_stats(p, v, L, W)
    ok = np.isfinite(rv["r"])
    ax[0, 0].loglog(rv["r"][ok], rv["w_abs"][ok], "o-", color=BLUE, lw=1.7,
                    label="inertial")
    if p_tracer is not None:
        rt = co.relative_velocity_stats(p_tracer, v_tracer, L, W)
        okt = np.isfinite(rt["r"])
        ax[0, 0].loglog(rt["r"][okt], rt["w_abs"][okt], "s--", color=GREEN,
                        lw=1.4, ms=4, label=ref_label)
    r = rv["r"][ok]
    if len(r):
        ax[0, 0].loglog(r, rv["w_abs"][ok][0] * r / r[0], ":", color=GREY,
                        lw=1.2, label="$\\propto r$ (smooth)")
    if sigma:
        ax[0, 0].axvline(sigma, color=RED, ls=":", lw=1.4)
    ax[0, 0].set_xlabel("separation $r$")
    ax[0, 0].set_ylabel("$\\langle|w_r|\\rangle$")
    ax[0, 0].set_title("radial relative velocity")
    ax[0, 0].legend(frameon=False)

    ax[0, 1].semilogx(rv["r"][ok], rv["frac_approach"][ok], "o-", color=NAVY,
                      lw=1.7)
    ax[0, 1].axhline(0.5, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2, label="unbiased")
    ax[0, 1].set_xlabel("separation $r$")
    ax[0, 1].set_ylabel("fraction approaching")
    ax[0, 1].set_title("approach bias")
    ax[0, 1].legend(frameon=False)

    vc = co.velocity_correlation(p, v, L, W)
    ax[1, 0].plot(vc["r"], vc["corr"], "-", color=BLUE, lw=1.8,
                  label="inertial")
    if p_tracer is not None:
        vt = co.velocity_correlation(p_tracer, v_tracer, L, W)
        ax[1, 0].plot(vt["r"], vt["corr"], "--", color=GREEN, lw=1.5,
                      label=ref_label)
    ax[1, 0].axhline(0, color=GREY, lw=1.0)
    ax[1, 0].set_xlabel("separation $r$")
    ax[1, 0].set_ylabel("$\\langle v_i\\cdot v_j\\rangle/\\langle|v|^2\\rangle$")
    ax[1, 0].set_title("velocity correlation")
    ax[1, 0].legend(frameon=False)

    ij, d, rr = co._pairs(p, L, W, 0.05 * min(L, W))
    if len(ij) > 20:
        rh = d / rr[:, None]
        w = np.abs(np.sum((v[ij[:, 0]] - v[ij[:, 1]]) * rh, axis=1))
        z = w / np.maximum(rr, 1e-15)
        ax[1, 1].hist(np.log10(z[z > 0]), bins=50, density=True, color=BLUE,
                      alpha=0.65, label="inertial")
        if p_tracer is not None:
            it, dt_, rt_ = co._pairs(p_tracer, L, W, 0.05 * min(L, W))
            if len(it) > 20:
                rht = dt_ / rt_[:, None]
                wt = np.abs(np.sum((v_tracer[it[:, 0]] - v_tracer[it[:, 1]])
                                   * rht, axis=1))
                zt = wt / np.maximum(rt_, 1e-15)
                ax[1, 1].hist(np.log10(zt[zt > 0]), bins=50, density=True,
                              histtype="step", color=GREEN, lw=1.6,
                              label=f"{ref_label} (caustic-free)")
        ax[1, 1].set_xlabel("$\\log_{10}(|w_r|/r)$")
        ax[1, 1].set_ylabel("PDF")
        ax[1, 1].set_title("stretching rate: a heavy tail = caustics")
        ax[1, 1].legend(frameon=False)

    fig.suptitle("collective behaviour", fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=(0, 0, 1, 0.955))
    return _save(fig, path)


def fig_collision_kernel(rows, path=None):
    """
    The collision kernel split into its two factors:
        Gamma = n sigma * g(sigma) * <|w_r|>
                        accumulation   transport
    Plotting them separately says WHY the collision rate changed with Stokes
    number, which the total rate alone cannot.
    """
    st = np.array([r["tau"] for r in rows])
    g = np.array([r.get("g_contact", np.nan) for r in rows], float)
    w = np.array([r.get("w_abs_contact", np.nan) for r in rows], float)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=(fs.WIDE, 2.2))
    for a, y, lbl, col in ((ax[0], g, "$g(\\sigma)$  (accumulation)", BLUE),
                           (ax[1], w, "$\\langle|w_r|\\rangle$  (transport)", RED),
                           (ax[2], g * w, "$g\\cdot\\langle|w_r|\\rangle$  (kernel)", NAVY)):
        a.plot(st, y, "o-", color=col, lw=1.8)
        a.set_xscale("symlog", linthresh=0.05)
        a.set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["tau"]); a.set_ylabel(lbl)
    ax[0].axhline(1.0, color=GREY, ls="--", label="uniform")
    ax[0].legend()
    fs.panels(ax)
    fig.tight_layout()
    return _save(fig, path)


# ----------------------------------------------------- angular structure
def fig_angular(p, v, L, W, path=None, p_tracer=None, v_tracer=None,
                ref_label="tracer"):
    """
    Angular structure: are clusters elongated, and which way do they point?

    In a wall-bounded flow this should NOT be isotropic -- streaks are
    elongated streamwise, so pair separations and cluster axes should both
    prefer theta = 0. A flat g(theta) would mean the streak structure is not
    imprinting on the particles.
    """
    import collective as co
    fig = plt.figure(figsize=(12.5, 8.4))
    gs = GridSpec(2, 3, figure=fig, hspace=0.52, wspace=0.34,
                  top=0.90, bottom=0.07)

    # 1. polar plot of pair-separation angles
    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 0], projection="polar")
    ap = co.angular_pair_distribution(p, L, W)
    if len(ap["theta"]):
        th = np.concatenate([ap["theta"], ap["theta"] + np.pi])
        g = np.concatenate([ap["g"], ap["g"]])
        ax.plot(np.append(th, th[0]), np.append(g, g[0]), color=BLUE, lw=1.8)
        ax.plot(np.linspace(0, 2 * np.pi, 100), np.ones(100), "--",
                color=GREY, lw=1.2)
    # Lift the title above the 90-degree tick and move radial labels away from
    # the streamwise lobe.
    ax.set_title(f"pair separation angle\n$S$ = {ap.get('nematic', np.nan):.3f} "
                 f"at {np.degrees(ap.get('nematic_angle', np.nan)):.0f}$^\\circ$",
                 fontsize=10, pad=14)
    ax.set_thetagrids([0, 45, 90, 135, 180, 225, 270, 315])
    ax.set_rlabel_position(67.5)
    ax.tick_params(labelsize=7.5)

    # 2. anisotropy vs scale
    ax2 = fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 1])
    an = co.anisotropy_vs_scale(p, L, W)
    if len(an["r"]):
        ax2.semilogx(an["r"], an["nematic"], "o-", color=BLUE, lw=1.7,
                     label="inertial")
    if p_tracer is not None:
        at = co.anisotropy_vs_scale(p_tracer, L, W)
        if len(at["r"]):
            ax2.semilogx(at["r"], at["nematic"], "s--", color=GREEN, lw=1.4,
                         ms=4, label=ref_label)
    ax2.axhline(0, color=GREY, lw=1.0)
    ax2.set_xlabel("separation $r$"); ax2.set_ylabel("nematic order $S$")
    ax2.set_title("anisotropy vs scale", fontsize=10)
    ax2.legend(frameon=False, fontsize=8)

    # 3. alignment angle vs scale
    ax3 = fig.add_subplot(gs[0, 2])
    if len(an["r"]):
        ax3.semilogx(an["r"], np.degrees(an["angle"]), "o-", color=NAVY, lw=1.7)
    ax3.axhline(0, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.2, label="streamwise")
    ax3.axhline(90, color=RED, ls=":", lw=1.2, label="spanwise")
    ax3.set_xlabel("separation $r$"); ax3.set_ylabel("alignment angle [deg]")
    ax3.set_ylim(-95, 95); ax3.set_title("alignment direction", fontsize=10)
    ax3.legend(frameon=False, fontsize=8)

    # 4. cluster orientations
    ax4 = fig.add_subplot(gs[1, 0], projection="polar")
    cor = co.cluster_orientation(p, L, W)
    if len(cor.get("angles", [])):
        a2 = np.concatenate([cor["angles"], cor["angles"] + np.pi])
        w2 = np.concatenate([cor["sizes"], cor["sizes"]])
        ax4.hist(a2, bins=24, weights=w2, color=ORANGE, alpha=0.75)
    ax4.set_title(f"cluster axes ({cor.get('n_clusters', 0)} clusters)\n"
                  f"$S$ = {cor.get('nematic', np.nan):.3f}, elong "
                  f"{cor.get('median_elongation', np.nan):.1f}:1",
                  fontsize=10, pad=14)
    ax4.set_rlabel_position(67.5)
    ax4.tick_params(labelsize=7.5)

    # 5. angular relative velocity
    ax5 = fig.add_subplot(gs[1, 1])
    ar = co.angular_relative_velocity(p, v, L, W)
    if len(ar["theta"]):
        ax5.plot(np.degrees(ar["theta"]), ar["w_mean"], "o-", color=RED, lw=1.7)
        ax5.axhline(0, color=GREY, lw=1.0)
    ax5.set_xlabel("separation angle [deg]")
    ax5.set_ylabel("$\\langle w_r\\rangle$")
    ax5.set_title("approach rate vs direction\n(negative = compressing)",
                  fontsize=10)

    # 6. velocity direction
    ax6 = fig.add_subplot(gs[1, 2], projection="polar")
    thv = np.arctan2(v[:, 1], v[:, 0])
    ax6.hist(np.mod(thv, 2 * np.pi), bins=36, color=BLUE, alpha=0.7)
    va = co.velocity_angle_stats(v)
    ax6.set_title(f"velocity directions\npolar {va['polar']:.2f}, "
                  f"fluct {va['polar_fluct']:.2f}", fontsize=10, pad=14)
    ax6.set_rlabel_position(67.5)
    ax6.tick_params(labelsize=7.5)

    fig.suptitle("angular structure", fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY, y=0.985)
    return _save(fig, path)
viz.pyFlow-field, particle-density and animation utilitiesSee code ↗

A periodic roll removes the measured downstream translation from an animation.

"""Visualisation tools for the DNS slab study.

The module covers turbulent structures, streak statistics, particle density
and animations of each:

    from viz import *
    flow_structures(out="structures.png")
    animate_flow(out="flow.mp4")
    animate_streaks(out="streaks.mp4", comoving=True)
    particle_density_figure("snap_tau0.npz", "snap_tau0.05.npz", out="density.png")
    animate_density_pair("snap_tau0.npz", "snap_tau0.05.npz", out="density.mp4")


The DNS flow fields are shown at full grid resolution without time averaging;
averaging would suppress resolved structure.  For reference, the remaining
fluctuation amplitude in one sample calculation was:

        snapshots averaged     u' rms remaining
              1                     100 %
              5                      84 %
             20                      56 %
            100                      26 %

Particle density has finite-N noise. At the streak scale there are about 120
particles per cell, so the density plots use coarse-graining and a rolling
time average.  The pattern persists over the short averaging window, reducing
sampling noise while retaining the resolved structure.

The two data types therefore use different smoothing choices.

Near-wall structures convect at U_c = 0.436 (U_c+ = 8.7), faster than the
local mean fluid velocity because they are influenced by fluid farther from
the wall.

In the laboratory frame the structures cross the box several times during the
record.  ``comoving=True`` removes this mean translation and makes their
evolution easier to see.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import os
import numpy as np
import h5py
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from matplotlib.animation import FuncAnimation, PillowWriter, FFMpegWriter
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter

import structures as st
import figstyle as fs
from slab_interp import (HX, HZ, LX, LZ, RE_TAU, Y_NODES,
                         N_SPAN, N_STREAM, N_PLANES)
import slab_interp as si
import dataset as ds

# Dataset location and geometry come from dataset.SPEC.  The default plane is
# the configured top of the slab.


def _plane_default(plane=None, a=None):
    """
    Which plane to sample.

    Priority is an explicit ``plane``, then the per-run particle radius ``a``,
    and finally ``PLANE_TOP`` with a note describing the sampled height.
    """
    if plane is not None:
        return plane
    if a is not None:
        return float(a)
    print("  note: no radius given; sampling the slab top "
          f"(y = {Y_NODES[st.PLANE_TOP]:.4g}). Pass a=<run radius> to sample "
          "the particle plane y = a.")
    return st.PLANE_TOP


def _plane_tag(fld):
    """Axis-note text for whichever height was sampled."""
    h = fld.get("height") if isinstance(fld, dict) else None
    if h is None:
        return ""
    return f"$y = {h:.4g}$"


def _n_snapshots():
    return ds.SPEC.read_geometry()["n_t"]


def _last_frame():
    return _n_snapshots() - 1


def _extent(crop=None):
    """
    (L, W) for a crop given as (n_stream, n_span), or None for the full box.

    A scalar crop is ambiguous because the two grid directions have different
    lengths; pass ``(n_stream, n_span)`` instead.
    """
    if crop is None:
        return N_STREAM * HX, N_SPAN * HZ
    if np.isscalar(crop):
        raise ValueError(
            f"crop={crop} is ambiguous: the grid is {N_STREAM} streamwise x "
            f"{N_SPAN} spanwise. Pass (n_stream, n_span).")
    return crop[0] * HX, crop[1] * HZ


def _times():
    return ds.SPEC.read_geometry()["t"]

NAVY = "#1F3864"
GREY = "#8A8A8A"
U_CONV = 0.43644
# Streak convection velocity measured on Slab_complete.h5. Recompute it with
# measure_U_conv() when using a different dataset.


def measure_U_conv(plane=None, n=200, max_shift=None):
    """
    Convection velocity of the streaks, from the streamwise cross-correlation
    between consecutive snapshots.

    For each pair of frames, find the streamwise shift that best aligns u'.
    The mean shift per unit time is U_c. It is a structure velocity rather than
    the local mean fluid velocity.
    """
    plane = st.PLANE_TOP if plane is None else plane
    g = ds.SPEC.read_geometry()
    n = min(n, g["n_t"])
    dt = g["dt"]
    prev, shifts = None, []
    import h5py
    with h5py.File(ds.SPEC.path, "r") as f:
        for k in range(n):
            u, _, _ = ds.SPEC.load_plane_stack(k, plane, f=f)
            up = u - u.mean()
            if prev is not None:
                m = max_shift or up.shape[1] // 4
                c = [float((prev * np.roll(up, -j, axis=1)).mean())
                     for j in range(m)]
                shifts.append(int(np.argmax(c)))
            prev = up
    U_c = float(np.median(shifts)) * HX / dt
    print(f"  U_conv = {U_c:.5f}  (median shift "
          f"{np.median(shifts):.1f} cells per {dt:g} time units, "
          f"{len(shifts)} frame pairs)")
    return U_c
LAMBDA = 0.2618           # spanwise streak spacing, measured

plt.rcParams.update({
    "font.size": 11, "axes.titlesize": 12, "axes.labelsize": 11,
    "xtick.labelsize": 10, "ytick.labelsize": 10, "legend.fontsize": 10,
    "figure.dpi": 110, "savefig.dpi": 200,
    "axes.linewidth": 0.9, "axes.grid": False,
    "image.interpolation": "nearest",
})

try:                       # let matplotlib find ffmpeg if it is pip-installed
    import imageio_ffmpeg
    matplotlib.rcParams["animation.ffmpeg_path"] = imageio_ffmpeg.get_ffmpeg_exe()
except Exception:
    pass


# ------------------------------------------------------------------- utils
def _save_anim(anim, path, fps):
    if path.lower().endswith(".mp4"):
        try:
            anim.save(path, writer=FFMpegWriter(fps=fps, bitrate=3000))
            print(f"  wrote {path}")
            return path
        except Exception:
            path = os.path.splitext(path)[0] + ".gif"
            print("  ffmpeg unavailable -> gif")
    anim.save(path, writer=PillowWriter(fps=fps))
    print(f"  wrote {path}")
    return path


def _save_fig(fig, path, dpi=600):
    """Vector PDF for the thesis plus a PNG for viewing (see figstyle.save)."""
    if path:
        fs.save(fig, path, dpi=dpi)
    return fig


def _panel(ax, F, L, W, v, cmap=fs.DIVERGING, label=None, title=None,
           fig=None, letter=None):
    """
    Draw one field panel. ``label`` is a key in ``figstyle.SYMBOL``.
    """
    im = ax.imshow(F, origin="lower", extent=[0, L, 0, W], aspect="equal",
                   cmap=cmap, vmin=-v, vmax=v, interpolation="nearest",
                   rasterized=True)
    if fig is not None:
        fs.cbar(fig, im, ax, fs.sym(label) if label else None, fraction=0.021,
                pad=0.008)
    if title:
        ax.set_title(title, fontsize=9)
    ax.set_ylabel(fs.SYMBOL["z"])
    if letter:
        fs.panel(ax, letter)
    return im


def comoving_roll(F, t, U_c=U_CONV, L=LX, hx=HX):
    """
    Shift a field into a frame moving downstream at U_c.

    The streamwise axis is periodic, so this is a circular roll by the integer
    number of cells the frame has travelled. Sub-cell precision is not needed:
    the residual is under half a cell, far below the streak width.
    """
    n = int(round((U_c * t / hx))) % F.shape[1]
    return np.roll(F, -n, axis=1)


def load_snap(path):
    z = np.load(path, allow_pickle=True)
    out = {"positions": z["positions"], "times": z["times"]}
    out["meta"] = {k[5:]: (z[k].item() if z[k].ndim == 0 else z[k])
                   for k in z.files if k.startswith("meta_")}
    if "total_collisions" in z.files:
        out["collisions"] = z["total_collisions"].item()
    return out


# =========================================================== FLOW: full res
def flow_fields(k, plane=None, crop=None, a=None):
    """
    Every structure field at one snapshot, full grid resolution.

    ``a`` is the run's particle radius and selects the particle plane y = a.
    """
    plane = _plane_default(plane, a)
    an = st.analyse_structures(k=k, plane=plane)
    F = {
        "u_fluct": an["streaks"]["u_fluct"],
        "w_fluct": an["field"]["w"] - an["field"]["w"].mean(),
        "div2d": an["div"]["div2d"],
        "vorticity": an["strain"]["omega"],
        "Q": an["strain"]["Q"],
        "strain": an["strain"]["S_mag"],
    }
    if crop is not None:
        # arrays are (span, stream); crop is (n_stream, n_span)
        if np.isscalar(crop):
            raise ValueError(
                f"crop={crop} is ambiguous: the grid is {N_STREAM} streamwise "
                f"x {N_SPAN} spanwise. Pass (n_stream, n_span).")
        ns, nz = int(crop[0]), int(crop[1])
        F = {k_: v[:nz, :ns] for k_, v in F.items()}
    F["_meta"] = a
    return F


def flow_structures(k=None, plane=None, out=None, crop=None,
                    comoving=False, a=None):
    """
    All five structure fields at full resolution, no averaging.

    This is the figure to show for "what does the turbulence look like".
    """
    k = _last_frame() if k is None else k
    F = flow_fields(k, plane, crop, a=a)
    L, W = _extent(crop)
    if comoving:
        t = _times()
        tk = float(t[k]) - float(t[0])      # offset from the record start,
        F = {kk: (comoving_roll(v, tk, L=L) if kk != "_meta" else v)
             for kk, v in F.items()}        # use the current record origin

    # Keep panel titles short; interpretation is supplied in the caption.
    rows = [("u_fluct", "streamwise fluctuation", fs.DIVERGING),
            ("w_fluct", "spanwise fluctuation", fs.DIVERGING),
            ("div2d", "slice divergence", "PuOr_r"),
            ("vorticity", "wall-normal vorticity", fs.DIVERGING),
            ("Q", "second invariant", fs.DIVERGING)]
    # Two columns keep five equal-aspect panels on a single page.
    ncol = 2
    nrow = int(np.ceil(len(rows) / ncol))
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(nrow, ncol, squeeze=False,
                             figsize=fs.panel_figsize(L, W, ncols=ncol,
                                                      nrows=nrow,
                                                      extra_h=1.2))
    ax = axes.ravel()
    for i, (key, ttl, cm) in enumerate(rows):
        vlim = fs.field_limits(F[key])
        _panel(ax[i], F[key], L, W, vlim[1], cm, key, ttl, fig,
               letter=chr(ord("a") + i))
    for j in range(len(rows), len(ax)):        # blank the unused cell
        ax[j].axis("off")
    for i, a_ in enumerate(ax[:len(rows)]):
        if i // ncol == nrow - 1 or i + ncol >= len(rows):
            a_.set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["x"])
        else:
            a_.set_xticklabels([])
        if i % ncol:
            a_.set_ylabel("")
    fig.align_ylabels(axes[:, 0])
    fig.tight_layout()
    fs.caption(fig,
               "Instantaneous fields on a wall-parallel plane, full grid "
               "resolution, no averaging"
               + (" (co-moving frame). " if comoving else ". ")
               + "In (c) negative values mark convergence of the cut, where "
                 "particles accumulate; in (e) negative $Q$ is "
                 "strain-dominated.")
    return _save_fig(fig, out)


def animate_flow(out="flow.mp4", k0=0, k1=None, n_frames=120,
                 plane=None, crop=None, fps=12, comoving=False,
                 fields=("u_fluct", "div2d")):
    """
    Animate instantaneous structures without time averaging.

    comoving=True holds the streaks still so their evolution is visible; in
    the lab frame they sweep across the box ~3 times over the record and you
    see advection instead.
    """
    k1 = _last_frame() if k1 is None else k1
    k1 = _last_frame() if k1 is None else k1
    plane = st.PLANE_TOP if plane is None else plane
    ks = np.linspace(k0, k1, n_frames).astype(int)
    tf = _times()
    t0 = float(tf[0])
    L, W = _extent(crop)

    stacks = {kk: [] for kk in fields}
    for k in ks:
        F = flow_fields(int(k), plane, crop)
        for kk in fields:
            v = F[kk]
            if comoving:
                v = comoving_roll(v, float(tf[k]) - t0, L=L)
            stacks[kk].append(v)
    stacks = {kk: np.array(v) for kk, v in stacks.items()}

    lbl = {"u_fluct": ("$u'$  streaks", "RdBu_r"),
           "w_fluct": ("$w'$  spanwise", "RdBu_r"),
           "div2d": ("slice divergence", "PuOr_r"),
           "vorticity": (r"vorticity $\omega_y$", "RdBu_r"),
           "Q": ("$Q$", "RdBu_r"), "strain": ("$|S|$", "viridis")}

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(len(fields), 1,
                           figsize=fs.panel_figsize(L, W, nrows=len(fields),
                                                    extra_h=0.9),
                           squeeze=False)
    ax = ax[:, 0]
    ims = []
    for a_, kk in zip(ax, fields):
        ttl, cm = lbl.get(kk, (kk, "RdBu_r"))
        ims.append(_panel(a_, stacks[kk][0], L, W, 2.5 * stacks[kk].std(),
                          cm, kk, ttl, fig))
    ax[-1].set_xlabel("streamwise $x$")
    sup = fig.suptitle("", fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=[0, 0, 1, 0.93])

    def upd(i):
        for im, kk in zip(ims, fields):
            im.set_data(stacks[kk][i])
        sup.set_text(f"instantaneous structures, t = {tf[ks[i]]:.1f}"
                     + ("   (co-moving frame)" if comoving else ""))
        return ims + [sup]

    return _save_anim(FuncAnimation(fig, upd, frames=len(ks), blit=False),
                      out, fps)


# ============================================================ STREAKS
def streak_analysis(k=None, plane=None, out=None, a=None):
    """
    The streaks in detail: field, threshold map, spanwise correlation and the
    streamwise/spanwise anisotropy that defines them.
    """
    k = _last_frame() if k is None else k
    plane = _plane_default(plane, a)
    an = st.analyse_structures(k=k, plane=plane)
    up, sp = an["streaks"]["u_fluct"], an["streaks"]

    fig = plt.figure(figsize=fs.panel_figsize(LX, LZ, ncols=2, nrows=2,
                                              extra_h=1.4))
    gs = fig.add_gridspec(3, 2, height_ratios=[1.15, 1.15, 1.0],
                          hspace=0.45, wspace=0.22)
    ax = fig.add_subplot(gs[0, :])
    _panel(ax, up, LX, LZ, 2.2 * up.std(), "RdBu_r", "$u'$",
           "streamwise velocity fluctuation — blue = low-speed streak", fig)

    ax2 = fig.add_subplot(gs[1, :])
    M = np.zeros_like(up); M[sp["low"]] = -1; M[sp["high"]] = 1
    ax2.imshow(M, origin="lower", extent=[0, LX, 0, LZ], aspect="equal",
               cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=-1, vmax=1, interpolation="nearest")
    # State the RMS threshold and its value in the same units as the colourbar.
    ax2.set_title(
        rf"$|u'| > \sigma_{{u'}}$, $\sigma_{{u'}} = {sp['rms']:.3g}$"
        rf"   ---   low-speed {100*sp['frac_low']:.0f}% of the area, "
        rf"high-speed {100*sp['frac_high']:.0f}%"
        rf"   (Gaussian would give 15.9% each)", fontsize=9)
    ax2.set_ylabel(fs.SYMBOL["z"]); ax2.set_xlabel(fs.SYMBOL["x"])

    ax3 = fig.add_subplot(gs[2, 0])
    ens = st.streak_spacing_mean(plane=plane, n=st.LAMBDA_SNAPS)
    ax3.plot(sp["lags"] * HZ, sp["corr"], color=GREY, lw=1.0, alpha=0.8,
             label="this snapshot")
    ax3.plot(ens["lags"] * HZ, ens["corr"], color="#1F5FA8", lw=2.2,
             label=f"mean of {ens['n_snapshots']} snapshots")
    ax3.axhline(0, color="k", lw=0.8)
    # Mark the measured minimum as well as the inferred spacing lambda = 2 r.
    ax3.axvline(ens["lambda"] / 2, color=fs.RED, ls="--", lw=1.5,
                label=rf"first min $= \lambda/2$, $R={ens.get('R_min', float('nan')):+.2f}$")
    ax3.axvline(ens["lambda"], color="#1E7B34", ls=":", lw=2.2,
                label=f"$\\lambda={ens['lambda']:.3f}$"
                      f"  ($\\lambda={ens['lambda']:.3f}$)")
    ax3.set_xlim(0, 1.0)
    ax3.set_xlabel("spanwise separation $\\Delta z$")
    ax3.set_ylabel("$R_{uu}(\\Delta z)$")
    ax3.set_title("spanwise correlation → the spacing", color=NAVY)
    ax3.legend(frameon=False, fontsize=9); ax3.grid(alpha=0.2)

    ax4 = fig.add_subplot(gs[2, 1])
    u2 = up - up.mean()
    m = 60
    cx = [float((u2 * np.roll(u2, i, axis=1)).mean() / (u2 * u2).mean())
          for i in range(m)]
    cz = [float((u2 * np.roll(u2, i, axis=0)).mean() / (u2 * u2).mean())
          for i in range(m)]
    ax4.plot(np.arange(m) * HX, cx, color="#1F5FA8", lw=1.8, label="streamwise")
    ax4.plot(np.arange(m) * HZ, cz, color="#C0392B", lw=1.8, label="spanwise")
    ax4.axhline(1 / np.e, color=GREY, ls="--", lw=1.1, label="$1/e$")
    ax4.set_xlim(0, 2.0); ax4.set_xlabel("separation")
    ax4.set_ylabel("correlation")
    ax4.set_title("long streamwise, narrow spanwise", fontsize=10.5, color=NAVY)
    ax4.legend(frameon=False, fontsize=9); ax4.grid(alpha=0.25)

    h = (float(plane) if isinstance(plane, float)
         and not float(plane).is_integer() else float(Y_NODES[int(plane)]))
    fig.suptitle(f"near-wall streaks   —   snapshot {k},   $y = {h:.4g}$",
                 fontsize=14, color=NAVY, y=0.995)
    return _save_fig(fig, out)


def animate_streaks(out="streaks.mp4", k0=0, k1=None, n_frames=150,
                    plane=None, fps=12, comoving=True, threshold=None):
    """
    Streaks evolving, at full resolution.

    With ``comoving=True``, the mean translation is removed so the evolution
    of the structures remains visible.

    The shift uses ``U_CONV``. Recalculate it with ``measure_U_conv`` when
    working with a different dataset; an inaccurate value leaves residual
    drift in the animation.

    threshold : also draw the |u'| > threshold*rms map beneath, which makes
                individual streaks countable.
    """
    k1 = _last_frame() if k1 is None else k1
    plane = st.PLANE_TOP if plane is None else plane
    ks = np.linspace(k0, k1, n_frames).astype(int)
    tf = _times()
    t0 = float(tf[0])
    UF = []
    for k in ks:
        a = st.analyse_structures(k=int(k), plane=plane)
        u = a["streaks"]["u_fluct"]
        UF.append(comoving_roll(u, float(tf[k]) - t0) if comoving else u)
    UF = np.array(UF)
    v = 2.2 * UF.std()

    nrow = 2 if threshold else 1
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(nrow, 1, squeeze=False,
                           figsize=fs.panel_figsize(LX, LZ, nrows=nrow,
                                                    extra_h=0.9))
    ax = ax[:, 0]
    im = _panel(ax[0], UF[0], LX, LZ, v, "RdBu_r", "$u'$", None, fig)
    ax[0].set_title("$u'$  —  blue = low-speed streak", fontsize=10.5, color=NAVY)
    im2 = None
    if threshold:
        s = UF.std()
        M0 = np.where(UF[0] < -threshold * s, -1.0,
                      np.where(UF[0] > threshold * s, 1.0, 0.0))
        im2 = ax[1].imshow(M0, origin="lower", extent=[0, LX, 0, LZ],
                           aspect="equal", cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=-1, vmax=1,
                           interpolation="nearest")
        ax[1].set_title(f"$|u'| > {threshold}\\,$rms", fontsize=10.5, color=NAVY)
        ax[1].set_ylabel("spanwise $z$")
    ax[-1].set_xlabel("streamwise $x$")
    sup = fig.suptitle("", fontsize=14, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=[0, 0, 1, 0.93])

    s = UF.std()

    def upd(i):
        im.set_data(UF[i])
        out_ = [im, sup]
        if im2 is not None:
            im2.set_data(np.where(UF[i] < -threshold * s, -1.0,
                                  np.where(UF[i] > threshold * s, 1.0, 0.0)))
            out_.append(im2)
        sup.set_text(f"near-wall streaks, t = {tf[ks[i]]:.1f}"
                     + ("   (co-moving: structures held still)" if comoving
                        else "   (lab frame: sweeping downstream)"))
        return out_

    return _save_anim(FuncAnimation(fig, upd, frames=len(ks), blit=False),
                      out, fps)


# ==================================================== PARTICLES: coarse
def density_grid(L, W, scale=LAMBDA, n_grid=64):
    """
    A coarse grid at the requested scale, using divisors of the source grid so
    the flow can be block-averaged onto the same cells without discarding data.
    """
    nb_x = st.nearest_divisor(n_grid, L / scale)
    nb_z = st.nearest_divisor(n_grid, W / scale)
    return nb_x, nb_z


def particle_density(p, L, W, nb_x, nb_z):
    H, _, _ = np.histogram2d(np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W),
                             bins=[nb_x, nb_z], range=[[0, L], [0, W]])
    return H / H.mean()


def _rolling(F, w):
    if w <= 1:
        return list(F)
    A = np.asarray(F); h = w // 2
    return [A[max(0, k - h):k + h + 1].mean(0) for k in range(len(A))]


def particle_density_figure(snap_tracer, snap_case, out=None, scale=LAMBDA,
                            L=None, W=None, time_avg=15, n_grid=None):
    """
    Coarse-grained, time-averaged particle density: tracer, case, difference.

    Both panels use the same colour limits, so if they look identical it is
    because they are. The difference panel makes that quantitative.
    """
    a, b = load_snap(snap_tracer), load_snap(snap_case)
    n = N_SPAN if n_grid is None else n_grid
    # Use the run dimensions stored in the snapshots, including cropped runs.
    ma = a.get("meta", {})
    L = L or float(ma.get("L", 0)) or N_STREAM * HX
    W = W or float(ma.get("W", 0)) or N_SPAN * HZ
    nb_x, nb_z = density_grid(L, W, scale, n)
    FA = _rolling([particle_density(a["positions"][i], L, W, nb_x, nb_z)
                   for i in range(len(a["positions"]))], time_avg)
    FB = _rolling([particle_density(b["positions"][i], L, W, nb_x, nb_z)
                   for i in range(len(b["positions"]))], time_avg)
    A_, B_ = FA[-1], FB[-1]
    v = 2.2 * max(A_.std(), B_.std())
    d = B_ - A_
    per = a["positions"].shape[1] / (nb_x * nb_z)

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(3, 1,
                           figsize=fs.panel_figsize(L, W, nrows=3,
                                                    extra_h=1.0))
    tau = b.get("meta", {}).get("tau", "?")
    for k, (F, lbl) in enumerate(((A_, r"tracer  $\tau_p = 0$"),
                                  (B_, rf"inertial  $\tau_p = {tau}$"))):
        im = ax[k].imshow(F.T, origin="lower", extent=[0, L, 0, W],
                          aspect="equal", cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=1 - v, vmax=1 + v,
                          interpolation="bilinear")
        fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax[k], fraction=0.021, pad=0.01,
                     label=r"$n/\langle n\rangle$")
        ax[k].set_title(lbl, fontsize=11, color=NAVY)
        ax[k].set_ylabel("spanwise")
    imd = ax[2].imshow(d.T, origin="lower", extent=[0, L, 0, W], aspect="equal",
                       cmap="PuOr_r", vmin=-v, vmax=v, interpolation="bilinear")
    fig.colorbar(imd, ax=ax[2], fraction=0.021, pad=0.01, label="difference")
    ax[2].set_title(f"inertial $-$ tracer   (rms {d.std():.4f} vs field rms "
                    f"{A_.std():.4f})", fontsize=11, color=NAVY)
    ax[2].set_ylabel("spanwise"); ax[2].set_xlabel("streamwise")
    fig.suptitle(f"particle density, {nb_x}$\\times${nb_z} cells at the streak "
                 f"scale, {time_avg}-frame average  ({per:.0f} particles/cell)",
                 fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=[0, 0, 1, 0.94])
    return _save_fig(fig, out)


def animate_density_pair(snap_tracer, snap_case, out="density.mp4",
                         scale=LAMBDA, L=None, W=None, time_avg=5, fps=10,
                         n_grid=64):
    """Tracer and inertial density side by side, time-averaged, animated."""
    a, b = load_snap(snap_tracer), load_snap(snap_case)
    n = N_SPAN if n_grid is None else n_grid
    # Use the run dimensions stored in the snapshots, including cropped runs.
    ma = a.get("meta", {})
    L = L or float(ma.get("L", 0)) or N_STREAM * HX
    W = W or float(ma.get("W", 0)) or N_SPAN * HZ
    nb_x, nb_z = density_grid(L, W, scale, n)
    t = a["times"]
    FA = _rolling([particle_density(p, L, W, nb_x, nb_z)
                   for p in a["positions"]], time_avg)
    FB = _rolling([particle_density(p, L, W, nb_x, nb_z)
                   for p in b["positions"]], time_avg)
    v = 2.2 * float(np.mean([f.std() for f in FA]))
    tau = b.get("meta", {}).get("tau", "?")

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(2, 1,
                           figsize=fs.panel_figsize(L, W, nrows=2,
                                                    extra_h=0.9))
    ims = []
    for k, (F, lbl) in enumerate(((FA, r"tracer  $\tau_p=0$"),
                                  (FB, rf"inertial  $\tau_p={tau}$"))):
        im = ax[k].imshow(F[0].T, origin="lower", extent=[0, L, 0, W],
                          aspect="equal", cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=1 - v, vmax=1 + v,
                          interpolation="bilinear")
        fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax[k], fraction=0.021, pad=0.01,
                     label=r"$n/\langle n\rangle$")
        ax[k].set_title(lbl, fontsize=11, color=NAVY)
        ax[k].set_ylabel("spanwise")
        ims.append(im)
    ax[1].set_xlabel("streamwise")
    sup = fig.suptitle("", fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=[0, 0, 1, 0.93])

    def upd(i):
        ims[0].set_data(FA[i].T); ims[1].set_data(FB[i].T)
        sup.set_text(f"particle density at the streak scale, t = {t[i]:.1f}")
        return ims + [sup]

    return _save_anim(FuncAnimation(fig, upd, frames=len(t), blit=False),
                      out, fps)


# ======================================================== COMBINED
def particles_on_structure(snap, out=None, k=None, plane=None, crop=None,
                           scale=LAMBDA, time_avg=15, field="div2d"):
    """
    Particle density beside the flow structure that causes it, each rendered
    the way its own noise properties demand: the flow at full resolution, the
    particles coarse-grained and time-averaged.

    The correlation printed is computed on the common coarse grid.
    """
    s = load_snap(snap)
    k = _last_frame() if k is None else k
    L, W = _extent(crop)
    n_grid = (crop[1] if crop is not None else N_SPAN)
    nb_x, nb_z = density_grid(L, W, scale, n_grid)
    D = _rolling([particle_density(p, L, W, nb_x, nb_z)
                  for p in s["positions"]], time_avg)[-1]
    F = flow_fields(k, plane, crop)[field]
    Fc = st.coarsen(F, nb_x, nb_z)
    x, y = D.ravel() - D.mean(), Fc.ravel() - Fc.mean()
    r = float((x * y).sum() / np.sqrt((x * x).sum() * (y * y).sum()))

    fig, ax = plt.subplots(3, 1,
                           figsize=fs.panel_figsize(L, W, nrows=3,
                                                    extra_h=1.0))
    im0 = ax[0].imshow(D.T, origin="lower", extent=[0, L, 0, W], aspect="equal",
                       cmap="RdBu_r", vmin=1 - 2.2 * D.std(),
                       vmax=1 + 2.2 * D.std(), interpolation="bilinear")
    fig.colorbar(im0, ax=ax[0], fraction=0.021, pad=0.01,
                 label=r"$n/\langle n\rangle$")
    ax[0].set_title(f"particle density  —  {nb_x}$\\times${nb_z} cells, "
                    f"{time_avg}-frame average", fontsize=11, color=NAVY)
    _panel(ax[1], F, L, W, 2.5 * F.std(), "PuOr_r", field,
           f"{field}  —  full grid resolution, instantaneous", fig)
    im2 = ax[2].imshow(Fc.T, origin="lower", extent=[0, L, 0, W],
                       aspect="equal", cmap="PuOr_r", vmin=-2.2 * Fc.std(),
                       vmax=2.2 * Fc.std(), interpolation="bilinear")
    fig.colorbar(im2, ax=ax[2], fraction=0.021, pad=0.01, label=field)
    ax[2].set_title(f"{field} block-averaged onto the particle grid   "
                    f"(correlation with density {r:+.3f})",
                    fontsize=11, color=NAVY)
    for a_ in ax:
        a_.set_ylabel("spanwise")
    ax[2].set_xlabel("streamwise")
    fig.suptitle("particles and the structure that organises them",
                 fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=[0, 0, 1, 0.94])
    _save_fig(fig, out)
    return {"corr": r, "nb": (nb_x, nb_z)}


def make_everything(snap_tracer, snap_case, outdir="viz", crop=None, fps=12):
    """All figures and animations, in the order you would put them in a thesis."""
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
    P = lambda n: os.path.join(outdir, n)
    print("figures:")
    streak_analysis(out=P("streaks.png"))
    flow_structures(out=P("structures_full.png"))
    particle_density_figure(snap_tracer, snap_case, out=P("density.png"))
    particles_on_structure(snap_case, out=P("mechanism.png"), crop=crop)
    print("animations:")
    animate_streaks(P("streaks_comoving.mp4"), comoving=True,
                    threshold=1.0, fps=fps)
    animate_streaks(P("streaks_lab.mp4"), comoving=False, fps=fps)
    animate_flow(P("flow.mp4"), comoving=True, fps=fps)
    animate_density_pair(snap_tracer, snap_case, P("density.mp4"), fps=fps)
    print(f"\nall output in {outdir}/")
snapshots.pyCompact particle-snapshot saving, loading and plottingSee code ↗

Run metadata is restored separately from the time-dependent arrays.

"""Save, load and plot particle snapshots.

The engine keeps every position frame in ``res["position_history"]`` during a
run.  This module writes an evenly sampled subset to a compressed archive so
the configurations can be inspected and replotted without rerunning the
simulation.

    from snapshots import save_snapshots, load_snapshots, fig_snapshot_series

    save_snapshots(res, "snap_tau0.5.npz", n_frames=50, tau=0.5)
    s = load_snapshots("snap_tau0.5.npz")
    fig_snapshot_series(s, L, W, "series_tau0.5.png")

Approximate sizes at N = 20 000 (float32, compressed):
    50 frames   ~  8 MB
    200 frames  ~ 30 MB
    every step  ~700 MB
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import numpy as np
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use("Agg")
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

import figstyle as fs

NAVY, BLUE, GREY = fs.NAVY, fs.BLUE, fs.GREY


def domain_of(snap, L=None, W=None):
    """
    The box the run actually used.

    A cropped run occupies only part of the DNS domain. Drawing it inside the
    full Lx x Lz would leave most of the frame blank. Order of preference:
    explicit L/W, then the recorded meta_L/meta_W, then the particle extents
    snapped up to a whole number of grid cells.
    """
    if L is not None and W is not None:
        return float(L), float(W)
    meta = snap.get("meta", {}) if isinstance(snap, dict) else {}
    if "L" in meta and "W" in meta:
        return float(meta["L"]), float(meta["W"])
    P = np.asarray(snap["positions"][0], float)
    try:
        from slab_interp import HX, HZ, LX, LZ
        Lx = min(float(np.ceil(P[:, 0].max() / HX)) * HX, LX)
        Wz = min(float(np.ceil(P[:, 1].max() / HZ)) * HZ, LZ)
    except Exception:
        Lx, Wz = float(P[:, 0].max()), float(P[:, 1].max())
    return Lx, Wz


# ------------------------------------------------------------- save / load
def save_snapshots(res, path, n_frames=50, save_velocity=False,
                   float32=True, **meta):
    """
    Subsample a run's history and write it to a compressed .npz.

    n_frames : how many evenly spaced frames to keep (None = all).
    float32  : halves the file size; positions are only ever plotted or
               histogrammed, so single precision is ample.
    **meta   : anything else worth recording (tau, N, radius, dt, ...).
               Stored alongside and returned by load_snapshots().
    """
    times = np.asarray(res["event_times"], float)
    hist = res["position_history"]
    idx = (np.arange(len(times)) if n_frames is None else
           np.linspace(0, len(times) - 1, min(n_frames, len(times))).astype(int))
    dtype = np.float32 if float32 else np.float64
    pos = np.stack([np.asarray(hist[i], dtype) for i in idx])
    out = {"positions": pos, "times": times[idx]}
    if save_velocity and "velocity_history" in res:
        vh = res["velocity_history"]
        out["velocities"] = np.stack([np.asarray(vh[i], dtype) for i in idx])
    # Spins use the pipeline order (streamwise, spanwise, wall-normal) and are
    # stored at the same frames as the positions.
    oh = res.get("omega_history")
    if oh is not None and len(oh):
        out["spins"] = np.stack([np.asarray(oh[i], dtype) for i in idx])
    # model E per-frame wall-friction diagnostics, aligned with `times`
    # (the engine writes no diagnostics row for the initial frame, so the
    # table is one row shorter than `times`: pad the t = 0 slot with NaN)
    dg = res.get("diagnostics")
    if dg is not None:
        for col in ("rolling_frac", "wall_work_cum", "slip_rms",
                    "pz_abs_mean", "pz_abs_max", "pz_exceed_cum",
                    "pz_exceed_rate"):
            if col not in getattr(dg, "columns", ()):
                continue
            arr = np.asarray(dg[col], np.float32)
            if len(arr) == len(times) - 1:
                arr = np.concatenate([[np.nan], arr]).astype(np.float32)
            if len(arr) == len(times):
                out[col] = arr[idx]
    out["total_collisions"] = res.get("total_collisions", 0)
    # Collision events are kept in full when logging is enabled. Separate
    # dtypes reduce the archive size without lowering the event-time precision.
    ev = res.get("collision_events")
    if ev is not None and len(ev):
        ev = np.asarray(ev)
        out["collision_t"] = ev[:, 0].astype(np.float64)
        out["collision_ij"] = ev[:, 1:3].astype(np.int32)
        out["collision_angle"] = ev[:, 3].astype(np.float32)
        out["collision_wr"] = ev[:, 4].astype(np.float32)
        if ev.shape[1] >= 7:
            out["collision_xc"] = ev[:, 5:7].astype(np.float32)
        if ev.shape[1] >= 10:
            # tangential contact slip U^-_par, normal impulse P_perp and
            # tangential impulse P_par on j (notes_sep26 Eqs. 7-9)
            out["collision_wt"] = ev[:, 7].astype(np.float32)
            out["collision_Pn"] = ev[:, 8].astype(np.float32)
            out["collision_Pt"] = ev[:, 9].astype(np.float32)
        if ev.shape[1] >= 11:
            # Hypothetical vertical impulse on j, recorded but not applied.
            out["collision_Pz_diag"] = ev[:, 10].astype(np.float32)
        if ev.shape[1] >= 12:
            # Vertical component of the full contact slip, retained so Pz~ can
            # be re-evaluated for any (e_tan, mu_pp).
            out["collision_Uy_diag"] = ev[:, 11].astype(np.float32)
        out["collision_log_truncated"] = bool(
            res.get("collision_log_truncated", False))
    for k, v in meta.items():
        out[f"meta_{k}"] = v
    np.savez_compressed(path, **out)
    import os
    print(f"  wrote {path}: {pos.shape[0]} frames x {pos.shape[1]} particles"
          f"  ({os.path.getsize(path)/1e6:.1f} MB)")
    return path


def load_snapshots(path):
    """Read back a saved run. Returns a dict with positions, times and meta."""
    z = np.load(path, allow_pickle=True)
    out = {k: z[k] for k in z.files if not k.startswith("meta_")}
    out["meta"] = {k[5:]: z[k].item() if z[k].ndim == 0 else z[k]
                   for k in z.files if k.startswith("meta_")}
    return out


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ plots
def _panel(ax, p, L, W, s=None, title=None):
    q = np.column_stack([np.mod(p[:, 0], L), np.mod(p[:, 1], W)])
    s = max(0.15, min(3.0, 3000.0 / max(len(p), 1))) if s is None else s
    ax.scatter(q[:, 0], q[:, 1], s=s, c="k", linewidths=0)
    ax.set_xlim(0, L); ax.set_ylim(0, W); ax.set_aspect("equal")
    ax.set_xticks([]); ax.set_yticks([])
    if title:
        ax.set_title(title, fontsize=9.5, color=NAVY)


def fig_snapshot_series(snap, L=None, W=None, path=None, n_show=8, ncols=4,
                        skip_transient=0.0, title=None):
    """
    A grid of snapshots through the run, so the build-up of structure is
    visible rather than only its end state.

    skip_transient : start after this engine time (use 5*tau_p).
    """
    L, W = domain_of(snap, L, W)
    t = np.asarray(snap["times"], float)
    P = snap["positions"]
    keep = np.where(t >= skip_transient)[0]
    if keep.size == 0:
        keep = np.arange(len(t))
    sel = keep[np.linspace(0, keep.size - 1,
                           min(n_show, keep.size)).astype(int)]
    nrows = int(np.ceil(len(sel) / ncols))
    # Derive panel height from the domain aspect and reserve a title band.
    pw = 3.1
    ph = pw * float(W) / float(L)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(nrows, ncols,
                           figsize=(pw * ncols, ph * nrows + 0.55),
                           squeeze=False)
    for k, i in enumerate(sel):
        _panel(ax[k // ncols][k % ncols], P[i], L, W, title=f"t = {t[i]:.1f}")
    for k in range(len(sel), nrows * ncols):
        ax[k // ncols][k % ncols].axis("off")
    fig.suptitle(title or "particle snapshots", fontsize=12.5, color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=(0, 0, 1, 1 - 0.55 / (ph * nrows + 0.55)))
    if path:
        fig.savefig(path, bbox_inches="tight", facecolor="white", dpi=200)
        print(f"  wrote {path}")
    return fig


def fig_snapshot_compare(snaps, L=None, W=None, path=None, t_target=None, labels=None):
    """
    The same instant across several runs, side by side. This is the figure that
    shows the effect of Stokes number directly, without any statistic in
    between.

    snaps : list of loaded snapshot dicts (or (times, positions) tuples)
    """
    n = len(snaps)
    L, W = domain_of(snaps[0], L, W)
    # Derive the height from the domain and reserve separate title spacing.
    pw = 3.4
    ph = pw * float(W) / float(L)
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, n, figsize=(pw * n, ph + 1.05),
                           squeeze=False)
    for k, s in enumerate(snaps):
        t = np.asarray(s["times"], float); P = s["positions"]
        i = -1 if t_target is None else int(np.argmin(np.abs(t - t_target)))
        lab = (labels[k] if labels else
               f"$\\tau_p$ = {s.get('meta', {}).get('tau', '?')}")
        _panel(ax[0][k], P[i], L, W, title=f"{lab}\nt = {t[i]:.1f}")
    fig.suptitle("same instant, different Stokes number", fontsize=12.5,
                 color=NAVY)
    fig.tight_layout(rect=(0, 0, 1, 1 - 0.50 / (ph + 1.05)))
    if path:
        fig.savefig(path, bbox_inches="tight", facecolor="white", dpi=200)
        print(f"  wrote {path}")
    return fig


def animate_snapshots(snap, L=None, W=None, path="particles.gif", fps=8, n_frames=None,
                      skip_transient=0.0):
    """
    Animated GIF of the particle field. Needs pillow (matplotlib's default
    writer). Useful for seeing whether structures persist or churn -- which no
    single statistic conveys.
    """
    from matplotlib.animation import FuncAnimation, PillowWriter
    L, W = domain_of(snap, L, W)
    t = np.asarray(snap["times"], float); P = snap["positions"]
    keep = np.where(t >= skip_transient)[0]
    if keep.size == 0:
        keep = np.arange(len(t))
    sel = (keep if n_frames is None else
           keep[np.linspace(0, keep.size - 1,
                            min(n_frames, keep.size)).astype(int)])
    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(7.5, 7.5 * W / L + 0.6))
    s = max(0.15, min(3.0, 3000.0 / P.shape[1]))
    q = np.column_stack([np.mod(P[sel[0]][:, 0], L), np.mod(P[sel[0]][:, 1], W)])
    sc = ax.scatter(q[:, 0], q[:, 1], s=s, c="k", linewidths=0)
    ax.set_xlim(0, L); ax.set_ylim(0, W); ax.set_aspect("equal")
    ax.set_xticks([]); ax.set_yticks([])
    ttl = ax.set_title("", fontsize=11, color=NAVY)

    def upd(k):
        i = sel[k]
        q = np.column_stack([np.mod(P[i][:, 0], L), np.mod(P[i][:, 1], W)])
        sc.set_offsets(q)
        ttl.set_text(f"t = {t[i]:.1f}")
        return sc, ttl

    an = FuncAnimation(fig, upd, frames=len(sel), blit=False)
    an.save(path, writer=PillowWriter(fps=fps))
    plt.close(fig)
    print(f"  wrote {path}  ({len(sel)} frames)")
    return path


def save_frames(snap, L=None, W=None, outdir="frames", prefix="frame", n_show=None,
                skip_transient=0.0, dpi=180, with_diagnostics=False,
                eps_factor=1.8, min_samples=10):
    """
    Write one image per timepoint rather than a single grid.

    Use this when you want individual frames -- to pick one for a figure, to
    inspect a particular instant closely, or to build an animation with an
    external tool.

    n_show          : how many frames (None = every frame in the file)
    with_diagnostics: use the full DBSCAN-segmented panel from figures.py
                      instead of a plain scatter. Much slower (DBSCAN per
                      frame), so reserve it for a handful of frames.

    Returns the list of paths written.
    """
    import os
    os.makedirs(outdir, exist_ok=True)
    L, W = domain_of(snap, L, W)
    t = np.asarray(snap["times"], float)
    P = snap["positions"]
    keep = np.where(t >= skip_transient)[0]
    if keep.size == 0:
        keep = np.arange(len(t))
    sel = (keep if n_show is None else
           keep[np.linspace(0, keep.size - 1,
                            min(n_show, keep.size)).astype(int)])

    paths = []
    for k, i in enumerate(sel):
        path = os.path.join(outdir, f"{prefix}_{k:04d}_t{t[i]:07.2f}.png")
        if with_diagnostics:
            import figures as fg
            fig = fg.fig_snapshot(np.asarray(P[i], float), L, W, path=None,
                                  eps_factor=eps_factor,
                                  min_samples=min_samples,
                                  title=f"t = {t[i]:.2f}")
            fig.savefig(path, bbox_inches="tight", facecolor="white", dpi=dpi)
            plt.close(fig)
        else:
            fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(7.5, 7.5 * W / L + 0.5))
            _panel(ax, P[i], L, W, title=f"t = {t[i]:.2f}")
            fig.savefig(path, bbox_inches="tight", facecolor="white", dpi=dpi)
            plt.close(fig)
        paths.append(path)
    print(f"  wrote {len(paths)} frames to {outdir}/")
    return paths
figstyle.pyShared figure dimensions, labels, colours and output helpersSee code ↗

Panel letters stay with the title instead of covering the plotted data.

"""Shared plotting style for the DNS analysis figures.

Importing this module applies the common Matplotlib settings.  The helpers
keep figure sizes, typography, colours, panel labels and output formats
consistent across the analysis scripts::

    import figstyle as fs
    fs.panel(ax, "a")
    fs.save(fig, "scales.png")

Figures are sized at their intended column width, use matching serif text and
math fonts, and map internal field names to publication labels.  Diverging
fields use symmetric limits around a visible neutral point, while sequential
fields use a perceptually uniform map.  ``save`` writes a high-resolution PNG
and, when requested, a vector PDF.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
import os
import numpy as np
import matplotlib as mpl
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from matplotlib.ticker import AutoMinorLocator

# ------------------------------------------------------------------- sizes
COL = 3.40          # single column, inches
WIDE = 7.00         # double column / full text width
GOLDEN = 0.618

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ colour
# Okabe-Ito: colourblind-safe, distinguishable in greyscale.
BLACK = "#000000"
BLUE = "#0072B2"
ORANGE = "#E69F00"
GREEN = "#009E73"
RED = "#D55E00"
PURPLE = "#CC79A7"
SKY = "#56B4E9"
YELLOW = "#F0E442"
GREY = "#7F7F7F"
CYCLE = [BLUE, RED, GREEN, ORANGE, PURPLE, SKY, GREY]

# kept so existing modules that import NAVY keep working
NAVY = BLACK

DIVERGING = "RdBu_r"        # zero-centred fields (u', divergence, Q)


def make_diverging(name="strong_div"):
    """Build a blue--orange diverging map with a visible neutral midpoint.

    Saturated endpoints keep the two signs distinct, and the light-grey centre
    remains visible against a white page or masked data.
    """
    from matplotlib.colors import LinearSegmentedColormap
    # Saturated endpoints keep positive and negative values visually distinct.
    stops = [
        (0.00, "#0050a0"),      # vivid blue    -- strongly negative
        (0.28, "#4a95d4"),
        (0.44, "#b8d6ea"),
        (0.50, "#efefed"),      # visible neutral grey, not white
        (0.56, "#fbd3a6"),
        (0.72, "#f28100"),
        (1.00, "#d13b00"),      # vivid orange  -- strongly positive
    ]
    return LinearSegmentedColormap.from_list(
        name, [(x, c) for x, c in stops])


DIVERGING_STRONG = make_diverging()
try:                                   # register so it can be named directly
    import matplotlib as _mpl
    if "strong_div" not in _mpl.colormaps:
        _mpl.colormaps.register(DIVERGING_STRONG, name="strong_div")
except Exception:
    pass
SEQUENTIAL = "viridis"      # positive-definite fields (|S|, density)
DENSITY = "RdBu_r"          # n/<n>, centred on 1


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ symbols
# Map internal keys to the symbols used on axes, legends and colourbars.
SYMBOL = {
    "u_fluct":    r"$u'/u_\tau$",
    "w_fluct":    r"$w'/u_\tau$",
    "v_fluct":    r"$v'/u_\tau$",
    "div2d":      r"$(\partial_x u + \partial_z w)\,\nu/u_\tau^2$",
    "dvdy":       r"$\partial_y v \, \nu/u_\tau^2$",
    "vorticity":  r"$\omega_y \nu/u_\tau^2$",
    "omega":      r"$\omega_y \nu/u_\tau^2$",
    "Q":          r"$Q\,\nu^2/u_\tau^4$",
    "strain":     r"$|S|\,\nu/u_\tau^2$",
    "S_mag":      r"$|S|\,\nu/u_\tau^2$",
    "density":    r"$n/\langle n\rangle$",
    "vmr_8":      r"VMR$_{8}$",
    "vmr_16":     r"VMR$_{16}$",
    "vmr_24":     r"VMR$_{24}$",
    "D2":         r"$D_2$",
    "g_contact":  r"$g(\sigma)$",
    "slip_mean":  r"$\langle|u-v|\rangle/u_\tau$",
    "tau":        r"$\tau_p$",
    # The DNS uses h = 1, so plain coordinate symbols are sufficient here;
    # captions or methods can state the normalisation once.
    "x":          r"$x$",
    "z":          r"$z$",
    "y":          r"$y$",
    "r":          r"$r$",
    "t":          r"$t$",
}


def sym(key, default=None):
    """Symbol for an internal key; falls back to the key with _ -> space."""
    return SYMBOL.get(key, default if default is not None
                      else key.replace("_", " "))


# ------------------------------------------------------------------- style
RC = {
    # fonts -- serif body with matching mathtext
    "font.family": "serif",
    "font.serif": ["DejaVu Serif", "Times New Roman", "Times", "STIXGeneral"],
    "mathtext.fontset": "dejavuserif",
    "font.size": 8.5,
    "axes.labelsize": 9,
    "axes.titlesize": 9,
    "xtick.labelsize": 8,
    "ytick.labelsize": 8,
    "legend.fontsize": 8,
    "figure.titlesize": 10,

    # axes
    "axes.linewidth": 0.7,
    "axes.labelpad": 3.0,
    "axes.titlepad": 4.0,
    "axes.spines.top": True,
    "axes.spines.right": True,
    "axes.prop_cycle": mpl.cycler(color=CYCLE),
    "axes.grid": False,                 # grids are added only when useful
    "axes.axisbelow": True,

    # ticks: inward, all four sides, with minors
    "xtick.direction": "in",
    "ytick.direction": "in",
    "xtick.top": True,
    "ytick.right": True,
    "xtick.major.size": 3.2,
    "ytick.major.size": 3.2,
    "xtick.minor.size": 1.8,
    "ytick.minor.size": 1.8,
    "xtick.major.width": 0.7,
    "ytick.major.width": 0.7,
    "xtick.minor.width": 0.6,
    "ytick.minor.width": 0.6,
    "xtick.minor.visible": True,
    "ytick.minor.visible": True,

    # lines and markers
    "lines.linewidth": 1.1,
    "lines.markersize": 3.2,
    "lines.markeredgewidth": 0.7,

    # legend
    "legend.frameon": False,
    "legend.handlelength": 1.8,
    "legend.handletextpad": 0.5,
    "legend.labelspacing": 0.3,
    "legend.borderaxespad": 0.4,

    # output
    "figure.dpi": 120,
    "savefig.dpi": 600,
    "savefig.bbox": "tight",
    "savefig.pad_inches": 0.02,
    "savefig.facecolor": "white",
    "pdf.fonttype": 42,              # embed TrueType, not Type-3: required by
    "ps.fonttype": 42,               # most publishers and keeps text selectable
    "image.origin": "lower",
    "image.interpolation": "nearest",
}


def apply():
    """Apply the style. Called on import; call again after any rcParams reset."""
    mpl.rcParams.update(RC)


apply()


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers
def panel(ax, letter, **kw):
    """
    Add a panel label to the left-aligned title.

    Keeping the label in the title avoids covering data or competing with a
    legend inside the axes.
    """
    t = ax.get_title()
    ax.set_title("")
    kw.setdefault("fontsize", 9)
    ax.set_title(f"({letter}) {t}" if t else f"({letter})", loc="left", **kw)
    return ax


def panels(axes, start="a", **kw):
    """Label a whole array of axes in reading order."""
    flat = np.atleast_1d(np.asarray(axes, dtype=object)).ravel()
    for i, ax in enumerate(flat):
        if ax is not None:
            panel(ax, chr(ord(start) + i), **kw)
    return axes


def minor(ax):
    """Minor ticks on a log-free axis (rcParams handles the linear default)."""
    ax.xaxis.set_minor_locator(AutoMinorLocator())
    ax.yaxis.set_minor_locator(AutoMinorLocator())
    return ax


def cbar(fig, im, ax, label=None, **kw):
    """Colourbar sized to its axes, with outward ticks and a symbol label."""
    kw.setdefault("fraction", 0.046)
    kw.setdefault("pad", 0.02)
    cb = fig.colorbar(im, ax=ax, **kw)
    cb.outline.set_linewidth(0.7)
    cb.ax.tick_params(which="both", direction="out", length=2.4, width=0.7)
    if label:
        cb.set_label(label, fontsize=9, labelpad=3)
    return cb


def save(fig, path, vector=True, dpi=600, close=False):
    """
    Write the figure. PDF for the thesis, PNG for quick viewing.

    The PDF keeps line art and text scalable; the PNG is convenient for quick
    inspection.
    """
    if path is None:
        return fig
    base, _ = os.path.splitext(path)
    out = []
    fig.savefig(base + ".png", dpi=dpi)
    out.append(base + ".png")
    if vector:
        try:
            fig.savefig(base + ".pdf")
            out.append(base + ".pdf")
        except Exception as e:                     # e.g. no PDF backend
            print(f"    (no vector output: {e})")
    print(f"  wrote {os.path.basename(base)}"
          + (".png + .pdf" if len(out) == 2 else ".png"))
    if close:
        plt.close(fig)
    return out[0]


def field_limits(F, q=0.995, symmetric=True):
    """
    Colour limits from a high quantile rather than the extremes.

    This prevents one outlying cell from flattening the contrast in the rest
    of the field.
    """
    F = np.asarray(F)
    if symmetric:
        v = float(np.quantile(np.abs(F - (0.0 if symmetric else F.mean())), q))
        return -v, v
    lo, hi = np.quantile(F, [1 - q, q])
    return float(lo), float(hi)


def image(ax, F, extent, cmap=DIVERGING, vlim=None, symmetric=True,
          interpolation="nearest"):
    """A field panel with sensible limits and a 1:1 aspect."""
    if vlim is None:
        vlim = field_limits(F, symmetric=symmetric)
    im = ax.imshow(F, origin="lower", extent=extent, aspect="equal",
                   cmap=cmap, vmin=vlim[0], vmax=vlim[1],
                   interpolation=interpolation, rasterized=True)
    ax.set_xlabel(SYMBOL["x"])
    ax.set_ylabel(SYMBOL["z"])
    return im


PAGE_H = 8.6        # usable text height on an A4/letter page, inches


def panel_figsize(L, W, ncols=1, nrows=1, width=WIDE, side_ratio=0.0,
                  extra_h=0.75, wspace=0.25, hspace=0.30, max_h=PAGE_H):
    """
    Choose a figure size that matches the data aspect ratio.

    Field panels use ``aspect="equal"``, so their height follows the domain
    ratio.  Computing the canvas from that ratio avoids unnecessary internal
    padding.

    Compute the height from the width instead:
        panel width  = usable width / ncols
        panel height = panel width * W / L
        figure height = nrows * panel height + margins

    side_ratio adds a non-field column (a text panel) whose width is that
    fraction of one panel.
    """
    usable = width * 0.86                       # leave room for labels/cbar
    pw = usable / (ncols + side_ratio + wspace * max(ncols - 1, 0))
    ph = pw * float(W) / float(L)
    h = nrows * ph * (1 + hspace) + extra_h
    if h > max_h:
        # Narrow the figure when a tall stack would exceed the page height.
        scale = (max_h - extra_h) / max(h - extra_h, 1e-9)
        if width * scale >= COL:
            width *= scale
            h = max_h
        else:
            # Keep at least one-column width and report when the stack is tall.
            h = h * COL / width
            width = COL
            print(f"    note: {nrows} stacked panels of aspect "
                  f"{float(W) / float(L):.2f} need {h:.1f} in at one column "
                  f"width.\n          Consider fewer rows, or two columns.")
    return (float(width), float(h))


def caption(fig, text, y=-0.01, fontsize=8):
    """
    Attach the explanatory sentence UNDER the figure, where a caption goes,
    instead of on an axis title.
    """
    fig.subplots_adjust(bottom=max(0.12, fig.subplotpars.bottom))
    fig.text(0.5, y, text, ha="center", va="top", fontsize=fontsize,
             wrap=True, transform=fig.transFigure)
    return fig
housestyle.pySemantic plotting styles and shared comparison limitsSee code ↗

Paired fields use one robust colour scale so visual contrast remains comparable.

"""Semantic plotting conventions shared across the analysis scripts.

``figstyle`` defines the typography, dimensions and basic plotting defaults;
this module adds consistent meanings for colours and line styles.  Collisional,
ghost, null and subset series each retain the same colour--dash combination,
including in greyscale output.  Helpers are provided for shared colour limits,
reference lines, uncertainty bands, panel statistics and legends.

Importing the module also pairs direct ``Figure.savefig`` calls so that each
figure is written as a 600 dpi PNG and a vector PDF.  Dense artists can be
marked with :func:`dense` to rasterise only that layer in the PDF.

The remaining choices still need visual review: titles should be short, axis
labels should name the observable, smoothing and fitted ranges belong in the
caption, and each multi-panel figure should support one clear comparison.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

import figstyle as fs

STAMP = "2026-08-11"

# --------------------------------------------------------- paired output
AUTO_PAIR = True        # each savefig call writes .png and .pdf
DPI = 600               # raster resolution at the figure's final size
PDF_WARN_MB = 6.0       # above this, an unrastered dense layer is likely
EXACT_SIZE = True       # saved size == declared size (see below)
_TIGHT_SEEN = []        # figures cropped by tight, reported by report()

# ``bbox="tight"`` can change the declared physical width by cropping to the
# ink. EXACT_SIZE keeps the canvas dimensions stable so LaTeX does not rescale
# nominally identical fonts by different amounts.


def dense(artist):
    """Mark a dense artist for rasterisation inside a vector PDF.

    Axes text, ticks and lines stay vector; only the marked artist is
    rendered to pixels. Use on scatters of thousands of points, on
    hexbins, and on pcolormesh/imshow layers.
    """
    try:
        artist.set_rasterized(True)
    except AttributeError:
